Pulled 2026-11-06 from api.igem.org (`tools/fetch_teams.py`). 463 teams; 426 have a published abstract. Grouped by village, alphabetical within.


## Agriculture (40)

### Aiglon College — UVitis: Improving the Vitality of Grapes
*CHE · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6239) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/aiglon-college)

In Vaud, high-altitude vineyards face UV indices reaching 9, causing heat damage, altered sugar and acid ratios, and undesirable aromas that threaten wine quality. While kaolin clay particle films reflect sunlight to reduce heat stress, it is easily washed off by rain. 



Project UVitis engineers a fusion protein that functions as biological double-sided tape, anchoring kaolin particles to grape waxy cuticles. The construct joins a silica/kaolin-binding peptide (Car9, SB7, or R5), a flexible linker, an sfGFP fluorescent reporter, and MacHis, a plant-wax-binding peptide. Expressed in non-pathogenic E. coli, the presence of the fusion proteins were evaluated through fluorescence. 



Therefore, through enhancing kaolin retention, UVitis offers a more sustainable bio-shield to protect viticulture against climate stressors.

### Alicante University — 1,3-Dimethoxybenzene: An insight on fungal compounds against nematodes
*ESP · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6397) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/alicante-university)

Plant-parasitic nematodes cause major crop losses worldwide. The fungus Pochonia chlamydosporia naturally produces 1,3-dimethoxybenzene (C5), a volatile compound with nematicidal activity, but its biosynthetic pathway remains uncharacterised. NemaVOC aims to reconstruct and optimise this pathway in Escherichia coli using genes from other organisms encoding a polyketide synthase (PKS), a decarboxylase and an O-methyltransferase (OMT). Plasmid constructs with codon-optimised sequences under constitutive promoters will be assembled following a modular approach: each enzyme is first tested individually, then combined to rebuild the full pathway, aiming for a strain that produces C5 from simple precursors and ultimately from glucose. We will assess C5 toxicity on E. coli, study C5 production by P. chlamydosporia strain Pc123 under different culture conditions including resorcinol supplementation, and begin with heterologous OMT expression using resorcinol as substrate. Molecular modelling and docking will explore C5 interactions with nematode target proteins.

### BIT-China — HERBAL——Herb Evaluation via Rapid Bacterial Assay for Licorice
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6368) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/bit-china)

Herbal materials can look similar yet differ substantially in their bioactive constituents. HERBAL aims to make compositional screening more accessible, using licorice as its first application. Our design combines enzymatic pretreatment with engineered yeast and Escherichia coli to sense glycyrrhetinic acid, liquiritigenin, and selected phenolic acids. We investigate two routes: direct fluorescence readout for practical screening and molecular communication with the computing strain to explore biological information processing. A compact reader integrates mixing, temperature control, and optical detection, while calibration and modeling connect fluorescence to concentration estimates.  We also developed BIO-NET, an open-source platform for biological network design and simulation. Guided by growers, traders, and testing specialists, HERBAL prioritizes batch comparison during purchasing, aiming to support better-informed decisions and future applications in other herbal materials.

### BJWZ-China — Tree Guard: Engineered Bacillus subtilis with LysM₃‑ChiA and RNAi Against Peach Gummosis
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6346) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/bjwz-china)

To address the challenge that Botryosphaeria dothidea conceals chitin via the LysM domain of its BdLM1 protein to evade peach PTI (PAMP‑Triggered Immunity), we constructed a dual‑action biocontrol system using engineered Bacillus subtilis. The engineered strain continuously secretes the LysM₃‑Cat fusion protein, which competitively masks pathogen cell‑wall chitin through high‑affinity multivalent LysM binding, thereby blocking immune evasion. Concurrently, the GH18 catalytic domain specifically hydrolyzes chitin at hyphal tips, releasing chitin oligosaccharides (DAMPs) that activate plant PTI immunity. Furthermore, we designed siRNA targeting the BdLM1 gene to silence its expression, abolishing the pathogen's immune‑suppressive strategy and restoring the tree's defense. Upon colonization in peach trees, this system achieves triple synergy: direct antifungal killing, immunity activation, and immune restoration. Laboratory validation confirms that this strategy efficiently inhibits hyphal growth and substantially reduces reliance on chemical pesticides, providing a green, long‑lasting synthetic‑biology solution for peach gummosis management.

### BWYA — Flowever
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6441) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/bwya)

Cut flowers have a limited vase life, leading to premature disposal and resource waste. Flowever aims to develop a sustainable flower preservative using melatonin produced through synthetic biology. We engineered Escherichia coli with an optimized nine-gene biosynthetic pathway, achieving a melatonin concentration of 159.02 mg/L after 96 hours. To reduce purification costs and improve stability, we developed a preservative combining filtered fermentation supernatant with citric acid. In laboratory trials, our formulation delayed visual deterioration in carnations. In one experiment, all treated carnations retained ornamental value on Day 6, while none in the water control did. Guided by stakeholder interviews and consumer research, we explored commercial applications in hotels and commercial complexes. Flowever connects synthetic biology with practical flower preservation, aiming to extend flower longevity and reduce waste.

### CCA-SanDiego — PestiSense
*USA · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6069) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/cca-sandiego)

Pesticide contamination threatens public health through potential neurotoxicity and associations with neurodegenerative diseases. However, conventional detection requires expensive equipment and time-consuming procedures, limiting on-site testing. Our project developed and experimentally evaluated a cell-free fluorescence resonance energy transfer (FRET)-based aptasensor for detecting rotenone and malathion. The system comprises a fluorophore-labeled DNA aptamer (FDNA) and a complementary quencher-labeled strand (QDNA). In the absence of pesticide, hybridization suppresses fluorescence. Target binding is expected to separate the fluorophore and quencher, restoring fluorescence. Two aptamer candidates, AX12 targeting rotenone and MalR targeting malathion, were designed and evaluated alongside existing candidates. Quenching assays showed reduced fluorescence with increasing QDNA concentrations, supporting proper quenching. Pesticide-response experiments demonstrated generally increasing fluorescence across the tested concentration ranges, indicating promising pesticide targeting despite some experimental variability. These preliminary findings establish a foundation for further development. Future work will validate binding independently, improve reproducibility, assess selectivity, and determine detection limits.

### CCU-Taiwan — NoFold: Engineering E. coli to Produce L-Limonene Against the Rice Leafroller
*TWN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6408) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/ccu-taiwan)

The rice leafroller (Cnaphalocrocis medinalis) is a migratory pest that damages rice across East Asia, and farmers rely heavily on chemical pesticides to control it. NoFold engineers Escherichia coli to produce L-limonene, a plant terpene reported to repel the rice leafroller and reduce its egg-laying. The limonene is extracted from the culture and applied as a spray, so no engineered cells are released into the field. Our strain expresses a geranyl diphosphate synthase and a limonene synthase, and we pursue two strategies to raise yield: overexpressing DXS and IDI to increase precursor supply through the native MEP pathway, and fusing the two final enzymes to bring their active sites closer together. We complemented the wet-lab design with metabolic and molecular modeling, consulted agricultural stakeholders, and ran outreach on sustainable farming. NoFold aims to give rice farmers a bio-based alternative to chemical pesticides.

### City-of-London-UK — Vinalysis - A Rapid Test for Smoke-tainted Grapes
*GBR · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6116) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/city-of-london-uk)

As climate change increases the occurrence and scale of wildfires, smoke taint is costing the global wine industry hundreds of millions every year. Volatile phenols are carried vast distances by wildfire smoke and bind to sugars in grapes undetectably. Fermentation of tainted grapes to produce wine releases these phenols, which have a strong smoky and medicinal flavour, rendering the wine unsellable. Current lab tests for smoke taint are expensive and take weeks, leaving a costly waiting period right before harvest. 



Vinalysis is an affordable, rapid triage test for use in the vineyard, which quantifies the concentrations of two common phenols: guaiacol and syringol. After enzymatic cleavage frees these compounds, guaiacol will bind to a guaiacol-binding aptamer in an RNA riboswitch, and syringol will cause the transcription factor DesX to undergo a shape change. This will result in the production of fluorescence via GFP, indicating the degree of smoke taint.

### CUHK-Shenzhen — Plant SOS: A plant hormone biosensor complex for early warning of biotic and abiotic stresses
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6355) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/cuhk-shenzhen)

3 critical hormones SA, JA, ABA play important roles in regulating plant growth and coping with biotic and abiotic stress. We design a stress onset sensor complex called Plant SOS. We aim to realize an detection system including SA, JA, ABA sensors and estimate the amount of different plant hormones via luminescence intensity. And we will also construct a model for interactive analysis of fluctuations across three hormones. Through this biosensor complex we can evaluate the state of plants more efficiently, economically, comprehensively than traditional methods, and give valuable advises in early disease prevention.

### ECUST — The Great Wall: Engineering Light-Programmable Living Microrobots for Precision Mucosal Vaccination in Aquaculture
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6447) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/ecust)

Immersion vaccination offers scalable protection for juvenile fish, yet the mucosal barrier limits antigen delivery. Building on our previous Wukong project, The Great Wall shifts to LOV–CheZ fusions designed for direct, protein-level regulation of bacterial motility. Through iterative DBTL cycles, we constructed three variants; the best candidate produced larger swimming halos under blue light than in darkness in soft-agar assays. To quantify motility and explore delivery, we developed bacterial tracking software and an agent-based model. Model simulations predicted 42.7% greater antigen transport across the mucosal barrier within five minutes under pulsed illumination than without illumination. We also developed a biomimetic microfluidic chip and integrated an illuminated screw immersion device with a smart vaccine workstation, achieving 95% vaccine utilization in controlled recirculation. Guided by stakeholder input and adhering to approved animal-use and biosafety protocols, this contained platform advances light-controlled mucosal delivery toward practical, environmentally responsible aquaculture vaccination.

### GeorgiaState — REDAlert: Engineering an Early Visual Warning System for Rice Blast Disease
*USA · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6275) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/georgiastate)

Rice blast, caused by Magnaporthe oryzae, is among the most destructive rice diseases, yet farmers usually notice symptoms only after preventive fungicide is no longer effective. REDAlert (Riceblast Early Detection Alert) is a synthetic biology system designed to make rice visibly signal infection before symptoms appear, giving smallholder farmers time to act. A chimeric receptor fusing the OsTPR1 ectodomain to OsCERK1 transmembrane and kinase domains detects MoChia1, an effector the fungus secretes to mask its chitin. Binding prompts the 14-3-3 adapter OsGF14f to recruit split-TEV protease, releasing GAL4-VP16 to drive RUBY betalain production, a red pigment readable without lab equipment. This season we validated protoplast isolation and are testing the circuit by PEG-mediated transfection of rice protoplasts, supported by structural modeling and design decisions shaped by regulators, industry scientists, researchers, ethicists, and farmers.

### Guelph — Lumifert
*CAN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6068) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/guelph)

Conventional synthetic fertilizers are unsustainable due to energy-intensive production, supply chain volatility, and ineffective nutrient release, resulting in financial losses for farmers and environmental destruction. The objective of this research is to develop a bioengineered slow-release fertilizer with tunable nutrient content that can be produced onsite at farming operations. The fertilizer biomass is composed of a consortium of duckweed (Lemna minor) and bacteria (Gluconacetobacter diazotrophicus) genetically engineered to accumulate phosphorous and nitrogen under optogenetic regulation. This work establishes a proof-of-concept for an optogenetically regulated plant-microbe fertilizer platform that could reduce reliance on conventional synthetic fertilizers and achieve comparable macronutrient delivery while reducing greenhouse gas emissions and runoff. This project is informed by ongoing consultations with stakeholders, academics, and industry professionals to ensure it is technically and commercially viable. Commercialization of the developed fertilizer would support the sustainable intensification of agriculture in alignment with global food security and climate mitigation goals.

### HainanU-YZ-China — GreenShield MicroControl · miRNA Biopesticide
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6129) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/hainanu-yz-china)

Gray mold seriously reduces tomato yield and quality in protected cultivation. Conventional chemical pesticides cause pesticide residues and pathogen resistance, while traditional dsRNA agents are costly, single-functional and easily degraded, limiting field application. We adopt tomato endogenous miR393 as the core factor to develop eco-friendly biopesticides.

 

miR393 exhibits dual functions: it enters Botrytis cinerea cells to inhibit cell wall synthesis and block infection; meanwhile, it triggers tomato PTI basal defense via JA/SA pathways to strengthen plant immunity. We use E. coli HT115 for prokaryotic expression and construct SPc and low-cost quaternized chitosan delivery systems. Seedling tests confirm good biosafety. We build an induction kinetic model for our fermentation optimization and a Hill‑XGBoost model offering universal references for global researchers. This work provides a green strategy for gray mold control and advances endogenous small RNA biopesticides.

### HIA-Taiwan — AiyUReady: A Methanol-Based Biosensor for Aiyu Ripeness and Gel Quality
*TWN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6156) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/hia-taiwan)

Taiwan produces approximately 700 metric tons of Aiyu annually, yet harvest decisions rely largely on farmers’ visual judgment and experience. Premature harvesting can compromise gel formation and product quality. AiyUReady investigates methanol, released during pectin demethylation, as a potential indicator of Aiyu quality.

We designed a two-plasmid biosensor in Escherichia coli K-12 MG1655. The upstream module expresses methanol dehydrogenase to convert methanol into formaldehyde, while the downstream module uses a formaldehyde-responsive promoter to drive AmilCP expression, producing a blue-purple signal. Mathematical modeling connects enzyme expression, formaldehyde production, cellular detoxification, and reporter output to guide construct design.

Farmer consultations informed our proposed application, emphasizing affordability, clear interpretation, and compatibility with farming experience. Educational outreach connected synthetic biology with local agriculture and Taiwanese food culture.

Further calibration against methanol concentration and gel performance, followed by field validation, is needed to determine whether AiyUReady can provide a reliable additional reference for harvest decisions.

### HongKong-JSS — MerMAID: MerR-based Mercury Assay for In-field Detection
*HKG · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6158) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/hongkong-jss)

Mercury is a potent, bioaccumulative neurotoxin that threatens Hong Kong's agriculture on two fronts. In local farmland, the historical use of mercury-containing agrochemicals has left elevated mercury in agricultural soils. In mariculture, mercury bioaccumulates up the food chain into farmed fish—a dietary staple—raising the burden on local consumers. Yet on-site monitoring stays costly and lab-dependent. We present a MerR-based biosensor for in-field detection, coupling the mercury-responsive merR/pMer element to the reporter mRFP1e for dual readouts: visible colour for equipment-free early warning, and fluorescence for precise quantification. After screening over ten protein-based variants, we optimised one construct showing a reliable dose-response, broad dynamic range, and a detection limit below WHO standards. We validated it in mercury-spiked garden-soil matrices and showed that alginate-bead encapsulation preserves function—enhancing field use and biosafety.

### HSET-China — Silicon Shield: A synthetic biology platform that enhances crop stress resistance using plant silicon transporters
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6501) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/hset-china)

This project uses Nicotiana benthamiana as a model plant for verification and constructs an efficient artificial gene pathway for silicon absorption, transport, and deposition based on the standardized approach of synthetic biology. By heterologously expressing highly active silicon transporter proteins from plants, the project enhances tobacco's ability to enrich silicon in soil/water and deposit it in cell walls. Utilizing the physical reinforcement, antioxidant, and osmotic stress resistance properties of silicon, the project aims to achieve multi-dimensional enhancement of crop resistance to drought, salt, high temperatures, and diseases.

### INHA-U Korea — SA-Responsive Genetic Circuit Dual-Targeting Microbial Control SDual Biocontrol of Bradysia agrestis and Rhizosphere Fungi
*KOR · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6477) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/inha-u-korea)

Larvae of Bradysia agrestis damage crops and create wounds that facilitate infection by soilborne pathogens such as Fusarium oxysporum. Backyard Guardian addresses both threats using Pseudomonas putida KT2440 engineered with a salicylic acid responsive NahR–XylS2 cascade regulating two defensive modules. We quantify SA in rhizosphere soil to determine whether naturally occurring concentrations fall within the circuit’s operating range and support reliable activation. We also evaluated designed Type VI secretion system to deliver the antifungal effector Tfe2 against F. oxysporum and Type I secretion system designed to export insecticidal proteins targeting B. agrestis larvae. Expanding the platform, we are engineering SA-responsive cascade for greater sensitivity to environmentally relevant SA concentrations and exchanging the T1SS toxin to assess modularity. By integrating environmental quantification, functional validation, and modular engineering, Backyard Guardian aims to establish a versatile microbial platform for targeted root protection while reducing reliance on conventional pesticides and supporting sustainable crop production.

### Institut-Le-Rosey-CH — AURA
*CHE · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6290) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/institut-le-rosey-ch)

AURA is developing an easy-to-use inline biosensor for early detection of subclinical mastitis in dairy cattle. Integrated into milking machines, it aims to support targeted treatment, reduce antibiotic use and veterinary costs, and improve animal welfare.



The sensor uses Field-Effect Transistor (FET) technology to detect bta-miR223, a milk microRNA associated with inflammation and subclinical mastitis. Its surface carries a DNA “catapult” designed to bind this target. Binding changes the DNA’s shape, altering the local electrical field and producing a measurable change in current. Reference electrodes help calibrate readings for differences between milk samples.



By monitoring each cow multiple times daily, AURA aims to identify infection before visible symptoms appear. The system is designed for straightforward integration into milking equipment, with minimal maintenance and technical expertise required.

### Ionis-Paris — NEWT - Next-generation Enzymatic Waterborne-pathogen Treatment
*FRA · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6161) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/ionis-paris)

Driven by climate crisis, hydroponics is emerging as an alternative to soil farming, offering higher yield and resource efficiency. However, closed-loop systems remain vulnerable to waterborne pathogens. Pythium causes root rot, weakening or killing leafy greens and herbs. Countermeasures are energy-intensive and environmentally damaging, causing toxic runoff, disrupting root microbiota, and driving resistance.



NEWT (Next-generation Enzymatic Waterborne-pathogen Treatment) is a biocontrol agent detecting and treating infection in real time. We engineer Pseudomonas putida, a rhizobacterium mutualistic with plants, to release lytic enzymes only upon recognition of Pythium cell wall biomarkers via a Two-Component System with a Biomarker-Binding Module. Bacteria act against pathogens only when detected, while promoting plant growth. We assess the transformation of P. putida KT2440 and whether the enzyme can be expressed successfully while maintaining bioactivity. Replacing unsustainable countermeasures with an autonomous, bio-based solution, NEWT aims to protect roots and support sustainable agriculture.

### MammothEdu-East — BioCall: Calling Nature Back to the City
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6246) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/mammothedu-east)

A sticky bicycle seat, a car coated in sugary droplets, a pavement beneath a tree that never seems clean—these everyday nuisances can share one source: aphid honeydew. Managing aphids on urban street trees requires balancing pest control with the protection of surrounding ecosystems. BioCall combines synthetic biology and chemical ecology to explore a different approach: communicating through nature’s own chemical signals. Using engineered yeast, we develop biosynthetic modules for five volatile semiochemicals: (E)-β-farnesene, methyl salicylate, β-caryophyllene, linalool, and limonene. Through enzyme-source comparison, promoter tuning, and multigene assembly attempts, we work toward a modular platform for producing and combining these signals. Our goal is to influence aphid behavior and recruit natural enemies such as ladybirds, helping reduce honeydew pollution and pesticide dependence. BioCall envisions urban pest management that works with ecological relationships to make greener cities more livable.

### Muenster-University — P.A.C. Immunity -Protect And Capture-
*DEU · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6339) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/muenster-university)

Plant diseases cause significant crop losses worldwide. To induce pathogen resistance, the current approach demands actively searching for natural occuring resistance genes in a wide range of organisms. Sadly, this approach is highly time-consuming and resource-intensive. Further, pathogens have shown a remarkable ability to adapt to newfound resistances, rendering them useless. Our project aims to transform plant immunity into a programmable system by engineering natural immune receptors, which recognize conserved pathogen proteases and trigger a direct and holistic defense response. Our projects goal is to develop an open-source modular platform that enables the rapid, precise design of tailored disease resistance for a wide range of crops. -We skip blindly searching for resistance, we design them.-

### NACIS SHANGHAI — MelanoShield: Melanin-Protected Bt Cry1Ac for Sustainable Control of Lepidopteran Pests
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6400) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/nacis-shanghai)

The diamondback moth (Plutella xylostella), a major cruciferous pest, causes USD 4–5 billion in annual global losses. While Bt biopesticides are environmentally friendly alternatives to chemical pesticides, their broad use is constrained by unstable efficacy under UV irradiation and total field usage costs. To tackle these constraints, we developed MelanoShield, integrating UV shielding for Bt Cry1Ac and toxin-delivery engineering, together with two hardware prototypes to increase field application efficiency. Using E. coli W3110(DE3), we built a melanin–Cry1Ac co-production system yielding 1.80 g/L melanin with tyrosine feeding; 0.05 g/L melanin blocked 95.4% of UV light. We engineered an SmChiA–Cry1Ac fusion construct to facilitate toxin penetration through the insect peritrophic matrix via linker optimization (expression optimization ongoing). Separately, we designed an ESM-2 protein engineering workflow that identified MelA W144K, which increased the titer by 14.5% over wild type (from 0.55 ± 0.04 g/L to 0.63 ± 0.03 g/L).

### NEFU-China — AAAgri——an AAA-grade（Accurate、Alternative、AI-assisted） pest control strategy via sustainable biomanufacturing
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6149) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/nefu-china)

AAAgri develops an AAA-grade pest control strategy via sustainable biomanufacturing, producing chrysanthemic acid in engineered Yarrowia lipolytica. Our approach centers on three "A" pillars:

 

Accurate: Green, relatively specific pyrethrins from chrysanthemic acid enable eco-friendly pest management, block vector-borne disease transmission, and serve as high-demand precursors for public health drugs.

Alternative: We upcycle low-value waste lipids as the sole carbon source, converting waste into high-value green pest control compounds.

AI-assisted: AI-assisted protein engineering optimizes rate-limiting enzymes in the MVA pathway, boosting pathway efficiency and chrysanthemic acid titers.

 

Long-term, we aim to establish a robust "waste-lipid-to-chrysanthemic-acid" platform for agriculture and public health, while developing a transferable high-DMAPP precursor module for universal isoprenoid biosynthesis.

### NJTECH-CHINA-A — Engineering and Immobilization of NJF26: Exploring Its Application in Zearalenone (ZEN) Detoxification
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6042) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/njtech-china-a)

Zearalenone (ZEN) contaminates maize, wheat and feed and causes reproductive and developmental toxicity in livestock. Conventional adsorption and chemical treatments lack selectivity and may damage nutrients. We built an integrated pipeline around the ZHD-family hydrolase NJF26: computational screening identified the enzyme, NJTECH-evolve and NJF-DuoStab prioritized mutants, expression optimization and site-directed mutagenesis produced improved variants, and calcium-alginate immobilization enabled reuse. NJF26 and the S33G/Y99V mutant degraded ZEN efficiently, and the immobilized beads retained activity over four uses, supporting a potential application in corn wet milling and feed detoxification.

### NKU-China — Field Detective: A Yeast Biosensor for Plant-Parasitic Nematodes
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6303) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/nku-china)

Plant-parasitic nematodes damage crops underground; by the time growers notice the symptoms, it is often too late for effective intervention. NemaKlear aims to enable affordable risk screening using yeast engineered to convert nematode chemical signals into yellow betaxanthin. We confirmed expression and plasma-membrane localization of the fungal receptor GPR2 in yeast and constructed computationally selected variants, contributing basic parts for biosensor development. Molecular docking and dynamics guided receptor selection; signaling and dual-channel models explored target-density estimation amid background nematodes; economic modeling assessed when testing could improve treatment decisions. Surveys, expert interviews, and farm visits sharpened our focus on pre-planting screening and brought affordability, sampling quality, and soil interference into research discussions and test-strip development. By connecting receptor engineering, predictive modeling, and stakeholder feedback, NemaKlear lays the groundwork for accessible nematode detection and more informed crop protection.

### OTIA-Hangzhou — Detect the Invisible
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6412) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/otia-hangzhou)

The moldiness of stored grains is a long-standing issue in food security: changes in temperature and humidity allow Aspergillus flavus to infect corn and peanuts, and produce aflatoxin B₁ before visible mold spots appear. Traditional detection methods such as high-performance liquid chromatography (HPLC) and enzyme-linked immunosorbent assay (ELISA) are accurate, but are limited by laboratory conditions, time-consuming and costly, resulting in the lack of practical early screening methods in grassroots warehouses. Therefore, we have developed a four-promoter whole-cell biosensor, in which hipB, dkgB, evgA and elfA drive the luxCDABE genome, converting Aspergillus flavus-related volatile organic compounds (VOCs) into bioluminescent signals that do not require exogenous substrates to self-illuminate. Chemically defined VOC mixtures can simulate the characteristic markers of different infection stages, and the combined interpretation of these four reporter genes can serve as early warning and later toxin indicators.

### Oxford — NECTAR
*GBR · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6391) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/oxford)

NECTAR is a programmable RNAi biopesticide platform that integrates optimised strategies for the design, production, and delivery of dsRNA targeting Varroa mites. Combined with extensive in vivo testing, NECTAR has been developed as an accessible, easy-to-deploy therapeutic solution to a global threat to honeybee health.

### Rochester — VERDANT (Vip3A Engineered Response for Defense Against Natural Threats)
*USA · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6208) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/rochester)

Millions of tons of synthetic pesticides are applied to agricultural land annually across the world. Not only does the consistent use of synthetic pesticides contribute to environmental harm by damaging surrounding ecosystems, but these chemicals can also become less effective over time due to the evolution of resistance in pest populations. Our project aims to solve this issue by engineering a bacterial chassis to produce VIP3A, a natural pesticide, in response to methyl salicylate, a volatile organic compound that plants produce under pest-related stress. By producing a natural pesticide only when it is needed, we can reduce the amount of synthetic pesticides that are used on plants while slowing the development of insect resistance.

### SHSD — BnDREB1C Overexpression in Arabidopsis: Toward Shorter-Season Rapeseed
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6266) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/shsd)

Rapeseed (Brassica napus) has a relatively long growth period, limiting its integration into crop rotation and multiple-cropping systems and the use of winter-fallow farmland. To explore a genetic approach to shortening this period, we overexpressed BnDREB1C, a rapeseed CBF/DREB1-family transcription factor, in Arabidopsis thaliana.

We constructed an overexpression vector and performed Agrobacterium-mediated floral dipping. Following antibiotic screening and molecular identification, RT-qPCR showed 4.4–11.9-fold higher relative BnDREB1C expression in five overexpression plants than in wild-type controls. These plants flowered earlier and matured sooner, suggesting that BnDREB1C can influence plant developmental timing.

Engagement with farmers, researchers, and other stakeholders helped us understand practical needs and challenges. Although further validation in rapeseed is needed, our results support BnDREB1C as a promising candidate for developing shorter-season rapeseed, with potential benefits for cropping flexibility and farmland utilization.

### SHSID — PlantGuard: Based on a Synthetic Biology Dual-Module System for Coordinated Drought Management in Plants
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6170) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/shsid)

To address global water scarcity and frequent droughts—where conventional irrigation relies on human experience and often leads to over- or delayed watering—we present  PlantGuard, a synthetic-biology-based dual-module drought management system using Saccharomyces cerevisiae as the chassis. The alert module drives expression of the blue chromoprotein amilCP under drought-inducible promoters, turning gel beads from white to blue upon water deficit as an intuitive visual warning. The soil-improvement module expresses dcsA and hasA-hasB to synthesize biodegradable cellulose and hyaluronic acid polymers in situ, enhancing rhizosphere water retention. Together, the two modules achieve "water retention + alerting." An accompanying ADRS hardware integrates in-situ sensing, ESP32 edge computing, wireless networking, and a mobile app to form a closed "sense–decide–respond" loop, offering a precise, autonomous, and sustainable water-management solution for both home gardening and large-scale agriculture.

### Sorbonne University — PureGrain-Zen
*FRA · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6093) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/sorbonne-university)

PureGrain is a modular cell-free biosensing platform that turns molecular recognition into a nucleic-acid signal and a fluorescent read-out. Our proof of concept targets zearalenone, a mycotoxin found in cereals and feed. We combine an aptamer, a DNA trigger and a toehold switch, supported by structural modelling and the Apta2Switch-Studio design software. Interviews with experts, regulators and feed producers shaped our requirements. Experimental validation of the full ZEN-to-fluorescence pathway is still ongoing.

### SRE-Shanghai — Fishfriend
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6384) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/sre-shanghai)

Bacterial fish diseases are a major challenge to the sustainable development of aquaculture, causing significant economic losses worldwide. Conventional control strategies rely heavily on antibiotics, which contribute to antimicrobial resistance, environmental contamination, and drug residues in aquatic products. To address these issues, our project employs synthetic biology to engineer Saccharomyces cerevisiae as a microbial cell factory for the biosynthesis of 1,8-cineole, a naturally occurring monoterpene with broad-spectrum antibacterial activity. The produced 1,8-cineole is expected to inhibit or eliminate pathogenic bacteria responsible for bacterial fish diseases, providing an environmentally friendly alternative to conventional antibiotics. As a biodegradable natural compound with low toxicity to humans and minimal environmental impact, 1,8-cineole offers significant advantages in terms of biosafety and sustainability. By combining renewable microbial production with natural antimicrobial compounds, our approach aims to reduce antibiotic dependence while promoting healthier aquaculture practices.

### SZ-SHD — S.A.F.E. Crops: Smart Anti-Freeze Endophytes for Crops
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6369) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/sz-shd)

Unpredictable frost events remain a severe natural disaster that drastically limits agricultural crop yields. To address this vulnerability, we are engineering the Bacillus subtilis 168Δ4 strain to function as a frost-resilient endophyte capable of producing anti-freeze proteins or protective simple sugars. A low-temperature-responsive gene circuit will autonomously control the synthesis and release of these frost-resistance substances when the plant is exposed to freezing stress. We will establish this beneficial endophytic relationship by inoculating the plants through seed soaking or direct foliar spraying.

### SZU-China — Ciricide: Multitarget cirRNA Pesticide Platform Integrating Circular RNA, with Minicell Based Production and Smart Release
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6076) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/szu-china)

Rapeseed, one of the world’s three major oil crops, suffers severe yield and quality losses caused by Sclerotinia stem rot. Team SZU-China develops an RNAi biopesticide built around innovatively structured dumbbellshaped cirRNA. We target three key virulence genes of S. sclerotiorum. With no free termini, this topologically stabilized cirRNA withstands nuclease digestion and achieves much higher environmental stability than linear dsRNA. Engineered E. coli minicells act as smart delivery carriers: they autonomously enrich and encapsulate cirRNA during biosynthesis, protect the payload from enzymatic degradation, and lyse to release cirRNA under the acidic microenvironment secreted by the pathogen. We also develop dedicated cirRNA intelligent design software and promote its industrial application. This reusable platform integrates computational design, molecular engineering, experimental validation and iterative optimization, providing the iGEM community with a reusable cirRNA toolkit and establishing a complete systematic workflow for developing multi-target cirRNA biopesticides.

### TUS-Japan — Soilmate
*JPN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6114) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/tus-japan)

Over one billion hectares of agricultural land worldwide are affected by soil salinization — a crisis driven by climate change, rising sea levels, and unsustainable irrigation practices. As arable land shrinks, ensuring stable food production for a growing global population becomes increasingly urgent. At the heart of this problem is salt-induced ethylene accumulation in plants, which stunts growth and devastates crop yields. Our team is engineering Bacillus subtilis to display ACC deaminase — an enzyme that degrades the ethylene precursor ACC — directly on its cell surface. Using a novel combination of the Twin-arginine translocation (Tat) secretion pathway and a LysM domain anchor, we aim to deliver a stable, active enzyme to the plant root environment without genetically modifying crops themselves. Our approach offers a scalable, plant-safe biological solution to one of agriculture's most pressing threats.

### UPF-Barcelona — Vinesys
*ESP · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6078) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/upf-barcelona)

In 2024, Catalan reservoirs fell to 14.3% of capacity and vineyards could no longer count on the water they need. Vinesys engineers Bacillus subtilis to colonise the vine’s roots and overexpress γ-PGA, a biopolymer that retains water, and ACC deaminase, which degrades the precursor of the plant’s stress hormone ethylene. Since both molecules are counterproductive when there's no drought, their production must be conditional. We conceptualized a double authentication factor: an osmotic stress promoter and a salt-dependent intein inside the proteins of interest that splices only when intracellular K⁺ rises. Proteins become functional only when both agree. Our candidate intein HutMCM2 activates at 1.5 M salt, well above the 600-725 mM our chassis reaches, so we tried to lower its threshold in silico. This same approach is not limited to vineyards. Agriculture takes around 70% of the world’s freshwater and our design could be adapted to other crops.

### URV-Tarragona — SCENTINEL: A Cell-Free OBP-Based Biosensor for Early Detection of Grapevine Fungal Infections
*ESP · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6136) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/urv-tarragona)

Powdery mildew (Erysiphe necator) threatens global viticulture, driving routine chemical treatments like copper and sulfur that severely contaminate soils. Early detection remains critical, as initial infections are visually undetectable.   SCENTINEL is a cell-free, non-GMO biosensing platform designed for early detection of defense-related plant volatile organic compounds (VOCs), primarily methyl salicylate (MeSA). Using our custom Python framework, SCENTINEL CODE, candidate Odorant-Binding Proteins (OBPs) were selected by scoring binding affinity, selectivity, and mixture discrimination. Optimized OBPs were expressed in Escherichia coli, purified via Strep-tag, and immobilized onto nanoporous anodic alumina (NAA) chips. Exposure to MeSA induced a measurable optical shift, providing solid proof of concept for volatile detection. Tailored for winegrower accessibility, SCENTINEL advances precision viticulture to significantly minimize preventative fungicide applications.

### UW-Poland — HYPPE: a foundation model for fungal promoters and a toolkit designed to test it
*POL · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6504) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/uw-poland)

Filamentous fungi produce antibiotics, enzymes and antifungal metabolites, yet remain almost unengineered: they transform at low efficiency, grow slowly, and have almost no characterised regulatory parts. For Trichoderma there is no measured promoter library and no model mapping promoter sequence to expression. HYPPE is a foundation model of fungal promoters trained from scratch on a corpus we assembled: 2,702 deposited RNA-seq runs requantified into 2,332 comparable samples across twenty genomes, every growth condition annotated by hand. It encodes a promoter as discrete hierarchical symbols rather than a dense vector, so sequences can be read, edited and decoded back to DNA, and reaches a rank correlation of 0.689 on a test split opened once. For the bench we measured a transformation protocol for T. atroviride, and built pHYPPE, which reports promoter strength as a ratio of two reporters, cancelling copy number and nuclear context.

### VUB-Brussels — VUBzzz – AI-Guided Biocontrol of Vespa velutina Using A-ENA Nanofibers
*BEL · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6425) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/vub-brussels)

The rapid spread of the Asian hornet (Vespa velutina) across Europe threatens native biodiversity and pollination-dependent agriculture. Existing control methods, such as manual trapping, have proven largely insufficient. VUBzzz proposes a targeted biocontrol strategy combining engineered nanofibers with computer vision-based species verification. Our approach centers on A-ENA nanofibers, derived from Bacillus thuringiensis and developed at our host lab (VIB-VUB Centre for Structural Biology), which naturally cluster spores and other biomolecules. We have functionalized these fibers to improve adhesion to insect cuticle, loading them with Beauveria bassiana spores as the active biocontrol agent. Because fiber-based targeting alone cannot achieve species-level specificity, we integrate a YOLO-based AI detection system into a custom box trap. The system confirms the presence of V. velutina before allowing entry into a secondary chamber where the hornet contacts the fiber-spore payload, maximizing specificity while minimizing non-target impact.

### Worldshaper-Shanghai — SweetCycle: Enzyme Engineering of Steviol Glycosyltransferases for Enhanced Rebaudioside M Production
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6497) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/worldshaper-shanghai)

Reducing sugar without compromising taste demands better sweeteners. Rebaudioside M (Reb M) offers a clean, sucrose-like taste, but its low natural abundance and costly purification limit wider use. SweetCycle combines glycosyltransferase engineering with Stevia hairy-root screening to address this challenge. We constructed eight variants of SrUGT91D2 and SrUGT76G1 and evaluated them through tobacco transient expression followed by screening in Stevia rebaudiana hairy roots. In this preliminary screen, SrUGT76G1 T284I showed approximately 42% higher estimated Reb M content than wild type, identifying a promising candidate for further validation. Guided by stakeholder engagement and techno-economic modeling, we envision applying these candidates to enzymatic conversion and using hairy-root screening to support future crop improvement. Our work connects enzyme design with evaluation in the source plant, laying the groundwork for more accessible, better-tasting stevia ingredients.


## Biomanufacturing (58)

### Arizona — Vascularizing 3D Cell Cultures With Embedded Recombinant E. coli
*USA · undergrad · withdrawn* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6516) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/arizona)

Organoid models are three-dimensional, miniature models of organs that replicate their major functions. They are important to the field of synthetic biology because they can provide methods for testing drugs and other medical developments without live subjects. The inability to cheaply and accurately develop vascular networks within organoids has been a major roadblock in developing viable synthetic organ transplants. There is a lack of standardized methods for, and a lack of quantification of, the development of vascular networks within organoids. Our project aims to investigate a novel method with the potential to both cheapen and accelerate the development of intestinal organoids. We intend to do this through demonstrating the viable replacement of an expensive culture material through the use of bacterial protein factories, as well as developing a machine learning model to quantify the development of veins and the area they deliver oxygen and nutrients to.

### AshesiGhana — Recovering Gold from E-Waste
*GHA · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6321) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/ashesighana)

Ghana leads Africa in gold production, at visible cost: mercury- and cyanide-based mining has polluted some 60% of its water bodies and stripped roughly 60,000 hectares of land since 2001. Meanwhile Agbogbloshie in Accra holds circuit boards far richer in gold than ore, at 300-400 g per tonne against 5-10 g, burned by hand today. SynMine recovers it biologically. Ground PCBs enter a bioreactor where secreted tetrathionate hydrolase generates thiosulfate, leaching gold into solution; a GolS-based biosensor reports concentration as an efficiency metric; E. coli displaying GolB via an Lpp-OmpA anchor bind Au(I) selectively while copper, nickel, zinc and tin stay in solution; bound gold is eluted with an acid wash and reduced to nanoparticles. We designed, assembled and verified leaching, sensing, recovery and reduction constructs, modelled the full chain, and built the grinder and reactor. Engagement with Agbogbloshie workers, miners and Ghana's EPA shaped each design decision.

### ASIJ-Tokyo — Project 茶(Sa)kure-it-all: Improving the Production of Sakura and Tea-derived Therapeutic Molecules
*JPN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6338) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/asij-tokyo)

As global demand for plant-derived products continues to rise, traditional extraction methods are placing increasing pressure on already vulnerable natural resources. Within Japan, this raises concerns regarding tea supply and sustainable sakura cultivation. While biosynthetic production in yeast offers a promising alternative, current methods remain limited by inefficiency, product build-up inside cells, and cellular stress. This reduces yields and limits large-scale production. Project 茶(Sa)kure-it-all addresses these challenges by engineering yeast to produce the therapeutic compounds sakuranetin, quercetin, L-theanine, and epigallocatechin gallate (EGCG) through a dual-strain production model with biosensor flux control, active transport engineering, and optimized growth conditions. These approaches aim to improve production efficiency by creating a scalable and sustainable alternative to plant extraction. By providing a reliable source of bioactive compounds with anti-inflammatory and neuroprotective properties, our system has the potential to support the needs of Japan's growing elderly population while protecting sakura and tea resources.

### BGI-College — BioH₂: Engineering Microbes for Sustainable Hydrogen Production and Byproduct Valorization
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6345) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/bgi-college)

Hydrogen is a promising clean energy carrier, yet its production remains heavily dependent on fossil fuels. Microbial dark fermentation offers a sustainable alternative, but its potential is constrained by competing metabolic pathways and stringent anaerobic requirements. BioH₂ addresses these challenges by developing Clostridium beijerinckii S63 as an alternative hydrogen-producing chassis. Guided by metabolic modeling, we explored strategies to enhance electron transfer and redirect carbon metabolism. Our engineered strains achieved a hydrogen content of 46.92%, compared with 40.20% in the original strain. Beyond improving hydrogen production, insights from integrated Human Practices and economic analysis encouraged us to rethink the entire production system. We demonstrated that spent fermentation broth could support Bacillus subtilis cultivation, highlighting its potential for value-added microbial biofertilizer production. By integrating metabolic engineering with byproduct valorization, BioH₂ lays the foundation for a circular biomanufacturing platform that aims to make biohydrogen production more resource-efficient, economically viable, and sustainable.

### Bioreactor Sentinels — *(no title)*
*GBR · None · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6562)

*(no abstract published)*

### BUCT-China — Upcycling Food Waste into Sustainable Transparent Material
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6203) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/buct-china)

CycloGlass introduces a sustainable route to transparent supramolecular materials by combining enzyme engineering, systematic host–guest screening, and bio-based manufacturing. First, we systematically screened dozens of guest molecules and identified L-phenylalanine and L-tryptophan as effective partners for native β-cyclodextrin (β-CD), forming transparent, amorphous films at an optimized host-to-guest molar ratio of 0.8:1. Second, the resulting materials achieved average transmittances of 97.8% and 97.3% across 350–1000 nm, respectively, compared with 18.6% for the control. Third, we established an upstream biomanufacturing strategy using machine-learning-guided CGTase screening, identifying a candidate that achieved 59.0% starch conversion, with β-CD accounting for 45.5% of cyclodextrin products. Combined with fermentation-based production routes for the guest amino acids, CycloGlass demonstrates a pathway toward manufacturing transparent supramolecular materials from renewable feedstocks, including starch-rich food waste.

### CaptuREE — *(no title)*
*CHE · None · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6546)

*(no abstract published)*

### Cell Forge — *(no title)*
*IND · None · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6567)

*(no abstract published)*

### Duesseldorf — Cozyme
*DEU · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6252) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/duesseldorf)

Cozyme is a synthetic yeast co-culture system that converts brewery spent grain, an abundant, low-value waste, into high-value compounds like jasmonic acid. Two engineered yeast strains work together, each handling part of the biosynthetic pathway, coordinated through co-culture regulation built with the MoClo Yeast Toolkit. The system is designed to be modular: the same regulatory circuit can be adapted to drive different product pathways, demonstrating flexibility beyond a single target compound. By turning brewery waste into valuable outputs without animal-derived inputs, Cozyme offers a sustainable, circular approach to biomanufacturing, while engaging local breweries and stakeholders through hands-on human practices work.

### ESSENTIAL KOREA — EcoPatch: A Multifunctional Hydrogel Patch for Chronic Wound Care
*KOR · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6046) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/essential-korea)

EcoPatch is a biomass-derived hydrogel platform designed to combine reactive oxygen species (ROS) modulation with mechanical deformation monitoring for future chronic wound care.



We expressed recombinant alginate lyases in Escherichia coli and screened alginate degradation products alongside water hyacinth extract. Water hyacinth extract reduced ROS-associated fluorescence in LPS-stimulated RAW 264.7 cells, whereas the tested alginate degradation products showed no significant reduction. These findings guided candidate selection for further development. We also fabricated an alginate–polyacrylamide hydrogel and developed a nanowire sensing prototype for real-time resistance monitoring during deformation.



By combining approaches from synthetic biology, biomass valorization, biomaterials engineering, and biosensing, this work establishes a foundation for developing biomass-derived wound-care materials while highlighting the potential of renewable and underutilized biological resources.

### GEMS-Taiwan — Releaf: A Stress-Responsive Optogenetic Bioreactor for Precision Plant Protection
*TWN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6072) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/gems-taiwan)

Producing the right biological compound, at the right time, at the right amount has traditionally required either expensive infrastructure or constant human oversight. Releaf is a closed-loop biological manufacturing platform built around engineered Bacillus subtilis that couples real-time environmental sensing directly to microbial production. The bacteria manufacture and secrete targeted biological protectants on-site and on-demand, delivered through a membrane for controlled release. The result is a programmable living factory where output tracks need, offering a more adaptive and decentralized approach to biological manufacturing for climate-resilient agriculture.

### HAMK — Regenerative Biofabrication from Agricultural Byproducts (ReFab)
*FIN · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6331) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/hamk)

How can we as humans make materials that behave more like living systems? ReFab explores regenerative biofabrication by studying how agricultural byproducts can be transformed into engineered living materials capable of maintaining viability and recovering from damage. To do so, we recombinantly expressed a protein that could potentially be applied to inactivated mycelial materials and confer self-healing properties. This project investigates a future in which materials are no longer disposable products, but living systems that can persist, adapt, and recover.

### HangzhouFourteenAP — Multi-Enzyme and Antimicrobial Peptide System for Dental Plaque and Calculus Prevention
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6197) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/hangzhoufourteenap)

Dental plaque and calculus (tartar) are major contributors to oral diseases such as cavities, gingivitis, and periodontitis. Current removal methods rely on mechanical brushing or harsh chemicals, which are often incomplete or inconvenient. Our project engineers a synergistic biological solution combining three enzymes—DNase, protease, and glycosidase—to break down the key components of biofilm matrix (extracellular DNA, proteins, and polysaccharides), thereby dispersing existing plaque and preventing new buildup. Additionally, we design antimicrobial peptides that bind directly to tooth surfaces, inhibiting bacterial reattachment and growth. Together, this system offers a targeted, non-toxic, and easy-to-use approach to reduce plaque accumulation and inhibit calculus formation, promoting long-term oral health.

### HI-Iceland — Making the strongest spider silk UV resistant
*ISL · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6378) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/hi-iceland)

Plastic fibers are prevalent pollutants, harming organisms and entire ecosystems. This creates pressure on biomanufacturers to offer a functional, competitive and environmentally friendly solution. Spider silks are promising as they have recently gained increased attention due to their low density combined with strength and toughness. However, they are challenging and costly in production, and prone to environmental weathering. We therefore had two main goals for our project. Our primary goal was to produce truncated spider silk proteins that originate from Darwin's bark spider, which is known for making the strongest spider silk, in E. coli. Our secondary goal was to explore melanins as UV protective additives to our silk proteins. Our results indicate that the production of at least one silk construct was successful. We did not finish the engineering cycle for the project's UV portion but will continue to work towards it in the future.

### HK-Joint-School — FlaminGone: An Engineered Amyloid Coating for Sustainable Fire Retardancy
*HKG · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6058) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/hk-joint-school)

Traditional chemical fire retardants pose severe health and environmental hazards, creating an urgent need for eco-friendly alternatives. FlaminGone delivers a sustainable bio-coating platform using E. coli’s CsgA curli subunit as a self-assembling amyloid scaffold. Fusing hydrophobic BslA for weatherproofing and polyethylene-binding peptides (PEbp) for substrate anchoring, our Two-Layer System (CsgA-PEbp + CsgA-BslA + APP/EG) overcomes secretion limits and steric hindrance. To accelerate construct engineering, we developed PyroForge, a sequence-only machine learning platform using ESM-2 features to predict protein melting temperature, folding free energy, and mutational stability. Under fire testing, the bio-matrix forms a dense char shield that halts flame propagation and self-extinguishes. BslA prevents rain washout across weathering, while Arabidopsis assays confirm zero ecotoxicity. Swapping binding domains extends this platform to diverse industrial materials.

### HSAS-China — BrewZyme
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6107) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/hsas-china)

Coffee shops generate interconnected solid and liquid waste streams. Spent coffee grounds contain valuable chemical components and a material structure that can be repurposed, while residual coffee, milk, and rinse water may promote biofilm formation and odor-related microbial activity. Our project develops a modular coffee-ground utilization platform. We characterize the chemical value of spent coffee grounds and process the remaining material into a reusable scaffold for enzyme immobilization. We load quorum-quenching enzymes onto this scaffold to interfere with bacterial communication in coffee-shop wet waste. AiiA, a characterized AHL lactonase, serves as the first proof of concept for enzyme loading, retained activity, leaching, and reuse. Further more, we characterised AidC, AhlX and QQ-2 as broader extension targeting AHL signalling.

### IIT Bombay — BIFROST: Engineering Yeast to produce Bio-Coolant for Sustainable Data Centre Cooling
*IND · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6489) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/iit-bombay)

The growing energy demands of AI data centres have intensified the need for efficient and sustainable cooling technologies. While single-phase immersion cooling can reduce cooling energy consumption, conventional dielectric fluids rely heavily on petrochemical feedstocks and PFA's which pose environmental concerns due to limited biodegradability.



BiFROST aims to develop a bio-based immersion coolant by engineering Saccharomyces cerevisiae to produce medium-chain free fatty acids (FFAs), particularly myristic acid (C14:0). We used PCR-based site-directed mutagenesis to introduce the V1254W mutation in the FAS2 ketoacyl synthase domain of fatty acid synthase, aiming to promote medium-chain fatty acid production. Through metabolic engineering, experimental characterisation, and computational fluid dynamics, we investigate FFA production and the potential of fatty acid-derived fluids for immersion cooling. Future work will focus on introducing in vivo methylation to convert the produced fatty acids into methyl myristate, the target cooling fluid, advancing a renewable route towards more sustainable data centre infrastructure.

### Jiangnan-China — Systematic Engineering of Yarrowia lipolytica for Efficient 7-Dehydrocholesterol Production
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6172) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/jiangnan-china)

SunForge-713 engineers Yarrowia lipolytica to produce 7-dehydrocholesterol (7-DHC), the precursor of vitamin D₃, from glucose. We combined precursor and sterol pathway reinforcement, intracellular remodeling, and competing-pathway regulation with computationally guided enzyme screening. DHCR24p.(E158H/D436N) achieved 5.82 times the whole-cell 7-DHC titer of the matched wild-type control in a substrate-supplemented assay. Molecular simulations and enzyme-constrained modeling supported interpretation of enzyme behavior and cellular limitations. The final strain, PE13, produced 462.1 mg/L in shake flasks and 4.93 g/L after 120 h in a 5 L bioreactor. Human Practices integrated 311 survey responses with academic and industry consultations to shape evaluation priorities for genetic stability, process robustness, impurity profiles, reproducibility, safety, and downstream costs. A company collaboration agreement established a basis for further technical evaluation. Our project connects strain engineering with stakeholder needs to advance microbial 7-DHC production.

### KAIST Korea — RATAtouille: Multi-Layer Genetic Circuits for Dynamic Metabolic Control
*KOR · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6500) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/kaist-korea)

In microbial production, performance is decided less by which genes are present than by when they switch on. Static control fixes expression once and holds it for the whole culture, while the cell moves through growth and production. Four costs follow: resources skew to the product and growth slows, intermediates accumulate and inhibit, flux leaks at branch points, and closing a branch collapses growth.



RATAtouille, a Reprogrammable Autoregulatory Transcriptional Architecture, uses what the cell already knows. Input modules reading precursor level, population density and external signals share one output format, so physiological context is read from several angles. Hierarchical logic turns that combination into a single expression state, and the delay through each layer divides the pathway in time.



Our first application is lutein, where one promiscuous cyclase feeds both product and byproduct and cannot be deleted. We change only when it is expressed, so substrate ratio decides the flux.

### Korea-HS — Melano Shield: Engineering E. coli to Produce Melanin-Based Fire-Retardant Coatings
*KOR · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6313) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/korea-hs)

Wildfires are growing more destructive, and the flame retardants used on wood are halogenated or petroleum-derived. Bio-based alternatives fail on supply, not chemistry: most need plant extraction or synthesis. MelanoShield engineered Escherichia coli BL21 to express a Priestia megaterium tyrosinase under the copper-inducible pCopA promoter, where copper both activates transcription and serves as the enzyme’s cofactor. The strain produced 419 mg of dried pigment per liter, matching a commercial Sepia officinalis melanin standard to within 0.5% in log-linear absorbance slope; an empty-vector control gave none. A two-layer spray coating of the crude cell extract reduced mass loss under 180 s of flame to 3.06 ± 0.73%, against 9.36 ± 1.82% for untreated oak and 3.67 ± 0.73% for a commercial fire protector. At three specimens per condition, we match that product, not beat it, and this is, to our knowledge, the first fire test of a bacterially produced melanin coating.

### MammothEdu-United — BioSonde: Turning Waste PET into Marine-Degradable Radiosonde Shells with Halophilic Halomonas
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6173) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/mammothedu-united)

Every year, roughly 600,000–950,000 radiosondes are released worldwide and almost none return; their expanded polystyrene shells drift into the sea and fragment into micro- and nanoplastics. Because release is part of the design, the shell material is the only point of control. BioSonde engineers the halophile Halomonas sp. LA5 to convert carbon from waste PET into polyhydroxyalkanoate (PHA), a polyester that erodes in seawater, for a foam shell designed to meet China’s radiosonde standard. Three modules build the route: a PETase delivery and expression pipeline in LA5, structure-guided PETase variants stabilised for high salt and temperature, and a ribosome-binding-site library that tunes flux through the PHA pathway. A life-cycle model, with the TPA co-product sold in place of virgin material, shows large carbon savings over conventional PHA production. Interviews with meteorological observatories shaped a four-gate adoption plan, from bench tests to licensed use.

### McGill — LanTurn: Lighting a Path for Mineral Recovery
*CAN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6040) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/mcgill)

Rare earth elements (REEs) are essential for technologies such as electric vehicles, wind turbines, and modern electronics, yet conventional extraction can be environmentally intensive and costly. LanTurn is a platform using Saccharomyces cerevisiae to recover REEs from secondary waste streams. Our system displays lanmodulin (LanM), a protein with high affinity for REEs, on the yeast surface to capture target metals. Using light to control yeast aggregation, we enable cells to clump together for easier collection. By combining selective metal binding with light-controlled recovery, LanTurn aims to complement existing mining processes and integrate into industrial workflows. Our computational model, Luminate, guides LanM protein engineering to improve binding stability and selectivity while evaluating system performance under different conditions and at scale. By transforming waste streams into potential sources of valuable minerals, LanTurn aims to advance a more circular and resource-efficient approach to critical mineral recovery.

### Mingdao — Ethanol from Paper Waste: One-Pot Enzymatic Saccharification and Microbial Fermentation of Plastic-Coated Containers
*TWN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6105) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/mingdao)

Electricity is scarce on the island of Taiwan, yet every school day at Mingdao High School more than 7,000 students throw away paper lunch boxes, coffee cups and ramen bowls whose plastic lining is bonded so tightly to the paper that no ordinary mill can take them back. Most end up in incinerators or landfills — and the energy locked inside them goes up with them. Instead of tearing the plastic off, we unlock the glucose from the cellulose inside the paper. We put shredded containers, a cellulase, a β-glucosidase and Zymomonas mobilis into a single vessel: the enzymes release glucose from the paper, and the bacterium ferments it into ethanol in a one-pot design — simultaneous saccharification and fermentation (SSF). We characterized both enzymes under fermentation-compatible conditions — far from their optimum — modeled the coupling between enzyme and microbe, and built a benchtop fermentor.

### MycoFab — *(no title)*
*TUR · None · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6565)

### NBJF — ThermoTdT: AI-Engineered TdT for Next-Generation DNA Synthesis and Storage
*USA · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6264) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/nbjf)

With global data booming, traditional silicon - based storage faces issues like high energy use, short lifespan, and low density. DNA storage, with high density, stability, and low energy needs, is a green future option. But DNA synthesis cost and inefficiency hinder it. TdT, a key for enzymatic DNA synthesis, has benefits but poor thermal stability. This study used deep - learning to engineer TdT for better thermal stability. The variants can work stably at higher temps, supporting an efficient, eco - friendly DNA storage system and offering a new data - storage sustainability solution.

### NFLSHS-CHINA — Improving Crocetin production in Escherichia coli through Semi-Rational Engineering of CsCCD2
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6439) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/nflshs-china)

Crocetin is saffron’s core active substance with excellent anti-neuroinflammatory, neuroprotective and antioxidant properties, holding great market potential in nutraceutical, cosmetic and pharmaceutical sectors. Traditional plant extraction yields little and costs heavily, making crocetin extremely pricey. Here we use Escherichia coli as a cell factory for crocetin biosynthesis via an eight-gene biosynthetic pathway. We first adopted pET-Duet1 vector to express these genes with dual T7 promoters. Though crocetin was detected in cell factory, low activity of CsCCD2 caused accumulation of zeaxanthin. Using semi-rational protein design, we screened three predicted high-activity CsCCD2 mutants. Wet-lab data confirmed one mutant boosted crocetin yield by 113%. We harvested crocetin-containing powder by freeze-drying bacterial cells and then designed topical soothing essential oil and oral sleep-aid capsules. This work explores a complementary alternative production route for stable, low-cost, green sustainable high-purity crocetin.

### NJTech-China — Symphony in the cell:Biomanufacturing of 5-Hydroxytryptophan via Multi-Scale Engineering
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6071) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/njtech-china)

The global burden of mental health disorders highlight the need for efficient production of bioactive compounds 5-Hydroxytryptophan (5-HTP) due to its outstanding physiological functions in regulating mood and treating depression. Sustainable biomanufacturing of 5-HTP using microbial synthesis offers attractive application prospects. However, it suffers from inherent bottlenecks including low tryptophan hydroxylase activity, inadequate cofactor regeneration, and difficulties in fermentation scale-up. We combined enzyme mining, AI-assisted protein engineering, expression optimization, and model-assisted fermentation to overcome these bottlenecks. Structure-guided design and protein language models generated a TPH7 quadruple mutant with 5.6-fold higher activity than its parent. Reconstructing tetrahydrobiopterin biosynthesis and regeneration enabled 5-HTP production in a tryptophan-producing Escherichia coli chassis. Following optimization of the fermentation contitions, the final engineered strain produced 20.0 g/L 5-HTP in 5 L bioreactor without exogenous tryptophan. This work offers an multi-level regulatory framework and provides reusable tools for synthetic biology.

### NNU-China — Engineered Enzyme System for Green Valorization of Shell Waste and Chitooligosaccharide Production
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6359) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/nnu-china)

Enzymatic production of chitooligosaccharides (COS) often requires elevated temperatures and yields predominantly low-degree-of-polymerization (DP) products. We identified Csn-387, a chitosanase retaining high relative activity across 20–50 °C, and developed a rational engineering strategy guided by structural analysis, substrate-binding modes, and catalytic-pocket interactions. Iterative engineering generated the triple mutant L147W/T229Y/Y263F, shifting the product profile from predominantly DP2–DP3 to DP3–DP6, with DP4–DP5 as the major products. The mutant retained high catalytic activity across 20–40 °C and was further validated in a 100 mL fed-batch hydrolysis system at 24–26 °C without external heating. These results establish a promising biocatalyst for low-energy production of higher-DP COS and a reusable engineering framework for controlling chitosanase product profiles, supporting future valorization of crustacean-processing waste.

### NNU-Nanjing-China — Pigment Green Revolution: Biosynthesis of Indigo from Escherichia coli
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6348) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/nnu-nanjing-china)

Traditional chemical indigo production produces hazardous waste. This project engineered Escherichia coli for green indigo biosynthesis through multiple strategies. Firstly, we constructed the TnaA-mFMO indigo pathway and obtained an original strain TMF02 with a yield of 0.580 g/L. Then using CRISPR-associated transposase for chromosome multi-copy integration of TnaA and mFMO, the yield reached 1.730 g/L. To overcome the limitations of classic intrinsically disordered proteins (IDPs), two artificial intelligence (AI) models were developed: PhaseLACE screened the functional natural IDPs and LACE-IDP de novo designed novel unnatural IDPs. A total of 12 candidate condensable IDPs were obtained, and several showed stronger condensation than classic RLP. The top Q97W73-M8 was further applied for the indigo synthesis, resulting in a yield of 3.199 g/L, which was 5.52 times that of the TMF02. This project demonstrated the Innovation of AI in novel IDPs screening and the advantages of artificial membraneless organelles in cell factories construction.

### NUS-Singapore — ORCHID: Observation of Real-time Cellular Health and Intracellular Dynamics for Predictive Biomanufacturing
*SGP · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6395) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/nus-singapore)

In biomanufacturing, achieving economically viable industrial-scale production requires optimization of titre, rate and yield (TRY). However, engineered genetic constructs and heterogeneous environmental conditions often trigger emergent stresses that disrupt cell metabolism and production efficiency. Current methods rely on reactive interventions, adjusting culture conditions or gene expression of biosynthesis pathways after observing impacts of stress, rather than anticipating and preventing these metabolic disruptions. To address this gap, we built ORCHID (Observation of Real-time Cellular Health and Intracellular Dynamics For Predictive Biomanufacturing). ORCHID enables predictive understanding of cellular physiology through modular multiplexed biosensors designed to simultaneously detect multiple intracellular signals. They capture stress responses in real-time across varying culture conditions and gene expression across biosynthetic pathways, linking these cellular dynamics to TRY. To enable predictive biomanufacturing, ORCHID integrates ML/AI to translate the high-dimensional data into predictive insights and actionable strategies, advancing understanding of cellular stress, and ultimately improving biomanufacturing efficiency and robustness.
