### OUC-Haide — Carbotex: Weaving Ecological Agri-waste into Valued Ecopolymers
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6147) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/ouc-haide)

Crop straw is abundant biomass with untapped valorization potential. Carbotex innovatively integrates upstream biological saccharification with an engineered Aureobasidium melanogenum A08 cell factory that efficiently converts lignocellulosic hydrolysate into high-molecular-weight poly(malic acid) (PMA), subsequently transformed into high-performance bio-based adhesives.



To address inefficient native xylose metabolism in A. melanogenum A08, we identified and elucidated the roles of the key transcriptional activators AraR and XlnR. Their co-expression significantly improved utilization of mixed hydrolysate sugars and PMA synthesis. Overexpressing a selected pyruvate carboxylase gene further increased precursor supply, enabling a PMA titer of 26 g/L from lignocellulosic hydrolysate. Direct dopamine modification of PMA-containing fermentation broth produced high-strength bio-based adhesives with promising chemical and medical applications.



Carbotex thus links lignocellulose saccharification, hydrolysate valorization, and advanced materials in a closed-loop biomanufacturing pathway, converting agricultural waste into high-value adhesives and offering a reference approach for biomass utilization and carbon neutrality.

### Phyto-X — *(no title)*
*PAK · None · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6560)

*(no abstract published)*

### PSI Korea — Bio Color ID: Engineering Biomaterials for Optical Authentication
*KOR · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6479) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/psi-korea)

Counterfeit products undermine consumer trust across industries, including cosmetics and pharmaceuticals. PSI Korea developed Bio Color ID to explore optical authentication using tags composed of polyhydroxybutyrate (PHB) and biopigments. We engineered microbial PHB and indigo production pathways and investigated enzymatic halogenation to expand pigment diversity. We also fabricated PHB tags containing biopigments and measured their RGB responses under five LED illumination conditions and four background colors, generating 60 optical features. Responses varied with pigment composition, loading, and film thickness. Three independently fabricated tags with the same formulation showed consistent profiles. Selected comparisons between visually similar tags and with watercolor imitations on paper revealed differences under specific measurement conditions. Feedback from experts, the public, and industry informed calibration and packaging requirements. These findings connect synthetic biology, material design, and optical measurement, providing a foundation for validating authentication performance using independent samples and assessing future applications to product packaging across diverse industries.

### RUBochum — Cat PURr - Catalytic Polyurethane Recycling
*DEU · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6168) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/rubochum)

Polyurethanes (PUR) rank among the most produced plastics globally, yet less than 10% is recycled,the majority is incinerated or landfilled. Chemical recycling via solvolysis recovers polyols but leaves aromatic carbamate byproducts (e.g., MDA-DEG) without viable valorization routes. Our Project Cat PURr aims to close this gap by identifying novel biocatalysts capable of cleaving aromatic carbamate bonds to recover methylenedianiline (MDA), enabling closed-loop recycling. We are Combining sequence- and structure-based enzyme discovery via the Pythia framework, to identify promising candidates for experimental screening. Our goal: expand the enzymatic toolkit for aromatic carbamate cleavage and turn PUR waste into a feedstock for new polymers and fine chemicals.

### SCU-BSE-China — SynAmine: Engineering Yeast for Efficient Polyamine Production
*CHN · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6326) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/scu-bse-china)

Spermidine and spermine are high-value polyamines, and their low titre is the main obstacle to producing them in yeast. Improving that titre requires relieving several constraints at once rather than overexpressing a single enzyme, so synthesis was addressed on two levels. Paired transcriptome and proteome analysis of a high-spermidine strain against its parent identified supply steps that had not kept pace with the pathway, and overexpression of MET6 and MET12 raised spermidine 1.20- and 1.12-fold. The terminal step was then strengthened by fusing Spe2 and Spe4 into one polypeptide, tested with six peptide linkers in both domain orders. The forward GGGS fusion produced 214.20 mg/L spermine, 1.8-fold the separate-expression control; the reverse orientation produced 118.41 mg/L, indicating that domain order predominates over linker identity. Thirty-eight parts were registered, comprising the fusion series, linkers, stop-free domains, XII-5 homology arms and nine integration cassettes.

### SDU-Denmark — Shewanode
*DNK · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6294) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/sdu-denmark)

Global energy policy is currently transitioning from fossil-fuel based toward low-carbon, sustainable energy systems. One alternative energy source to conventional batteries that does not require limited resources or produces large amounts of carbon dioxide are microbial fuel cells (MFCs), which use bacteria to convert chemical energy into electrical energy.

Certain bacteria such as Shewanella oneidensis MR-1 can donate electrons to their environment by transporting them through cytochrome complexes in the outer membrane. Additionally, they form outer membrane vesicles (OMVs) carrying the same complexes to enhance extracellular electron transfer (EET).

Our project focuses on improving the naturally found EET by overexpression of the outer membrane cytochrome omcA and complex mtrCAB and destabilizing the outer membrane by deqQ deletion to increase OMV formation for the use in an improved MFC setup to generate electricity. Furthermore, the project aims to establish a better understanding of Shewanella's OMVs through isolating and analyzing them.

### Squirrel-Guangzhou — Cardivit： Engineered E. coli for Salvianic acid A Production and Cardiovascular Protection
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6127) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/squirrel-guangzhou)

Salvianic Acid A (SAA) from Salvia miltiorrhiza has great value in treating cardio - cerebrovascular diseases and is a key precursor for polyphenolic compounds. Current production by plant extraction has issues like long cycles, low yields, and high costs, unable to meet demand. Our project explores a sustainable biomanufacturing strategy. Using engineered microbial systems, we aim to build a cell factory for stable and scalable SAA production, guided by synthetic biology principles, offering a greener alternative for sustainable pharma manufacturing.

### SZSAIS-BaoAn — From Fermenter to Cold Table: Engineering Microbes for Better Ready Refrigerated Meals
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6512) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/szsais-baoan)

The refrigerated ready-meal market is growing fast, yet lipid and protein oxidation during processing, cold-chain transport and reheating darkens colour, creates warmed-over off-flavours and reduces water-holding capacity and tenderness. Ergothioneine (EGT), a natural sulfur-containing antioxidant with metal-chelating potential, is a candidate for food quality retention, but low microbial titres have limited its use. We reconstructed an EGT pathway in Escherichia coli, reaching 23.37 mg/L. Testing four SAM-regenerating enzymes, we found ADO1 raised the titre to 39.1 mg/L. Guided by structural analysis, molecular docking and AI-assisted screening, we built six Egt1 single-point variants; K406S performed best, improving the titre by 52.3% over the Egt1/Egt2 baseline. Through consumer surveys, central-kitchen and factory visits and expert interviews, we identified oxidative reddening of high-value peeled shrimp as our first application scenario, and plan to extend biosynthetic EGT to other prepared foods, making cold meals better-tasting and safer.

### Thailand-XCLASB — Melanify, Upcycling PET into Protective Melanin
*THA · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6318) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/thailand-xclasb)

PET plastic has become deeply embedded in modern consumer culture, appearing in nearly every aspect of our daily lives. Yet, millions of tonnes of PET plastic still evade recycling each year. The reality is that conventional PET recycling is often inefficient and economically unattractive. Each recycling cycle can degrade polymer quality, limiting how many times the material can be effectively reused before its performance declines. But our solution rewrites the economics: our two host cascade turns consumer plastic into allomelanin, a high-value UV shielding biomaterial. Pichia pastoris secretes a thermostable hydrolase to crack the polymer, glycosylating the enzyme at the same time to increase its thermal stability. The resulting monomers are then utilized by engineered Escherichia coli with surface displays of downstream pathway enzymes to efficiently convert PET derived intermediates into a valuable pigment.

### Tongji China — Virock：Intelligent Microbial Self-Healing Concrete System
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6272) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/tongji-china)

As various infrastructures continue to be built, concrete cracking remains an unavoidable challenge in the engineering field, and conventional repair solutions are not only costly but also fail to deliver long‑lasting repair effects. Perhaps synthetic biology can offer a distinct solution for concrete crack repair! Using Bacillus subtilis as the engineering chassis, this project designs engineered strains capable of expressing mineralization‑assisting proteins to realize biological self‑healing of internal concrete cracks through calcium carbonate deposition. To avoid environmental leakage risks posed by engineered microbes, we have simultaneously devised a biosafety containment system that restricts the survival of engineered bacteria in uncontrolled environments via cell lysis and growth inhibition. We expect this microbial self‑healing system to provide an innovative synthetic‑biology‑based solution for building‑structure protection.

### TU Delft — MOSAIC: MOdular Scaffolds for Adhesive Implant Coatings
*NLD · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6110) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/tu-delft)

Medical implants can fail through infection, poor tissue integration and more, causing pain, lost independence and 

repeated treatment for patients. MOSAIC is a biomanufacturing platform powered by synthetic biology which can address these challenges through modular implant coatings made from elastin-like polypeptides (ELPs), engineered proteins inspired by human elastin. We produced and purified ELPs to assess coating performance: surface tests supported attachment, and cell assays showed improved adhesion, spreading and growth. A protease cut the ELP at built-in sites, a route to on-demand drug release, and an antimicrobial peptide inhibited bacterial infections. Our computational models predict how a coating will behave before it is made, while MOSAIC Designer simplifies repetitive gene design and assembly. Stakeholder engagement, production models and regulatory analyses informed a route to scalable biomanufacturing and commercialisation. Together, these advances provide building blocks for multifunctional, personalised implant coatings that could improve the lives of many patients.

### UCNZ — NanoBrew: Transforming brewers' spent grains into high-value nanocellulose
*NZL · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6301) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/ucnz)

Brewers’ spent grain (BSG) is a major waste stream from the brewing industry. It is primarily sold as low-value animal feed or landfilled. BSG is a cheap substrate to produce nanocellulose, an emerging material with applications in biomedicine, electronics and packaging. NanoBrew is developing a sustainable, enzyme-oriented approach through recombinant expression of xynA xylanase from 𝘉𝘢𝘤𝘪𝘭𝘭𝘶𝘴 𝘴𝘶𝘣𝘵𝘪𝘭𝘪𝘴 and ancestral Cel5A endoglucanase in 𝘌𝘴𝘤𝘩𝘦𝘳𝘪𝘤𝘩𝘪𝘢 𝘤𝘰𝘭𝘪. The xylanase breaks down hemicellulose to increase cellulose accessibility, along with other pre-processing methods. The high-activity ancestral endoglucanase will degrade the cellulose into short fibrils, reducing the processing required to produce nanocellulose. The recombinant enzymes will be secreted out of 𝘌. 𝘤𝘰𝘭𝘪 by fusion to YebF, allowing our process to be more easily upscaled. The economic and sustainable feasibility of a one-pot bioreactor will be assessed using simulations based on our experimental data. We aim to tackle issues in waste management and current nanocellulose production methods.

### UCS-A — Tea to Bioplastic: A Tea-Waste-Driven Microbial Cell Factory for PHB Production
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6526) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/ucs-a)

The rapid growth of the milk tea industry has generated large amounts of tea residue waste, while the widespread use of disposable plastic products continues to intensify environmental pollution. Although tea residues are rich in cellulose and polysaccharides, most are discarded without effective utilization. To address this challenge, we developed a sustainable “Tea-to-Bioplastic” platform that transforms tea waste into biodegradable PHB bioplastics. Through biomass pretreatment, complex polysaccharides in tea residues are converted into fermentable sugars, which are then utilized by metabolically engineered microbes to produce PHB. The resulting bioplastic can be further processed into disposable products such as drinking straws, creating a closed-loop system from tea waste to sustainable consumer goods. By integrating synthetic biology, metabolic engineering, and circular economy principles, our project offers a green solution for waste valorization, plastic reduction, and sustainable biomanufacturing.

### UCSC — AMPiary
*USA · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6215) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/ucsc)

The 2026 UCSC iGEM team presents AMPiary, a streamlined, modular, computationally informed platform for rapidly synthesizing novel antimicrobial peptides (AMPs). We built a standardized AMP expression plasmid and AMPexchange, a method for swapping AMP genes without full reassembly. Engineered E. coli hosts with a dual-mechanism biosafety system produce the peptides. Our machine-learning genetic algorithm, reVAMP, generates candidate AMPs and improves with assay data. We developed this platform to enable Lyme disease research. Lyme disease, caused by Borrelia burgdorferi, can progress to antibiotic-unresponsive Post-Treatment Lyme Disease Syndrome. Preliminary studies show honey bee venom AMPs are active against resistant B. burgdorferi, yet no clinical trial has tested them. We developed our AMP manufacturing platform using the sequences for AMPs found in bee venom. We designed, synthesized, purified, and validated apiarin, a novel AMP engineered through AMPexchange. Together, these parts form a modular AMP manufacturing pipeline for developing therapeutic AMPs.

### UESTC-China — E. coli Cell Factory for Itaconic Acid Biosynthesis from Ethylene Glycol
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6521) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/uestc-china)

Over 400 million tons of polyethylene terephthalate (PET) are produced annually, with less than 10% effectively recycled. Current enzymatic depolymerization strategies primarily target PET breakdown but stop at low-value monomers—particularly ethylene glycol (EG)—which accumulates and inhibits further catalysis. We present UpPlast, a closed-loop upcycling platform that converts PET degradation byproducts into bio-based 3D-printable resins using engineered Escherichia coli.

Our system operates in four stages: (1) EG utilization — we introduced a three syntheti plasmids into E. coli BL21(DE3), enabling EG as the sole carbon source; (2) itaconic acid (IA) biosynthesis — a four-gene module (cadA-acnA-Cg-gltA-Cg-Pyc) diverts carbon flux through the TCA cycle to produce IA at titers significantly above baseline; (3) dimethyl itaconate (DMIC) synthesis — IA is esterified into DMIC, a polymerizable monomer with an intact C=C bond; (4) bio-based resin formulation — DMIC serves as a reactive diluent in UV-curable resins suitable for DLP 3D printing.

### ULethbridge — Utilizing MOSMAGE to engineer optimized microbial chassis for natural compound production
*CAN · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6067) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/ulethbridge)

The scalable production of natural-derived compounds is constrained by low yields and limitations in microbial biosynthesis. To address these challenges, we aim to optimize microbes like Escherichia coli and Saccharomyces cerevisiae, using Multiplex Automated Genome Engineering (MAGE) for natural production biosynthesis. To edit metabolic pathways, we designed the Modular Open-Source Multiplex Automated Genome Engineering (MOSMAGE) platform - a low-cost, modular hardware system designed to automate MAGE workflows. Built from consumer-grade components and customizable modules, MOSMAGE enables high-throughput metabolic engineering through integrated thermal, fluidics, electroporation, and spectrophotometric, systems. To validate the engineering pipeline, we will measure Triacetic acid lactone (TAL) production following genome edits.

### Ulink-SZ — Chitinova: A Crab Shell Upcycling Platform for Joint Health
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6167) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/ulink-sz)

Osteoarthritis causes chronic pain and mobility loss for millions of people, while large quantities of shrimp and crab shells are discarded every year. Chitinova aims to address both challenges by developing a green bioconversion platform that transforms chitin-rich shell waste into N-acetylglucosamine (NAG), a bioactive precursor associated with cartilage matrix synthesis. We engineer Escherichia coli to produce and secrete a synergistic enzyme system combining lytic polysaccharide monooxygenases (LPMOs) and chitinases. LPMOs disrupt the crystalline structure of chitin, creating accessible cleavage sites for downstream enzymatic hydrolysis, while chitinases further convert the resulting fragments into NAG. Through enzyme engineering and chassis optimization, we aim to improve catalytic efficiency, stability, and product yield. Chitinova seeks to provide a cleaner alternative to acid hydrolysis while promoting circular use of local seafood-shell waste and supporting future joint-health applications.

### UniCRETE — RHAea
*GRC · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6457) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/unicrete)

RHAea is iGEM uniCRETE’s 2026 project idea, dedicated to valorizing Olive Mill Wastewater (OMWW), a toxic by-product of two-phase olive oil production in Crete, by mitigating its environmental impact while unlocking its industrial potential through three distinct outputs. In Stage 1, a chimeric protein (CBM3–linker–HyT12) anchored on bacterial cellulose selectively captures hydroxytyrosol, the waste's most abundant phenolic antioxidant, which holds significant value for the cosmetic, food, and pharmaceutical industries. In Stage 2, an engineered, non-pathogenic strain of Pseudomonas putida utilizes the detoxified waste's remaining organic load to biosynthesize eco-friendly rhamnolipids for green detergents. Finally, in Stage 3, we aimed to test whether the treated water is validated for circular reuse as nutrient-rich fertigation water in local olive groves.

### VGA-Pinion — AstraEV
*USA · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6353) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/vga-pinion)

Chronic UV exposure drives reactive oxygen species accumulation in skin, causing oxidative damage and photoaging. Cells defend themselves through the KEAP1–NRF2–ARE axis, where NRF2 is released under stress and switches on protective genes such as HMOX1/HO-1. Many antioxidants can engage this axis in principle, but poor stability and limited penetration keep them from reaching the cells that need them. Vesicle delivery is a promising answer, yet mammalian-derived exosomes face cost and regulatory acceptance.



Astragalus-EX asks whether plant-derived exosome-like vesicles can serve as that platform instead. We isolate vesicles from Astragalus membranaceus (Huang Qi) and pursue two cargo strategies against the same pathway: quercetin in HaCaT keratinocytes as an epidermal model, and siKeap1 in NIH/3T3 fibroblasts as a dermal model. Two computational models — one kinetic, one spatial — help us locate the limiting step and prioritize experiments.

### Worldshaper-Nanjing — MUSCLE-SHIELD: A Green Biomanufacturing Platform for L-Carnosine to Support Sarcopenia Intervention
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6508) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/worldshaper-nanjing)

As global aging deepens, sarcopenia has emerged as an overlooked crisis that strips the elderly of their independence through progressive muscle loss, while sustainable access to supportive nutrients remains limited. We plan to engineers E. coli as a green cell factory for L-carnosine production. By optimizing metabolic pathways, we aim to improve substrate uptake, product export, and biosynthetic yield. Our project seeks to provide a low-cost, sustainable L-carnosine supply for future nutritional strategies supporting sarcopenia intervention and healthy aging.

### YCYW-China — BioCapsule Delivery Platform
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6415) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/ycyw-china)

Our project aims to develop a pH-responsive capsule for targeted enzyme delivery to help manage lactose intolerance. The capsule is designed using carboxylated lignin and cellulose, with citric acid serving as a crosslinking agent to support polymerization and glycerol to improve flexibility. To introduce pH-responsive properties, we explored lignin modification using a laccase–TEMPO oxidation system to introduce carboxyl groups into lignin. Although we produced recombinant laccase, the yield was insufficient for subsequent experiments. Therefore, commercially available laccase was used for lignin modification. The modified lignin was then characterises using Fourier-transform infrared spectroscopy (FTIR) to evaluate changes in its functional groups and support further capsule development.

### YHA-HWFLA — Fel d1 Eraser: A Pet‑Friendly Neutralizing Spray That Enzymatically Degrades the Cat Allergen Fel d1
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6182) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/yha-hwfla)

As allergic reactions triggered by the cat allergen Fel d 1 become increasingly common, existing management strategies generally suffer from limitations such as incomplete degradation, poor safety, and difficulty in large-scale application, which greatly hinder the harmonious coexistence between allergic individuals and their pets. We aim to construct a safe, efficient, and precisely controllable Fel d 1 degradation system, complete product development tailored to practical scenarios. Using synthetic biology approaches, we achieve precise optogenetic regulation of enzyme activity, ultimately forming a product format consisting of a light-protective spray paired with a portable blue light activator. This study not only provides a safe pathway for heterologous expression of proteases, establishes standardized synthetic biology components, and expands the application scenarios of light-controlled enzyme systems, but also addresses the root cause of cat allergy symptoms, promotes harmonious human–pet coexistence, and offers a new paradigm for the management of pet allergies.

### YNU-China — Engineered RBC-membrane platform for mitochondrial delivery
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6342) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/ynu-china)

Mitochondrial dysfunction underlies numerous diseases, yet delivering intact mitochondria to target cells remains challenging. Free mitochondria lose membrane potential rapidly and distribute non-specifically without a directing signal.

We engineered a mitochondrial capsule consisting of a mitochondrial core enclosed within a DMSO-induced MEL erythroid membrane hybridized with a defined artificial-lipid layer. A hypotonic swelling–loading–resealing strategy solved the key encapsulation barrier, achieving reproducible high-efficiency loading. Screening seven lipid formulations identified Formulation 2 (CHOL/DOPC/POPC/LPC/DSPE-PEG2K) as optimal. Replacing DSPE-PEG2K with DSPE-PEG2000-mannose generated a targeting variant for CD206 macrophage mannose receptor-mediated uptake. Uptake was assessed in RAW264.7 macrophages versus NIH/3T3 controls using a mito-GFP reporter as a dye-independent readout. The entire system uses murine Risk Group 1 cell lines at BSL-1, providing a reproducible, tunable foundation for mitochondrial delivery research.

### Yucai-SZ — SpotAway
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6463) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/yucai-sz)

Oil stains—composite stains of grease carrying hydrophobic pigments like capsanthin deep into fabric fibers—resist conventional surfactant- or oxidant-based removers, which require water and scrubbing. We designed SpotAway, a dry-activatable, portable bio-enzyme emergency stain patch. Stored as a free-water-free porous xerogel with trehalose–mannitol lyoprotectants, it activates in situ with just 1–2 drops of water. Its functional layer employs a dual fusion-protein system: a CBM–capsanthin-binder fusion that specifically captures hydrophobic pigments, and a triolein-binding domain–lipase fusion that anchors oil droplets at the oil–water interface and degrades triglycerides. Computationally, we built a pipeline combining structural screening with docking-based binding pre-evaluation; experimentally, four Design–Build–Test–Learn cycles delivered validated candidate binders and modular Binder–lipase fusion exploration. SpotAway offers a portable, targeted, gentle solution—stopping stains, and embarrassment, at the very first moment.

### ZJU-China — Native-like programmablE cartilage Scaffold for Tissue repair (NEST)
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6102) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/zju-china)

Cartilage regeneration has long stood as one of the defining challenges of regenerative medicine: true repair requires rebuilding a load-bearing microenvironment where mechanics, anisotropic architecture, and biochemical signals act together. NEST, a native-like programmable engineered cartilage scaffold for tissue repair, uses synthetic biology to manufacture a biomimetic hydrogel that recreates this native logic from molecule to material. Its silk-elastin-like protein backbone is rationally designed to tune mechanical strength, crosslinking behavior, and tissue compatibility, while functional peptide modules introduce cartilage-oriented capabilities such as magnetic alignment, glycosaminoglycan retention, and inflammation-responsive cargo release. A modular ligation system enables these functions to be flexibly assembled and exchanged, tailoring the scaffold to patient-specific needs and diverse clinical conditions. By aligning molecular design with tissue-level function, NEST moves beyond replacing damaged cartilage toward restoring the biological principles that sustain it, envisioning biomaterials as dynamic microenvironments that help tissues recover their native form and function.


## Bioremediation (50)

### Aix-Marseille — Antibio Wars
*FRA · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6440) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/aix-marseille)

Nowadays, antibiotics usage is increasing worldwide, and these end up in the environment. Indeed, multiple antibiotics are found in surface and underground waters. The presence of antibiotics in the environment increases the risk of a global bacterial antibiotic resistance threat. Sulfonamides are antibiotics well known to be disseminated in the environment when rejected after being used to treat humans or by the pharmaceutical industries.

We wants to address that problem by providing an enzyme-based solution. We Transformed Bl21 and C41 strain of E.coli to produce 5 different laccases.

Laccases are oxydases that catalyze the oxidation of phenolic compounds. It has been proved that several laccases have the capacity to degrade sulfonamides. 

We purified these laccases, characterized and attached them on a surface, first via a 6His-tag and then via a cellulose binding module to test their ability to act as a filter able to degrade sulfonamides

### Beijing-HS — A Uric Acid Degradation Scheme for Aquaculture Wastewater Based on Synthetic Biology
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6385) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/beijing-hs)

Uric acid is a typical high-risk nitrogenous pollutant in livestock and aquaculture wastewater. When discharged into water bodies, it can easily cause environmental problems such as water acidification and eutrophication. Current traditional physical, chemical, and single-microorganism degradation methods suffer from limitations including low efficiency, incomplete substrate coverage, and a tendency to cause secondary pollution. This project aims to construct a dual-functional cascade system for both uric acid detection and degradation, converting uric acid in poultry manure wastewater into allantoin. Directly used for crop irrigation, the harmful effects on crop root growth are mitigated, as allantoin actually benefits plant growth. When discharged into water bodies, this approach avoids causing water acidification and eutrophication. The engineered bacteria will ultimately be prepared into sodium alginate-immobilized beads suitable for fluidized bed reactors, providing a green, non-polluting, low-cost, and recyclable remediation solution for the bioremediation of contaminated water from aquaculture.

### Benenden — AQUA SHIELD
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6327) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/benenden)

Heavy metal contamination poses a severe public health threat, but current detection methods fall short. Laboratory instruments are accurate but slow and expensive, while portable chemical kits cannot distinguish between specific metals, leading to false positives, a flaw our team experienced firsthand.



To address this, we engineered AQUA SHIELD: two specific, whole-cell bacterial biosensors in E. coli TOP10. Our mercury biosensor uses the MerR–PmerT circuit with GFP. To guide future optimization, we employed ESM-2 AI models to computationally screen over 2,700 protein substitutions and prioritize MerR mutants for enhanced sensitivity. Our lead biosensor is a novel composite part using the PbrR–PpbrA system to control mCherry. Experimental results validated both baseline systems, demonstrating strong, target-specific fluorescence with no cross-reactivity to other metals.



Paired with a custom 3D-printed drone for safe, remote water sampling, our platform transforms heavy metal detection into an accessible, field-deployable tool.

### Berlin — Sense PFASt - Yeast-Based Biosensor for PFAS Detection
*DEU · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6152) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/berlin)

‘Forever chemicals’ are everywhere. Per- and polyfluoroalkyl substances (PFAS) are toxic, persistent, and pervasive chemicals that accumulate silently in ecosystems and the human body. In response, the European Union is aggressively tightening regulations on permissible PFAS levels in drinking water, forcing industries to find immediate solutions. However, standard quantitative methods are too costly and slow for routine adoption. To overcome this limitation, we are developing a yeast-based biosensor to screen for PFAS in less than an hour and at a fraction of the cost. Rather than relying on a single mechanism, our architecture integrates three distinct strategies: a synthetic transcriptional reporter, a binding-induced conformational sensor, and an inducible bacterial promoter layer for amplified signal detection. This parallel profiling builds a comprehensive library to optimize the sensor’s sensitivity and specificity without compromising the response time. Ultimately, expanding this framework links on-site detection with tailored enzymatic degradation for complete aquatic remediation.

### BYHS-CHN — BioShield: Biology for Controlled Surface Treatment
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6435) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/byhs-chn)

BioShield explores how synthetic biology could support controlled surface treatment of asbestos-relevant materials. We studied three complementary modules: oxalic-acid production, metal-responsive circuits, and a candidate MgFP/VsTyr protein material. In a seven-condition copper reporter comparison, the ZupT-containing PFL design produced higher output than the NR design, alongside higher baseline expression. Separate assays examined metal readouts, oxalic-acid signals and recovered magnesium-silicate solids. Protein-material observations guided a revision of the MgFP construct. Clinicians, researchers, construction professionals and communities helped frame research priorities around exposure prevention, containment and responsible material management. Our education work combined completed classroom dialogue, visual storytelling and a documented bilingual game prototype for future testing. We share construct references, measurement calculations and experimental lessons for future teams. These component-level and proxy-material studies establish a basis for further testing; integrated treatment performance and asbestos-specific risk reduction remain to be demonstrated.

### DTU-Denmark — ByeByeBlade: A SynBio approach to Wind Turbine Blade Waste
*DNK · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6060) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/dtu-denmark)

Current end-of-life wind turbine blade treatment routes often recycle the mineral fraction but not  organic products from the resin. ByeByeBlade investigated the enzymatic degradation of thermoset resin to explore an alternative valorisation route. We identified relevant laccases and unspecific peroxygenases (UPO). Developed a drylab pipeline to identify homologs and design mutants with truncations, wider channels and higher solvent tolerances. We worked with 37 enzymes in Escherichia coli and Komagataella phaffii, including literature enzymes, previously unexpressed homologs, and our own designs. Four purified laccases and three UPOs showed activity on our substrate N,N-Bis(2-hydroxypropyl)-p-toluidine, a model compound of an epoxy thermoset resin. MALDI-TOF suggests substrate depletion and new product formation. On an extract from cured epoxy resin, active ItL-03 removed a peak that persisted in all controls. Results for other homologs and mutants remain inconclusive. We also conducted a life cycle assessment of the process.

### GeorgetownPrep — SENTINEL-LOOP: Detecting, Removing, and Verifying Lead and Cadmium in Water
*USA · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6070) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/georgetownprep)

Finding lead or cadmium in water is only the first step; a practical system should also remove the metals and confirm that treatment worked. SENTINEL-LOOP connects these tasks in one modular framework. We designed two engineered whole-cell biosensors: CadR/PcadA-lacZ for cadmium and PbrR/PpbrA-amilCP for lead. We paired them with a removal module displaying the metal-binding domains PbBD and EC20 on bacterial surfaces. For independent verification, we developed a bismuth-film screen-printed-electrode workflow using square-wave anodic stripping voltammetry to measure remaining Cd(II) and Pb(II). We also built mechanistic models and an open-source analysis pipeline for sensor response, adsorption, column breakthrough, and electrochemical peak analysis, with provenance labels separating simulations from experimental evidence. Full wet-lab and electrochemical validation was not completed before the Wiki Freeze; we plan to complete the remaining experiments before our judging session. The completed work provides a transparent, modular foundation for a self-verifying water-treatment system.

### Harvard — MlrAcle on the Charles
*USA · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6472) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/harvard)

Algal blooms in the Charles River are a significant environmental and public health issue within the Greater Boston area. Overgrowth of cyanobacteria can lead to the production and release of algal toxins such as microcystins which can cause liver damage in humans. We aim to reduce microcystin levels by engineering bacteria to produce encapsulins, self-assembling protein components designed to carry a bacterial enzyme called MlrA to degrade microcystins. By linking encapsulin production to the detection of cyanobacterial overgrowth, this system is intended to eliminate microcystins specifically during bloom conditions, providing a targeted strategy to reduce toxin levels and improve the safety of the Charles River for surrounding communities and ecosystems

### HK League — Bioprinted ElectroMining: Engineering Microbes and Hydrogel Devices for Battery Metal Recovery
*HKG · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6461) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/hk-league)

Electric vehicle growth is increasing the need to recover nickel, cobalt and lithium from spent batteries. Bioprinted ElectroMining explores a biological recovery stage intended to complement upstream recycling. We designed six candidate metal-binding proteins and twelve plasmid constructs, immobilised construct-bearing Escherichia coli in calcium alginate, and performed preliminary metal assays. A modular six-stage bead cartridge was operated with laboratory-prepared mixed-metal solution through loading, washing and separate elution of cartridge pairs. In parallel, we developed a syringe-based bioprinter and BioPrintCAD to explore printed hydrogel geometries for future cartridge designs. This laboratory proof-of-concept study connects protein design, cell immobilisation, measurement and device engineering. The operated bead cartridge and proposed bioprinted cartridge are distinct development stages. Further work will test protein-specific capture, selective separation, product purity, reuse and performance with real battery leachate. Our goal is to establish a practical research platform for evaluating biological approaches to battery-metal recovery.

### HK-PLKNPLC — Engineered Lanmodulin Variants for Selective Recovery of Terbium from Fluorescent Lamp E-Waste Leachate
*HKG · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6057) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/hk-plknplc)

Terbium (Tb³⁺) is concentrated at 3.4 wt% in fluorescent lamp phosphor powder yet less than 1% is currently recovered, because dissolved phosphor leachate contains large molar excesses of yttrium (Y³⁺), lanthanum (La³⁺), europium (Eu³⁺), and cerium (Ce³⁺) alongside major non-REE impurities, and no low-cost selective capture platform exists. This project engineers lanmodulin (LanM) from Methylorubrum extorquens AM1 to selectively capture Tb³⁺ from this matrix. Three variants — T90Y, E95D, and D43N — are designed to shift LanM's EF-hand coordination geometry toward the smaller Tb³⁺ ionic radius (9-coordinate: 1.095 Å), based on crystallographic distances measured directly from the 1.01 Å X-ray structure PDB 8FNS. All constructs are expressed as His₆-LanM-GS-CBDcex fusions in E. coli NiCo21(DE3) and immobilised on microcrystalline cellulose. T90Y introduces a Tyr antenna providing built-in Tb³⁺ luminescence at 545 nm without additional reagents. Wild-type LanM serves as a benchmark.

### HS-Kentucky — A Dual Approach to PFAS Detection: Transcriptomic and Cell-Free Biosensors for Environmental Monitoring
*USA · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6164) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/hs-kentucky)

PFAS are persistent “forever chemicals” that contaminate water and soil and pose health risks. Current detection methods are costly and inaccessible, so we aim to engineer a low-cost biosensor with a visible fluorescent signal. We pursued two approaches. In the cell-based approach, we exposed E. coli BL21(DE3) to two PFOA concentrations at three time points and profiled transcriptome-wide changes. Genes including glpA, gadE, cadA, and cadB were significantly up-regulated across all six conditions, making them promoter candidates. In the cell-free approach, we mutated the maltose-binding protein to enhance FRET signals and PFAS specificity. Using LigandMPNN, AlphaFold3, Gromacs, and OpenMM, they generated and validated over 50 mutant structures. A final mutant increased docking scores by 2.7 to 3.4 kcal/mol and was analyzed with FPMOD to simulate a FRET sensor environment. The results provide promoter shortlists for cloning and validation, plus protein targets, advancing a field-deployable PFOA biosensor for testing.

### HUBU-WUHAN — Engineering a Two-Strain System for Naphthalene Degradation and Salicylic Acid-Based Biosensing
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6049) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/hubu-wuhan)

Polycyclic aromatic hydrocarbons (PAHs) are widespread, persistent pollutants; global emissions of 16 priority PAHs reached higher recent years. Naphthalene, a frequently detected PAH in urban soils and its Group 2B IARC classified. Conventional detection is costly and laboratory-bound. We developed a two-strain E. coli system: a degrader strain carrying the nah cluster from Pseudomonas putida G7 converts naphthalene to salicylic acid, which a sensor strain detects via a bidirectional NahR–Psal/Pr-driven eGFP reporter, i.e. NahR-salicylic acid complex activates Psal-driven eGFP. Through multi-layer engineering—promoter optimization (100 to 20 μM), NahR saturation mutagenesis (20 to 0.2 μM), and ARTP evolution (0.2 to 0.05 μM), achieved lowest detection limit of 0.05 μM, the lowest reported to date. Promoter replacement, and Fe²⁺ tuning lead to near-complete naphthalene degradation within 4 h at low concentrations (20–100 μM). Integrated portable device with mobile App, enables on-site PAH screening, leading to future field deployment.

### IISER-TVM — SANJEEVANI
*IND · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6340) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/iiser-tvm)

India’s mines leave behind tailings, the waste left once ore is processed. Heavy metals seep from these dumps into soil and groundwater, surfacing years later as chronic illness in nearby villages, most of them tribal. Today such land is dug out and dumped elsewhere, washed with acid, or capped over. These traditional Bioremeditation methods are slow, costly.



Sanjeevani is a living alternative. We are engineering the moss Physcomitrium patens to pull these metals into its tissue. We would spray it onto contaminated rock and tailings by hydroseeding, then harvest it with the metals still inside.



The moss is mostly haploid and recombines readily, so edits land where we put them and show at once. We are strengthening how much metal it takes up and how much it can safely hold, to further improve this bio-engineered design to tackle this problem in an environment friendly way

### KCIS — ReBile: Reprogramming Waste Bile Acids into Therapeutic UDCA
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6376) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/kcis)

Ursodeoxycholic acid (UDCA) is an important therapeutic agent widely used for the treatment of liver and biliary diseases and remains the only FDA-approved drug for primary biliary cholangitis. However, current production methods face significant challenges, including low efficiency, complex chemical synthesis, and ethical concerns associated with traditional bear bile extraction. To address these limitations, we developed a sustainable biosynthesis platform that converts poultry bile waste into high-value UDCA. Using Escherichia coli as a microbial chassis, we engineered a biocatalytic pathway by introducing and optimizing 7α- and 7β-hydroxysteroid dehydrogenases, while enhancing cofactor regeneration to improve conversion efficiency. Computational protein engineering was further applied to optimize key enzymes and maximize production performance. By transforming agricultural by-products into valuable pharmaceuticals, our project combines synthetic biology, enzyme engineering, and circular bioeconomy principles to enable greener UDCA production and promote sustainable waste utilization.

### Keystone — Cracking the Code of Coffee Wastewater — Engineered Microflora "Dismantle" Organic Pollution
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6194) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/keystone)

Yunnan is China's primary coffee-producing region. However, the direct discharge of untreated coffee wastewater from small-scale processing facilities has led to severe water pollution and ecological damage. Conventional treatment methods are limited by high costs, high energy consumption, and low removal efficiency, which hinder their practical application. To address these challenges, this project adopts a synthetic biology approach to construct two engineered yeast strains with a coordinated division of labor, each designed to target and degrade specific components of coffee wastewater—pectin and lactic acid, respectively. These strains will be co-immobilized using a sodium alginate-chitosan matrix to achieve efficient and stable degradation performance. Additionally, a kill switch will be introduced to ensure biosafety by preventing environmental release. Collectively, this work aims to provide a safe, efficient, and low-cost synthetic biology-based solution for the treatment of high-strength acidic organic wastewater, offering significant scientific value for ecological protection.

### LYON — BioThetis- binding TFA, the smallest forever chemical: protein design for cleaner water, shaped by society
*FRA · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6280) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/lyon)

PFAS, known as “forever chemicals”, persist in the environment and accumulate in water, soil and living organisms. Trifluoroacetic acid (TFA), the smallest PFAS, is found in tap water across Europe and is difficult to remove with usual treatments. BioThetis proposes to capture TFA with protein binders anchored on cellulose and to release it by heating, with the aim of reusing the material. This year we designed and produced binders, from a natural enzyme scaffold and from de novo design, showed that they interact with TFA, and built cellulose-anchored versions. We also explored enzymatic degradation. The scaffold defluorinated difluoroacetate (DFA), a related compound, but not TFA, which led us to focus on capture. The project was shaped from the start by researchers, water professionals, associations, elected officials and the public, and we shared it through games, a short film, a video game, and school and public events.

### Macquarie-Australia — MagniMO: Engineering Magnetic Microbes for 1,4-Dioxane Bioremediation and Recoverable Synthetic Biology Solutions
*AUS · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6323) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/macquarie-australia)

MagniMO aims to address the persistent environmental challenge of 1,4-dioxane contamination in groundwater and wastewater through an innovative synthetic biology approach. 1,4-Dioxane is highly water-soluble, resistant to conventional treatment, and a potential human carcinogen. 



Our project investigates the use of a soluble di-iron monooxygenase (SDIMO) to support 1,4-dioxane degradation, alongside magnetite biomineralisation to enable magnetic cell recovery. By combining bioremediation with magnetic recovery, MagniMO explores a potential pathway towards more efficient and sustainable water treatment, with biocontainment and environmental safety central to future implementation. 



Beyond the laboratory, human practices shaped MagniMO through expert consultation, stakeholder engagement, and engineering design reviews. These practices guided the development of a safe and practical treatment approach. Our education and outreach programmes engaged diverse audiences through interactive workshops, school activities, university presentations, and community events. These initiatives made synthetic biology accessible while highlighting its potential to address environmental challenges and protect water resources.

### Madrid-UCM — Encapsulated engineered microorganisms for phosphorus removal and revalorisation from wastewater
*ESP · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6124) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/madrid-ucm)

Phosphorus pollution from industrial wastewater is a major contributor to eutrophication, causing algal blooms, oxygen depletion, and damage to aquatic ecosystems. At the same time, phosphorus is a finite and strategically important resource. Our project, rePhlow aims to recover this phosphorus using biodegradable alginate capsules that combine microorganisms and enzymes to capture and process phosphorus from wastewater. These capsules would be used in a modular bioreactor to treat wastewater safely, prevent the release of engineered organisms, and allow the recovered phosphorus to be transformed into useful high-value products. By turning a pollutant into a resource, rePhlow connects wastewater treatment with circular economy principles.

### MCA-SWIS — Pesticide Cleanup: Broad-Spectrum Degradation and Real-Time Monitoring of Organophosphate Pesticides
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6413) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/mca-swis)

Widespread use of organophosphorus pesticides, glyphosate and other agrochemicals in modern agriculture creates severe co contamination in soil and water, endangering ecological health and food safety. Conventional remediation techniques are hampered by high expenses and the risk of secondary pollution. With synthetic biology approaches, we built engineered Escherichia coli strains for colorimetric detection and substrate biotransformation, aiming project provides a low cost, efficient and sustainable bioremediation alternative.

We selected Escherichia coli DH5α as the cloning host and Escherichia coli BL21(DE3) as the expression host, and completed the construction of recombinant plasmids. For the sensing module, we constructed pETDuet-1-chpR-eforRed and pETDuet-1-phnF-AmilCP. For the biotransformation module, we constructed pET28a-OPD and pET28a-pelB-gat.

### Missouri-Rolla — Metlock: A Modular, Reproducible Biomining Platform
*USA · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6189) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/missouri-rolla)

Growing demand for critical minerals has increased the need for sustainable metal recovery technologies. Metlock develops a modular synthetic biology platform using engineered *Escherichia coli* to investigate controlled zinc uptake, capture, and retention.



The platform integrates three interconnected modules: ZnuABC-mediated zinc uptake, AHL–LuxR-regulated gene expression, and an Ag43–SmtA fusion protein designed for zinc binding and surface display. Together, these modules aim to establish a tunable and reproducible system for biological metal recovery.



Experimental work demonstrated AHL-responsive fluorescence using a dual-plasmid LuxR/pLuxR-sfGFP reporter system. Subsequent engineering cycles focused on characterizing gene expression, refining experimental methods, and improving zinc uptake strategies. Parallel protein modeling efforts supported the development and optimization of the surface-display construct.



By combining genetic engineering, experimental characterization, and iterative design, Metlock aims to establish a versatile biomining platform that can be adapted for the recovery of zinc and other valuable metals.

### MPAYF-USA — Environd ：Detection and remediation of 2,4-DNT
*USA · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6507) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/mpayf-usa)

2,4-Dinitrotoluene (2,4-DNT) is an important pollutant in the polyurethane foam industry chain and is characterized by high toxicity, carcinogenicity, and environmental persistence.

This project employs synthetic biology to construct an engineered microbial system that integrates “pollutant detection–signal response–pollutant degradation.” A YhaJ–PyqjF biosensor is utilized to enable rapid detection of 2,4-DNT, while the degradation pathway is reconstructed to achieve its efficient mineralization.

This system combines real-time monitoring, green remediation, and low cost advantages, providing an innovative solution for pollution control around furniture manufacturing and polyurethane material production industries.

### NTHU Taiwan — CatharSys: Engineered Bacillus subtilis for TMAH Treatment in MBBR
*TWN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6416) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/nthu-taiwan)

Tetramethylammonium hydroxide (TMAH) is a highly toxic chemical used in semiconductor photolithography. Its use in Hsinchu, a major semiconductor hub in Taiwan, highlights the need for safer wastewater treatment. Conventional biological treatment takes weeks or months to prepare microbial communities and consumes large amounts of electricity for aeration. It also produces excess sludge that requires further treatment. 

To address these problems, CatharSys introduces seven genes (Msil_3603–3609) from Methylocella silvestris BL2 into industrial Bacillus subtilis to enable the degradation of trimethylamine (TMA). We also promote biofilm growth on moving bed biofilm reactor (MBBR) carriers to improve bacterial retention. In addition, we developed CathaBench, a three-tank laboratory reactor with automated sensing and control, to test our system. Together with three layers of biosafety measures, CatharSys aims to reduce energy use and sludge production and support more sustainable semiconductor wastewater treatment.

### NWU-CHINA — HYDRA: Engineering a Modular Aspergillus niger Platform for Complex Wastewater Remediation
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6362) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/nwu-china)

Water pollution often involves mixtures of nutrients, heavy metals, and persistent organic contaminants, sometimes under saline conditions. Northwestern China illustrates this challenge: it contains headwaters of major river systems and extensive mining activity. Motivated by this regional context, we developed HYDRA, a modular wastewater remediation platform based on Aspergillus niger. At its core, we engineered seven specialized fungal strains, each designed to target a different organic pollutant or pollutant class. This division of labor is intended to enable flexible treatment configurations. Representative assays using real wastewater demonstrated 31.5% benzene removal and 44.9% styrene removal by engineered strains. The fungal chassis also tolerated 5% NaCl and achieved 63.3% phosphate removal and 57.2% Cd removal in separate assays. These findings lay a foundation for a configurable fungal consortium. HYDRA connects local water challenges with adaptable bioremediation, while future work will assess strain compatibility, pollutant fate, and long-term treatment performance.

### NYU-Abu-Dhabi — DuneLock: Sand Stabilization through Gene Overexpression and Transgenesis
*ARE · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6086) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/nyu-abu-dhabi)

Sandy deserts, such as the Arabian Desert, are historically, culturally, and economically significant. However, for cities rising around them, sandy deserts are a hub for loose sediments that are continuously being re-suspended by winds, which then travel short and far distances, degrading air quality, decreasing visibility, coating solar panels, and jeopardizing daily life - with 800 million people affected globally. Dunelock focuses on sand stabilization by engineering a microbially-produced adhesive material. Gene overexpression of γ-PGA is used to enhance the stickiness of biofilms while exogenous gene expression of Carbonic Anhydrase is used to precipitate stabilizing crystals between sand grains, all whilst sequestering carbon dioxide and avoiding toxic gas release. With clearer air, Dunelock is a step towards a healthier outdoors.

### NYU-New-York — PeBet: Plastic Degradation via Bacteria Consortium
*USA · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6365) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/nyu-new-york)

Our project aims to solve the escalating issue of plastic pollution. As of currently, the amount of plastic that pollutes cities, oceans, and environments is exponential but the efforts put into reducing the amount of plastic on our earth is minimal. Current conventional means of recycling and degrading plastic are inefficient as they take years for only a small percentage of the plastic to be degraded. Our goal is to degrade the most common type of plastic, PET. In naturally occurring I. Sakainesis bacteria, there exists enzymes, PETase and MHETase, that degrade PET thoroughly and much more efficiently than conventional ways (mechanical and chemical). By creating an optimal environment via a tabletop bioreactor, we can control the conditions where our I. Sakainesis can degrade PET at its optimal rate.

### Oakridge-HYD — BioChronos: A DNA-Based Environmental Memory System in E. coli
*IND · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6503) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/oakridge-hyd)

Transient contamination can escape periodic sampling. BioChronos explores a DNA-based exposure recorder in Escherichia coli through a copper-responsive sfGFP sensor (M1) and separate arabinose-controlled Bxb1 inversion modules. Four linked computational models guided architecture choices; simulation checks and an independent implementation assessed numerical robustness. Our preliminary M1 assay used three independently picked DH5alpha colonies: raw fluorescence at nominal 1000 micromolar copper was approximately 2.08-fold the zero-copper condition, while 1–200 micromolar showed no clear increase. This mismatch prompted model revision. Clone sequences remain unverified, controls are incomplete, and neither the memory modules nor the integrated recorder is experimentally validated. Stakeholder engagement informed proposed containment, retrieval and communication requirements. Our distributed high-school team addressed financial and geographical participation barriers while documenting unresolved inequities. We release Registry designs, reproducible models and experimental records to support future teams and scrutiny of both our findings and limitations.

### RUM-UPRM — R-DetoX 2.5: Engineering Escherichia coli for RDX Bioremediation within a Serial Bioreactor System
*PRI · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6282) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/rum-uprm)

RDX contamination threatens the environment and motivates the development of biological remediation strategies. R-DetoX 2.5 engineers Escherichia coli with the XplA/XplB enzyme system to target RDX, incorporating inducible and riboswitch-regulated genetic devices. In batch assays monitored by HPLC, the IPTG-inducible construct reduced extracellular RDX concentration by 85% within 20 hours, while abiotic controls remained unchanged. To translate this biological approach into a treatment process, we designed SeBiS 2.0, a proposed serial bioreactor system integrating bacterial growth, continuous treatment, biomass recirculation, and byproduct mitigation. Expert consultations informed process design and safety considerations, while educational activities engaged students and communities with synthetic biology and environmental remediation. By connecting biological engineering, process design, education, and stakeholder perspectives, R-DetoX 2.5 advances a research foundation for contained RDX bioremediation, with further validation needed to establish degradation pathways and treatment performance.

### Saarland — OWLET: On Board Water Logger For Environmental Tracking
*DEU · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6311) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/saarland)

Industrial pollutants such as polycyclic aromatic hydrocarbons (PAHs) and cadmium enter rivers like the Saar, reducing water quality. Monitoring relies on a few fixed stations and lab analyses that take days. OWLET brings biosensing to the water. It is a hybrid platform that combines encapsulated whole-cell biosensors and a field hardware design for continuous biosensing, mapping and tracking of water pollutants.  The biosensors detect PAHs and heavy metals like cadmium. To read them in the field, we built a low-cost, 3D-printed fluorescence reader that pumps water into a six-well strip, measures fluorescence and converts it into fluorescein-equivalent units, validated with fluorescein standards and GFP-expressing E. coli. The reader is designed to ride on our boat, which samples, steers toward rising signals and tags each reading with its position, while a live dashboard shows the readings and the track. OWLET is a monitoring system that identifies hotspots for in-depth lab analysis.

### SCAU-China — CyanoGuard
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6510) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/scau-china)

Microcystin-LR threatens freshwater safety during cyanobacterial blooms. CyanoGuard investigates an integrated approach to toxin monitoring and remediation using engineered bacteria, computational models and modular devices. We constructed START (Synthetic Trans-Acting Riboswitch with Triggering RNA) RNA-sensing circuits with LuxI/LuxR amplification and sfGFP/TurboRFP readout, compared circuit variants, and tested light-dependent growth control in a DAP-auxotrophic chassis. Structure-guided Trigger design and a focused RNA library support sensor development. A model using the previous seven days of environmental observations estimates total-microcystin exceedance risk; fluorescence calibration and literature-based degradation modelling support concentration mapping and treatment design. Our monitoring boat and detachable drone release module have documented prototype tests, design files and controller software. Stakeholder input shaped the project's screening role and need for laboratory confirmation, while outreach addressed toxin risks and protective actions. We share parts, methods and reusable resources to support further development.

### SMS-Shenzhen — kisMET: A Biosorption Platform for Low-Concentration Metal Recovery from End-of-Pipe Battery Wastewater
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6165) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/sms-shenzhen)

Lithium-ion battery recycling retains low-concentration residual metal ions in end-of-pipe wastewater, causing resource loss and environmental burden with conventional chemical treatment. We present kisMET, a biosorption platform built on engineered Komagataeibacter rhaeticus that produces bacterial cellulose microspheres displaying metal-binding peptides to selectively capture Li⁺, Co²⁺, Ni²⁺ and Mn²⁺. Our chassis secretes cellulose and functional peptides synchronously in fermentation, forming reusable adsorbent microspheres. Through multi-stakeholder interviews with battery enterprises, regulatory experts and iGEM peers, we iteratively defined kisMET as a supplementary end-of-pipe treatment unit rather than a full replacement for existing processes. We performed TEA and LCA analysis to evaluate cost and carbon footprint, alongside public science outreach. This modular system provides a mild, low-reagent route for metal ion circularity, aligning with multiple UN SDGs, though scale-up and real wastewater validation remain ongoing.

### Squirrel-Beijing — pNProbe: A Whole-Cell Biosensor for Bioavailable p-Nitrophenol Monitoring in Soil
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6125) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/squirrel-beijing)

p - Nitrophenol (pNP), a hazardous pollutant from pesticide, industrial and chemical processes, is common in agricultural soils, posing ecological and health risks. Conventional methods can't well reflect its bioavailable fraction. We developed pNProbe, a whole - cell biosensor. It screens and optimizes pNP - responsive elements, using fluorescent and chromoprotein reporters to convert pNP exposure into interpretable outputs. This enables easy, in - situ monitoring in various soils. pNProbe offers an accessible tool for soil pollution assessment, showing synthetic biology's role in sustainable agriculture.

### Stockholm — T.I.D.E - Targeted Interception and Degradation of Endocrine disruptors
*SWE · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6292) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/stockholm)

Bisphenol A and its alternatives (BPA+) are widely used monomers in polycarbonate plastics and epoxy resins. BPA+ are endocrine-disrupting chemicals, they interfere with hormone-mediated pathways. Culminating in widespread dysregulation of reproductive, metabolic, and developmental processes, and in growth and developmental defects. Their ubiquitous presence has significant health risks to humans and aquatic animals, posing an environmental threat to population stability that is under researched and poorly understood. 



Our project, TIDE, is a modular 3-vector platform that integrates sensing, reporting, and bioremediation for BPA+. These modules permit specific targeting with broad-spectrum degradation. In parallel, we worked to redesign a receptor with BPA-alternative specificity. Due to the poorly understood risks of BPA alternatives, our team worked to educate and engage the public through multiple initiatives and to integrate the outcomes into our project design. These initiatives raised awareness of bisphenol risks and sustainable development, the role biotechnology could play in both.

### THIS-China — ArtemiSilence: MSN-Mediated RNAi Delivery to Reduce Mugwort Pollen Allergens
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6240) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/this-china)

Mugwort (Artemisia) pollen is a major trigger of fall seasonal allergies in Beijing and many other regions. Current control methods, such as large-scale plant removal and herbicide application, are non-specific and may damage urban ecosystems. ArtemiSilence aims to develop a plant-targeted strategy using mesoporous silica nanoparticles (MSNs) as carriers to deliver RNA interference (RNAi) molecules into mugwort plants. The delivered RNAi is designed to silence selected genes associated with pollen-allergen expression, thereby reducing allergen levels during flowering without eliminating the plants. We will identify candidate allergen-related genes, design RNAi molecules, optimize RNAi loading into MSNs and delivery to plant tissues, quantify gene-silencing efficiency and changes in allergen levels, and evaluate specificity, persistence, biosafety, and environmental feasibility. Our goal is to develop a precise and environmentally responsible approach to reducing exposure to airborne mugwort pollen allergens.

### TJI-Seoul — CPS Cycle: Integrating Citrus Waste, Protein (Laccase(CotA)), and Soil for Post-Fire Restoration
*KOR · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6310) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/tji-seoul)

Wildfires can leave soils chemically and biologically impaired, while citrus-processing waste remains an underused biomass resource. We developed the CPS Cycle, a synthetic biology platform combining processed citrus waste, recombinant CotA laccase, and soil for post-fire restoration research. CotA from Bacillus subtilis was expressed in Escherichia coli, optimized for soluble production, and validated using ABTS and guaiacol activity assays. Citrus waste was washed, dried, pulverized, and incorporated into soil to improve plant compatibility. The optimized CotA preparation was then integrated with processed citrus soil to create the final CPS material. In a simplified burned-biomass model, CotA treatment reduced detectable phenolic signal, while lettuce assays showed improved growth performance under selected CPS conditions. CotA-associated activity also remained detectable after incorporation into the citrus-soil matrix. Our work establishes a proof-of-concept for a non-living, recombinant-protein-based approach linking waste valorization, enzyme biotechnology, and environmental restoration.

### TJUSLS-China — PUReCycle——Discovery and Engineering of PURases
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6121) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/tjusls-china)

Polyurethane waste creates serious plastic-pollution challenges. MDI-based polyether polyurethane, occupying ~75% foam-market share, is hard to degrade naturally, and effective urethanases for its biorecycling are limited.

Following the DBTL cycle, our project integrated metagenomic mining and semi-rational enzyme engineering. From ~30 million metagenomic sequences in SoilMicrobeDB, GTDB and JGI, we performed multi-layer computational screening and selected 19 PUC urethanase candidates for wet-lab tests. We also engineered seed enzyme AbPURase through docking, MD simulation and site-directed mutagenesis. The double-point mutant AbPURase_DM2 exhibited high activity at both 50 °C and 60 °C. Using MDC as model substrate, we built a standardized UPLC activity-quantification system. This study enriches urethanase resources and supports future biological recycling of PU waste.

### Toronto — PetaBite: Mapping Earth's Biodiversity to Discover Room-Temperature Plastic-Degrading Enzymes
*CAN · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6187) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/toronto)

Plastic has become so abundant that its carbon content now exceeds that of all animals on Earth. Fortunately, nature offers an ecological solution with PETases, enzymes capable of degrading polyethylene terephthalate (PET). Previously, fewer than 500 validated sequences had been characterized, limiting humanity’s ability to discover variants suitable for industrial deployment. 



In response, we created the PETadex, the largest open-source database of plastic-degrading enzymes, by searching 27.3 million public sequencing datasets. This expanded the catalogue from 495 sequences to over 300 million naturally occurring homologs, unlocking unprecedented scale and diversity.



PetaBite creates an integrated workflow to leverage the database. We rank candidates with a protein language model trained on the PETadex, screen them with a high-throughput copper-release assay in E. coli, and further test them in our low-cost open-source bioreactors. Our goal: to discover and validate room-temperature PETases that surpass current benchmarks and enable scalable microplastic degradation.

### UCL — SHELLter: A purification system valorising shellfish waste to filter platinum-based chemotherapy residues in hospital wastewater
*GBR · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6381) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/ucl)

Platinum-based chemotherapy drugs such as cisplatin are life-saving, but their environmental impact has received far less attention than their clinical importance. Up to 90% of these cytotoxic drugs are excreted by chemotherapy patients unmetabolised, with 59% of this load persisting in surface water discharge. Meanwhile, around 2 million of the 16.9 million tonnes of seafood waste landfilled each year is chitin-rich shellfish waste, an abundant and underused resource. SHELLter links these two problems with a single solution. Our team proposes a sustainable enzymatic strategy to convert chitin from shellfish waste into chito-oligosaccharides, a heavy metal chelating agent. Using recombinantly produced novel enzymes such as a ChitinaseA-Chitin Deacetylase fusion, these chito-oligosaccharides are produced and incorporated into an adsorption cartridge for toilets, enabling cisplatin capture at the point of excretion. Ultimately, this system can transform into a platform technology, offering a sustainable bioremediation strategy to tackle heavy metal water contamination.

### UMaryland — AEGIS (Aquatic E. coli Guard and Interception using Sinorhizobium)
*USA · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6146) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/umaryland)

In specific areas, the E. coli spikes are much higher due to combined sewer systems. These systems, unlike the standard sewer system, have connected storm water and sewage pipes, which can lead to an overflow of combined sewage and storm water beyond the flow regulator and into the local waterways. Inspired by the recent Potomac River sewage overflow in Maryland, AEGIS addresses the local issue of combined sewage systems, which often overflow into streams, by killing diarrheagenic E. coli in water contaminated by sewage. This will help countless organisms and can prevent water-borne infections.

### UNIMI-IT — M.A.N.Z.O.N.I. — Microbial Approaches for Nanoparticles Zeroing and Optimizing Natural Integrity
*ITA · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6320) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/unimi-it)

99% of people breathe polluted air. A large share of it is particulate matter, which is not one pollutant but a mixture of many. We engineer Escherichia coli MG1655 to absorb and process three of them: sulfate, nitrate and heavy metals. To overexpress whole pathways without plasmid burden, we built an integrative overexpressor: a λ-Red cassette combining a translation-enhancing MlcR 5′ UTR, an FRT-flanked kanamycin marker and one to five tandem Tac promoters, retargetable to any locus through 40 bp homology arms. With it we boosted the assimilatory sulfate pathway (cysNDC, cysJIH, plus cysZ, cysQ, trxA, cysG), nitrate uptake and reduction to ammonia (narU, narGHI, nirBD), and heavy-metal tolerance and bioaccumulation (sodA, zupT, and a synthetic operon pairing metallothionein ShMT3 with a periplasm-targeted OmpA–lead-binding peptide). Removal is quantified spectrophotometrically against wild type, supported by a model linking particle capture to metabolic kinetics.

### UOregon — AquaFlock
*USA · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6041) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/uoregon)

Nitrogen fertilizers are an essential component of modern agriculture. Roughly half of applied nitrogen fertilizer is absorbed by plants; the rest gets converted into nitrate and leaches through the soil into ground and surface water. Elevated nitrate levels can harm ecosystems, contributing to eutrophication, and humans when nitrate-contaminated water is consumed. To address this issue, we engineered E. coli to sequester and store excess environmental nitrate within the cell. Nitrate gets converted into the nitrogen storage polymer cyanophycin; the bacteria start expressing flocculation proteins when a high concentration of cyanophycin is reached. The resulting flocs can be collected and used as fertilizer, recapturing the lost nutrients. We hope that this project will improve sustainability of nitrogen fertilization and reduce nitrogen pollution.

### UOttawa — Nulfur: Sustainable Crude Oil Processing Through Engineered Biodesulfurization
*CAN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6077) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/uottawa)

70% of crude oil is sour, rich in toxic sulfur compounds such as dibenzothiophene. Refineries remove these by hydrodesulfurization, an energy- and hydrogen-intensive process leaving spent catalyst as hazardous waste. Our project, Nulfur, develops a biodesulfurization alternative using the 4S pathway in engineered Pseudomonas putida. We developed Cofactor, an agentic AI tool guiding mutagenesis of bottleneck enzymes DszB and DszC. We sanitized broad-host-range plasmid pBBR1mcs-2 for Golden Gate assembly and confirmed expression in E. coli and P. putida. To optimize activity, we built and characterized modular Golden Gate-compatible promoter blocks spanning a graded expression range, designed a 24-member library tuning dszA–dszD, assembled eight, and detected desulfurization activity from one. Because metabolic burden caps single-strain throughput, we modelled a consortium where partner strains supply rhamnolipid and formate, relieving identified substrate-availability and NADH limits. Ultimately, Nulfur delivers cleaner oil processing while providing a framework for designing synthetic consortia for future applications.

### UTArlington-TexasUSA — ForNever Chemicals: Bioelimination of Environmental Per- and Polyfluoroalkyl Substances (PFAS)
*USA · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6392) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/utarlington-texasusa)

Per- and polyfluoroalkyl substances (PFAS) persist in the environment and accumulate in organisms and food webs, raising concerns about ecological and human health. Unfortunately, the PFAS persistence is attributed to a number of highly stable Carbon-Fluorine bonds that hinder normal biological degradation of the PFA backbone. Rather than relying on effective but potentially harmful byproduct producing radical processes, our design involves the use of heterolytic enzymatic processes to safely ensure PFA bioremediation. 

Through the use of F19 vector ligated with 4 DNA fragments, crcB, dhaA, ssuE and fluoroacetate dehalogenase(FAcD), we aim to combine complementary enzymatic functions: breaking down carbon halogen bonds using FAcD, ssuE, and dhaA and removing toxic intracellular fluoride ion degradation products with crcB. Hence, this approach's goal is to understand whether this combination of DNA fragments can cause the defluorination and more complete degradation of PFAS within cells without generating unwanted partial breakdown products.

### UVU-Utah — GreenARC: Atrazine Remediation by Chlamydomonas reinhardtii
*USA · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6137) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/uvu-utah)

Atrazine, a widely used herbicide, poses severe ecological threats. It exhibits high toxicity to aquatic life and amphibians and is recognized as an endocrine disruptor by the US EPA. Even though it is banned in over 60 nations, Atrazine remains widely used in other countries including the US, Canada, and Australia. Atrazine persists in soil for months or even years and eventually runs off into groundwater where it degrades at an even slower rate. The enzyme pathway AtzA-F has shown the ability to degrade atrazine into less harmful compounds.
