We aim to express these enzymes in the microalgae Chlamydomonas reinhardtii, utilizing its ability to handle large genetic constructs and its resilience in aquatic and soil environments.

### VIS — Cadture
*TWN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6423) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/vis)

Cadmium pollution is an environmental concern because of its harmful effects on human health and ecosystems. Detecting and removing cadmium requires expensive equipment or chemical treatments. In Project Cadture (Cadmium capture), we are developing an engineered Escherichia coli system that can both detect and capture cadmium. We built on a green fluorescent protein (GFP)-based cadmium detection system by introducing four metallothioneins (MTs). If any of the engineered MTs can capture cadmium, the GFP signal will decrease in comparison to the detection system, pMH45. When we monitored the bacterial culture in real time, we observed GFP intensity decrease compared to the sensor system, suggesting a decrease in cadmium concentration. We then expressed MTs in the cytoplasm. Bacteria with cytoplasmic MTs expressed very little GFP, suggesting that most cadmium was captured by MTs before activating GFP expression. Through this project, we demonstrate the potential of using MT-expressing bacteria to remove cadmium.

### VIT-Vellore — Ammonia Capture Using a Glutamine Synthetase–PPK2 Coupled Cyclic Reaction System for Poultry Air Quality Management
*IND · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6322) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/vit-vellore)

Ammonia emissions arising from the microbial breakdown of uric acid and undigested proteins in poultry litter threaten poultry health, productivity, and air quality, causing respiratory damage and increasing disease susceptibility when concentrations exceed 20–25 ppm. To address this, the iGEM team of VIT-Vellore aims to develop a biosynthetic system that converts harmful gaseous ammonia into value-added glutamine. Using a feedback-resistant Bacillus subtilis glutamine synthetase coupled with a Sulfurovum lithotrophicum PPK2-mediated ATP regeneration cycle, ammonia is assimilated with glutamate (MSG), while MgCl₂, sodium polyphosphate, and ADP sustain a continuous ATP supply. This bio-based approach fixes ammonia into non-volatile glutamine, providing a sustainable alternative to chemical scrubbers and passive absorbents. Aligned with UN SDGs 8, 11, 12, 13, 15, and 17, Team VIT-Vellore promotes ethical, collaborative, and environmentally responsible biotechnology for sustainable poultry farming and improved environmental health.

### WageningenUR — Triton: How to make the forever chemical forever no more!
*NLD · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6222) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/wageningenur)

Trifluoroacetate (TFA) is the shortest PFAS molecule, and the most overlooked. It circulates through our rivers, drinking water, and bodies. Long dismissed as harmless, there is now growing evidence for its hazardous effects on the environment and humans. Through feedback from our amazing stakeholders, we have identified how to tackle the root cause of the problem. Currently used techniques filter, but do not destroy TFA. That is why we have developed Triton. Triton is a holistic system that first concentrates TFA by binding it to a de novo TFA-binding protein attached to bacterial cellulose beads. Next, TFA flows into our bioreactor, where it is defluorinated by our evolved TFA dehalogenase. Here the TFA is broken down into harmless oxalate. Finally, to monitor the reduction in TFA concentration, we have designed a biosensor using a de novo TFA riboswitch. Together, these components combine to make the forever chemical forever no more!

### WestviewHS SanDiego — Project PEARL: Protein Enabled Absorption and Recovery of rare earth eLements
*USA · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6257) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/westviewhs-sandiego)

Rare earth elements (REEs) are critical for most modern technologies ranging from smartphones to wind turbines, yet the current extraction process often leaves behind contaminated water that conventional methods struggle to address. Metal-binding proteins from certain bacterial strains are candidates for a more environmentally-friendly possibility. In this project, we engineered Escherichia Coli to display metal binding proteins pedA1, mshA, and etfB on its outer surface to hopefully create a biological system that can capture REEs from contaminated water. Plasmids encoding these proteins were constructed with loop assembly and transformed into the E. Coli and were eventually tested using an Alizarin-based colorimetric assay to determine the efficiency of the proteins. Using this data, we wanted to see whether or not these proteins could possibly be utilized for the bioremediation of REEs.

### WLSA-ShanghaiAcademy — ReLeaf: Converting Agricultural and Garden Waste into Bioethanol with Five Engineered Pichia pastoris Strains
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6087) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/wlsa-shanghaiacademy)

Garden waste, including leaves and branches, and agricultural residues, such as straw, corn cobs, and wheat stalks, are often burned or landfilled, causing air pollution, land consumption, and resource loss. ReLeaf aims to convert this lignocellulosic biomass into renewable bioethanol.



Using Pichia pastoris GS115 and the pPIC9K expression system, we produced five lignocellulose-degrading enzymes: laccase, endoglucanase, cellobiohydrolase, β-glucosidase, and xylanase. Measured activities included 1.66 U/mL for laccase, 2.58 U/mL for xylanase, and 0.283 U/mL total cellulase activity on Avicel. We also explored magnetic-bead-based enzyme recovery for potential reuse.



Our design combines enzymatic hydrolysis with yeast fermentation, using Saccharomyces cerevisiae to ferment glucose and engineered P. pastoris to utilize xylose. Across three Design–Build–Test–Learn cycles, we investigated enzyme production, biomass hydrolysis, and fermentation, assessing the feasibility of lignocellulosic waste valorization while emphasizing biosafety through cell-free enzyme preparations.

### Worldshaper-Hangzhou — AirNova: An Enzyme-Powered Odor-Control Spray for Enclosed Public Spaces
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6495) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/worldshaper-hangzhou)

Odor control is a critical challenge in crowded public spaces. Conventional methods, such as fragrance masking or chemical sprays, are often irritating and only temporary. To solve this, we engineered a bacterial system to produce a mix of natural enzymes that destroy bad smells at the molecular level. These enzymes break down foul-smelling compounds into completely odorless, non-volatile substances. Applied as a textile spray, the product remains on surfaces and is activated by ambient humidity to lock in and continuously degrade odors. This provides a safe, long-lasting, and sustainable biopurification solution for transit systems and shared indoor spaces.

### YK Pao — The Golden Cicada’s Escape: A Smart-Sensing, Self-Floating Harvester for Heavy Metal Ions in Water
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6183) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/yk-pao)

Heavy metal pollution in water, including lead, cadmium, and mercury, threatens ecosystems and human health. This project proposes a synthetic biology-based “capture-and-recovery” system using engineered E. coli BL21(DE3). A surface-display module, Lpp–OmpA–SmtA, is designed to bind extracellular metal ions, enhancing adsorption while reducing intracellular toxicity. Metal-responsive ZntR and PzntA regulation activates downstream functions in the presence of Zn²⁺, Cd²⁺, or Pb²⁺. Upon metal detection, the bacteria express antigen 43 (Ag43) to promote self-aggregation and gas-vesicle proteins (GVPs) to generate buoyancy. The cells are expected to form visible floating biorafts that can be collected with simple floating barriers or filters. By integrating sensing, adsorption, aggregation, and flotation, this system aims to enable low-cost, recoverable remediation of contaminated water while reducing secondary-pollution risks.

## Conservation (22)

### ABOA — Benzophe-none
*FIN · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6245) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/aboa)

Benzophenone-3 is an aromatic compound that has been commonly used in cosmetics, sunscreen and other items such as plastic as an UV filter. It is harmful to aquatic animals and coral because it is an endocrine disruptor. This has led to its use being reduced, but it is still used in controlled amounts in Asia, America and Europe. Since it is commonly used, harmful to the environment and current waste water treatment plants can’t remove it completely, we decided on a biological approach. Normal biological waste water treatment is inconsistent so our goal is to investigate a new proposed benzophenone breakdown pathway from Rhodococcus Oxybenzonivorans and try to incorporate it into current wastewater treatment plants. The pathway is new and experimental data is very limited. We chose to test two of the breakdown steps in E.coli to figure out how it could be used to remove benzophenone from waste water.

### Alma — Com-BAT-ing White Nose Syndrome
*USA · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6261) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/alma)

Pseudogymnoascus destructans (Pd) is a pathogenic fungus responsible for White‑nose syndrome (WNS). Since its emergence, WNS has caused catastrophic declines in bat populations across North America. WNS develops during the bat hibernation period, when proteolytic enzymes produced by Pd degrade collagen in the nose, ears, and wings. This tissue damage forces bats to arouse prematurely from hibernation, triggering abnormal winter activity, rapid energy depletion, and ultimately death by starvation due to the absence of seasonal food resources. Despite the severity of this crisis, no effective large‑scale treatment currently exists. Our project proposes engineering Escherichia coli to secrete serine protease inhibitor(s) that target the key virulence factor (destructin-1) driving tissue degradation. By blocking collagen breakdown, these inhibitors would help bats maintain uninterrupted hibernation, improving survival outcomes and offering a promising biological strategy to protect one of our most essential ecological allies.

### Athens — ApiconIASIS
*GRC · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6478) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/athens)

European Foulbrood is a bacterial disease of honeybee brood caused by Melissococcus plutonius. Infected larvae die before pupation, weakening or collapsing the colony. Across Europe control rests on destruction: past a defined level of infection the colony is destroyed and its frames burned. Antibiotics are unauthorised in the EU, no residue limit exists for honey, resistant strains are documented, and broad-spectrum treatment depletes the gut community that suppresses it.

ApiconIASIS is a targeted enzyme therapy from the pathogen’s own genome. Phages infecting M. plutonius encode endolysins, peptidoglycan hydrolases that evolved to lyse it. We screened published genomes for dormant prophages, ranked their lytic enzymes, and carried two forward. Both were expressed in Escherichia coli, purified, and assayed against a strain of reduced virulence; optical density fell. Neither acted on two honeybee gut commensals of the same order. The agent is a purified, non-replicating protein; the production strain stays contained.

### BNUZH-China — PineProtector: An Engineered Bacillus subtilis Platform for Dual Control of Pine Wilt Disease
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6336) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/bnuzh-china)

Pine wilt disease (PWD), caused by the pine-wood nematode (Bursaphelenchus xylophilus) and transmitted mainly by the Japanese pine sawyer (Monochamus alternatus), is a devastating forest disease; infected pines can wilt and die within 40 days. We propose PineProtector, a synthetic-biology-based platform that engineers Bacillus subtilis to colonize pine trunks and express two proteins with nematicidal and insecticidal activity against B. xylophilus and M. alternatus. Specifically, the engineered strain expresses the alkaline serine protease BLG4 under phenolic acid induced system to target nematodes and the Bacillus thuringiensis toxin Cry3Aa to target the vector insect. To ensure biosafety, a KillerRed-based suicide switch is incorporated: when the bacteria leave the pine interior and are exposed to sunlight, KillerRed produces ROS, which in turn trigger bacterial suicide. PineProtector is expected to provide a green, low-cost, and durable new strategy for the control of pine wilt disease, with the ultimate goal of protecting pine forests.

### DNHS-SanDiego-Ca — Limiting Coral Bleaching By Targeting Pathogenic V. Coralliilyticus Using Engineered Bacterial Secretion of Phage-Derived Endolysin
*USA · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6188) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/dnhs-sandiego-ca)

Increased ocean temperatures are rapidly accelerating the severity of coral bleaching events worldwide. Thermal stress weakens coral immune defenses, triggers production of toxic reactive oxygen species (ROS), and above 27°C, activates virulence in the pathogen Vibrio coralliilyticus, further harming the coral host. Current treatments struggle to target this pathogen without disrupting the coral holobiont. To address this, our team engineered coral symbiont Bacillus subtilis to secrete Lysdvp001, a phage-derived endolysin that lyses V. coralliilyticus, under a promoter activated by high concentrations of ROS, matching the conditions when the risk of virulence is the highest. Experimental validation demonstrated that our engineered coral probiotic remains comparable to a wild-type environment under low-stress conditions and inhibits V. coralliiyticus when exposed to ROS. Thus, we have created a proactive, biosensing intervention strategy that protects vulnerable coral ecosystems from pathogen-driven bleaching while preserving the natural balance of the coral holobiont.

### GHCIS — ToeholdX: From AMR Detection to a Universal RNA Sensing Platform
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6166) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/ghcis)

Antimicrobial resistance (AMR) is rapidly emerging as one of the greatest threats to global health. Resistance genes such as MCR-1, NDM-1, and KPC-2, which undermine last-resort antibiotics including colistin and carbapenems, are increasingly spreading through environmental pathways such as agricultural water systems. However, current detection methods rely on expensive instruments and specialized laboratories, making large-scale field surveillance difficult. To tackle this challenge, we developed a portable, low-cost diagnostic platform based on synthetic biology. Our system employs computationally designed RNA Toehold Switches to specifically recognize multiple antimicrobial resistance genes and generate distinct colorimetric outputs in a cell-free expression system. Coupled with RPA isothermal amplification and paper-based detection, the platform enables rapid, user-friendly, and equipment-free testing. By bringing AMR monitoring out of the laboratory and into the field, our project aims to support early warning, environmental surveillance, and more accessible public health protection.

### GYHS — ReefBond: A Bifunctional Bioinspired Adhesive for Coral Reef Restoration
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6283) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/gyhs)

Secure attachment of coral fragments to the reef substrate remains a critical bottleneck in coral reef restoration. Conventional fixation strategies—cement, epoxy resins, and mechanical fasteners—face substantial limitations in underwater settings, including poor handling, long curing times, and the release of non-biodegradable materials into the marine environment. Inspired by mussel adhesion, the ReefBond project has designed and expressed a bifunctional fusion protein comprising mussel adhesive protein domains coupled to a mineralization-inducing peptide. Preliminary characterization demonstrates strong underwater adhesion and mineralization-promoting activity. Ongoing and future work will assess its adhesion performance and stability under marine conditions, its biodegradation profile, and its biocompatibility with coral tissue and other reef-associated organisms. By offering a biodegradable, bio-derived alternative to synthetic adhesives, ReefBond aims to provide a safer and more sustainable tool for coral outplanting and reef restoration, contributing to the protection of marine ecosystems.

### HG-Tokyo — Recombinant Endolysin-royalisin for Invasive Gram-positive Neutralization (R.E.I.G.N.) - A Sustainable Strategy Against American Foulbrood
*JPN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6352) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/hg-tokyo)

Honeybees are essential to the agricultural, food, and cosmetic industries, but their populations are increasingly threatened by American foulbrood (AFB), a highly contagious bacterial disease caused by Paenibacillus larvae. Current strategies require the destruction of infected hives, often through burning. To tackle this problem, we are leveraging royalisin, a natural antimicrobial peptide found in royal jelly. While royalisin exhibits activity against Gram-positive bacteria, P. larvae’s protective cell envelope and distinctive surface structures may hinder its effectiveness. To overcome this, we engineered a genetic construct in E. coli that produces genetically fused royalisin and P. larvae phage endolysin PlyPl23, creating a synergistic system that attacks the bacterium through complementary mechanisms. We envision application via a spray containing purified endolysin-royalisin together with uric acid and L-tyrosine, targeting both vegetative P. larvae cells and dormant endospores. By eliminating the pathogen without destroying colonies, we provide beekeepers a sustainable alternative to current control methods.

### Hong Kong-CUHK — BeeTrix: A Self‑Sustaining Biocontrol System Against Varroa Mites
*HKG · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6171) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/hong-kong-cuhk)

We are developing BeeTrix, a biological control system that leverages engineered Pseudomonas putida to continuously produce hop beta acids (lupulone) directly inside honeybee hives for sustainable Varroa mite management. Our biological system mimics a contact‑based killing mechanism, disrupting mite cellular ion balance and inducing oxidative stress with lupulone, without harming bees or contaminating honey. By using living bacteria as self‑renewing factories, BeeTrix overcomes the short half‑life of pure lupulone and eliminates the need for repeated chemical applications. Our aim is to create a single‑application, season‑long Varroa control solution that is safe, effective, and easily adopted by beekeepers worldwide.

### IISER-Kolkata — FINS - Fish Intersex Neutralization Strategy
*IND · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6450) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/iiser-kolkata)

Estrogenic pollutants like estrone (E1) and 17β-estradiol (E2) enter waterways through wastewater, causing feminization, intersex conditions and fish population collapse. Our engineered E. coli carries two modules. The sensing and degradation module uses the EdcR repressor and its Pb promoter to detect estrogens, triggering a T7 RNA polymerase cascade that secretes BPUL laccase, which inactivates them by oligomer formation. The kill switch module expresses λ-phage Holin and T4 Endolysin to lyse the bacteria after treatment, preventing environmental release. We modelled the circuit deterministically and stochastically, and studied estrogen diffusion to test feasibility. Constants come from literature or multi-paper estimates, and sensitivity and error analyses support real-world use. For deployment, we designed tanks, columns and UV treatment tanks to neutralise runaway bacteria. Calculations, simulations and wet-lab experiments complemented one another, revealing the circuit's true behaviour.

### IL Tech Chicago — SynBeeGee
*USA · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6469) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/il-tech-chicago)

Honeybees are essential to agriculture and ecosystem health, yet pesticide exposure threatens their survival. Organophosphate pesticides can disrupt neurological function and contribute to declining colony health. SynBeeGee, developed by the Illinois Institute of Technology iGEM team, investigates a synthetic biology strategy to protect honey bees by engineering their native gut microbiome.

Our project focuses on Snodgrassella alvi, a bacterium naturally associated with the honey bee gut. By introducing genetic machinery for organophosphate degradation, we aim to enable this microorganism to break down harmful compounds and reduce pesticide exposure within its host. This approach could complement existing pollinator protection strategies without requiring farmers to abandon pest management practices.

SynBeeGee combines genetic engineering, laboratory experimentation, computational design, and stakeholder engagement to assess technical feasibility, biosafety, and practical implementation. Through collaboration with beekeepers, researchers, and agricultural stakeholders, the project explores how engineered microbial partnerships might support healthier colonies and more sustainable agricultural systems.

### Lambert-GA — SWARM: Protecting the Hive
*USA · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6185) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/lambert-ga)

Honeybee populations face a growing threat from Varroa destructor mites, which spread deformed wing virus (DWV), a major driver of colony losses worldwide. Between April 2024 and April 2025, U.S. beekeepers lost 55.6% of managed colonies, threatening pollinators essential to global agriculture.

Lambert iGEM developed SWARM, a two-step approach targeting both the virus and its vector. The first component is a de novo minibinder designed to neutralize DWV, the first experimentally validated binder against an iflavirus, designed at less than 1% of typical industry costs. The second is an RNA interference system targeting three genes critical to mite survival.

Supporting this work, we built PRINCE, a software for identifying favorable protein-binding regions, and Vapor-X, a low-cost hardware for vacuum-drying reagents. Through inclusive outreach and education, SWARM advances the United Nations Sustainable Development Goals and supports a sustainable future for global pollinators.

### Nanjing-China — PhosArmor
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6083) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/nanjing-china)

Metal corrosion reduces the service life of equipment, generates solid waste, and necessitates frequent anti-corrosion treatments for metallic components. However, traditional metal anti-corrosion methods often cause environmental pollution.  PhosArmor explores a synthetic biology approach to corrosion protection that prioritizes environmental health. We engineered *Shewanella oneidensis* MR-1 to overexpress *phoU*, a gene encoding a regulator of phosphate homeostasis, to promote protective mineralization on metal surfaces. Comparative strain testing, electrochemical characterization, and mineral analysis support its potential for corrosion protection under laboratory conditions. We also designed a light-controlled kill switch intended to enable strain elimination after mineralization, pending functional validation. 

By combining biologically driven surface protection with biocontainment design, PhosArmor aims to reduce corrosion-related material waste and dependence on chemically intensive maintenance. The project provides a foundation for developing more sustainable wastewater infrastructure management.

### NU Boston — Soil Shield
*USA · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6300) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/nu-boston)

Polycyclic aromatic hydrocarbons (PAHs) persist in soil and can bioaccumulate, posing risks to agricultural, environmental, and urban systems. To solve this problem, we developed a bacterial bioremediation system by engineering Pseudomonas Putida KT2440 to degrade naphthalene, a representative PAH, in dry, post-wildfire soil conditions, where analogous soil bacteria die. We investigated a salicylate-dependent kill-switch with flow cytometry methods and assessed salicylate degradation and the effectiveness of hydroxyectoine as an osmoprotectant with plate-reader assays and a purpose-built soil chamber. We also designed biodegradable alginate beads for bacterial delivery into soil and created an application to define environmental conditions and map pollution to optimize deployment of our bacteria.

### NWAFU-China — AMPlify: AI-Designed Antimicrobial Peptides for Animal Health and Conservation
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6084) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/nwafu-china)

Antimicrobial resistance demands new approaches that balance antibacterial activity, host compatibility, and environmental responsibility. Inspired by dairy-goat mastitis management, AMPlify integrates AI-guided antimicrobial peptide design with experimental validation and a One Health perspective. Our generative model, TAM-Flow, generated 10,000 sequences, from which seven candidates underwent molecular dynamics simulations and in vitro evaluation. Three candidates exhibited relatively broad antibacterial activity within the tested strain panel, while hemolysis and cytotoxicity assays informed iterative refinement. Recombinant production yielded a fraction with antibacterial activity. Complementary software evaluates peptide properties and explores degradation, environmental fate, and resistance pressure. Stakeholder engagement shaped research priorities, while multilingual materials and interactive games supported public education. Together, AMPlify connects computational discovery, experimental evidence, production research, and societal needs to support responsible development of antimicrobial peptides for animal health.

### Peking — TermiTox: Engineered Yeast for Targeted Biocontrol of Termites in Wooden Heritage Conservation
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6366) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/peking)

Historic timber structures across China—palaces, temples, and old residences—are threatened by termites which cause hidden damage that undermines their preservation. Current control relies on chemical pesticides, which persist and harm non-target organisms. In our iGEM project, we engineered Saccharomyces cerevisiae as a bait-based delivery chassis; termites acquire it from a contained station and spread it via trophallaxis. Guided by termite life history—wood digestion relies on the termite and its gut protists—we designed two complementary modules: a pH-sensitive H311A LLO variant that targets gut protists, and an RNAi module with two single-hairpin constructs targeting RsEG (cellulase) and Hexamerin-1 (caste regulation). A glucanase-assisted release module aids payload escape. Biosafety is enforced by hypoxia- and acetate-responsive promoters, sequence specificity, and physical containment. Overall, TermiTox aims to provide targeted, modular, and environmentally responsible termite control for wooden heritage and forest ecosystems.

### RDFZ-China — GlacierGuard: Engineering a biodegradable bacterial cellulose material for glacier protection via water retention
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6409) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/rdfz-china)

Glacier retreat is accelerating under global climate change, threatening ecosystems and water resources. Existing glacier-covering materials are mainly made from plastics or synthetic fibers, which may cause secondary pollution after long-term use.

Our project aims to develop a biodegradable glacier-protection material based on bacterial cellulose (BC). Owing to its high water-retention capacity, BC can absorb and retain meltwater during the daytime. Under low nighttime temperatures, the retained water can refreeze, reducing net ice loss and helping preserve glacier mass. Produced by engineered microorganisms, BC is renewable, environmentally friendly, and biodegradable.

By utilizing the natural water-holding properties of bacterial cellulose, this project seeks to provide a sustainable bio-based strategy for mitigating glacier melting and protecting vulnerable glacier ecosystems.

### REC-CHENNAI — THULIR – Technology for Harnessing Unique stress-responsive proteins for Long-term Integrated conservation of Recalcitrant seeds
*IND · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6153) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/rec-chennai)

Seeds are the lifeline of plants, facilitating their propagation and conservation. Orthodox seeds, such as rice, beans can withstand conventional seed banking methods, remain viable for extended periods. In contrast, recalcitrant seeds are highly sensitive to dehydration and freezing, making their long-term preservation challenging, endangering the conservation of ecologically important plant species. This is due to high expression of Late Embryogenesis-Abundant (LEA) proteins in orthodox seeds where it protects the seeds from stress. THULIR provides a solution by engineering the LEA domains with metal ion binding motif and a cell-penetrating peptide, that facilitates their delivery into the embryonic axis of the Artocarpus heterophyllus seeds. LEA proteins are engineered with pET (blank) vector and expressed in E. coli BL21, purified through His-tag/Ni-NTA column chromatography. Once delivered, the engineered LEA proteins enhance the cryo-stress tolerance and improve their survival without modifying the plant genome, facilitating a sustainable strategy for long-term cryopreservation.

### SarvBiome — *(no title)*
*CAN · None · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6550)

*(no abstract published)*

### Shanghai-city — Sentinel of Light Pollution: Fluorescent Biosensors for Sensing Light Pollution
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6481) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/shanghai-city)

Light pollution is an increasingly severe global environmental problem, disrupting circadian rhythms of organisms and destabilizing ecosystems. Traditional light detectors rely on electronic hardware, which are costly and lack biological relevance to reflect how living organisms perceive light signals. Our project develops fluorescent biosensors as biological sentinels to detect light pollution.

### UNILausanne — Detect, Disarm, Defend: A Multi-Layered Strategy Against the Crayfish Plague
*CHE · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6045) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/unilausanne)

Crayfish plague, caused by Aphanomyces astaci, has pushed native European crayfish toward extinction. Invasive North American crayfish spread the pathogen while remaining resistant. Guided by discussions with experts and stakeholders, we developed a multi-layered strategy. Detect: We developed a LAMP (loop-mediated isothermal amplification) assay for in-field monitoring, validated for specificity, sensitivity, and robustness, and built a portable incubator to run LAMP in the field. Disarm: We propose RNA interference to silence genes encoding KPI2 and KPILA, which confer resistance in the invasive host. We engineered the necessary double-stranded RNA-producing plasmids and showed that four terminators on either side of the gene significantly increased dsRNA yield. Defend: We cloned, expressed, and purified KPI2 and KPILA, hypothesizing that exogenous delivery could improve native crayfish survival. We adapted a water-in-oil-in-water emulsion into alginate-chitosan feed beads for oral delivery. Together, this multi-layered strategy equips us to preserve native crayfish in Swiss rivers and lakes.

### UZurich — SeisMIC - UZurich 2026: Stabilizing the soil ahead of us
*CHE · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6269) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/uzurich)

Shallow landslides threaten settlements, infrastructure, and ecosystems in the Swiss Alps. SeisMIC investigates microbially induced calcite precipitation (MICP) as a potential approach to stabilising shallow alpine soils. Our project addresses three main challenges: bacterial activity at low temperatures, controlled calcium carbonate precipitation, and environmental impacts.



In the wet lab, we investigated directed evolution of Sporosarcina pasteurii (S. pasteurii) for cold adaptation and strategies to regulate urease expression and carbonate formation. Cold-adapted populations showed shorter lag phases under cold conditions and retained qualitative precipitation ability. Engineered Escherichia coli strains exhibited induction-associated urease responses, while carbonic anhydrase results remained open. 



Complementary models explored slope stability and treatment feasibility, while Human Practices integrated stakeholder perspectives. SeisMIC establishes a foundation for further research, highlighting the need to validate treatment efficacy, environmental safety, and practical applicability before real-world deployment.


## Diagnostics (45)

### Aptalxero — *(no title)*

### ASU — BaylyEYE: A Portable Whole-Cell Biosensor for Tuberculosis and Drug Resistance
*USA · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6372) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/asu)

Tuberculosis (TB) is treatable, yet barriers to diagnosis and detection of drug resistance continue to delay effective care (WHO). In response, our team developed BaylyEYE, a whole-cell biosensor platform designed to detect TB DNA and identify a mutation linked to rifampicin resistance. BaylyEYE uses the natural ability of Acinetobacter baylyi to take up DNA: matching TB rpoB DNA repairs an interrupted sensing region, restoring antibiotic resistance as an initial detection readout. We plan to add a CRISPR interference module targeting D435V to distinguish this mutation from the wild-type sequence. We also designed bioluminescent reporters to explore distinguishable optical outputs for future multiplexed detection. A portable kit combines incubation controls, optical sensing, and software to measure reporter signals. By connecting biological sensing with portable instrumentation, BaylyEYE aims to provide an accessible foundation for TB DNA detection that future teams can adapt to additional resistance mutations and other pathogens.

### BAID-China — Neurofilux: Early screening of neurodegenerative diseases based on a sandwich antibody assay for NfL protein
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6089) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/baid-china)

Neurodegenerative diseases cause irreversible neuronal damage several years before the symptoms appear. Neurofilament light chain (NfL) is a promising blood biomarker, but existing detection methods are either costly or have low sensitivity, making large-scale screening difficult. Therefore, we developed Neurofilux, a low-cost and highly sensitive NfL blood testing system. We identified NfL antigen fragments, designed and screened antibodies using RF antibody, and identified two pairs of highly sensitive antibodies using the sandwich ELISA method. Further, we optimized the design of nanobodies to improve stability and reduce costs. Our future work will iteratively optimize antibody sensitivity through in vitro experiments and adopt digital ELISA technology to achieve a detection sensitivity of up to fg/mL. This detection platform provides an economical and non-invasive solution for the early screening of neurodegenerative diseases.

### BIT — SENSE-CE: Synthetic Biology-Enabled Non-invasive Screening for Endometriosis
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6360) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/bit)

SENSE-CE explores menstrual effluent to study endometriosis-associated inflammation, aiming to develop an accessible testing approach. We are developing a paper-based aptamer sensor integrated into menstrual products for earlier clinical assessment. The sensing panel targets IL-6, IL-10, and TNF-α. Our design combines TAMRA-labelled aptamers with TCPP-loaded UiO-66, where target binding induces conformational changes to restore quenched fluorescence. Computational tools (NucleoLoop, molecular docking, GROMACS) guide aptamer ranking and structural analysis. Experimental work includes material quenching studies, paper-chip testing, and a custom reader with FluoroCheck software for image analysis. Human Practices address public needs. Next steps involve establishing repeatable measurements and evaluating performance in real menstrual samples.

### BK-Denmark — Cocktail Control – An Investigation into the Design of a Methanol Biosensor
*DNK · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6364) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/bk-denmark)

Methanol poisonings occur as a result of the adulteration of alcoholic beverages, in which methanol is added for profit or during illicit alcohol production. A simple, inexpensive methanol detection test offers a prevention strategy against methanol poisoning. We present two biosensor systems with colorimetric outputs and a hardware sensor design. The systems rely on alcohol oxidase from Phanerochaete chrysosporium (PcAOX) to selectively detect methanol. PcAOX was genetically engineered to enhance its selectivity towards methanol, resulting in seven variants. Molecular simulations showed that four variants exhibited increased selectivity towards methanol relative to WT selectivity. The variants were expressed in Escherichia coli, and the presence of purified proteins was confirmed with Western blotting, although enzymatic activity could not be detected in kinetic assays. An investigation into the reduction of food colorants in a beverage sample using ultrafiltration was conducted. The results suggest that ultrafiltration could be incorporated into the hardware design.

### BYU-Provo — ChompSafe – Fostering Independence for Children with Oral Food Allergies
*USA · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6421) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/byu-provo)

Children with oral food allergies experience increased stress and anxiety as they navigate what to eat each day. The burden extends not only to kids, but also to their caregivers. ChompSafe serves to reduce the mental health burden for both children and their parents. The solution is a dinosaur-shaped device that a child can carry like a toy, to play and to school. The dinosaur detects the presence of food allergens using aptamer technology via an automated extraction, detection, and cleaning process. The device is paired with the caregiver’s phone to relay device information in real time, allowing trusted adults to stay aware of their children. Each action taken allows both children and caregivers to feel safe, secure, and in control of their well-being.

### Charlotte-HS — PanCrisp - A Non-Invasive Cas13a Diagnostic for Early Detection of Pancreatic Cancer
*USA · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6442) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/charlotte-hs)

Pancreatic ductal adenocarcinoma (PDAC) ranks 11th in incidence but 6th in cancer mortality, a gap driven almost entirely by late diagnosis. Current diagnostics are confirmatory rather than preventive, and no routine screening protocol exists even for high-risk patients. PanCrisp is a non-invasive CRISPR diagnostic that detects salivary miRNA biomarkers of early pancreatic tumorigenesis using LwaCas13a. We designed and validated crRNAs against a five-miRNA panel, contributing 19 parts to the Registry. Each target runs in a separate reaction, so a positive signal identifies the specific miRNA despite Cas13a’s collateral cleavage. Target recognition triggers cleavage of a fluorescent reporter, quantified by plate reader, and our wet lab confirmed Cas13a-mediated cleavage using miR-216a. We built three open software tools for crRNA design, plate reader analysis, and kinetic modeling, and published a synthetic biology book and learning platform to broaden access to the field.

### DelNorte-SD — Intracellular Delivery of Catalytic Hairpin Assembly and Broccoli Aptamers for Biosensing of Aberrant Exon Splicing
*USA · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6227) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/delnorte-sd)

Monitoring pre-mRNA splicing dynamics in live mammalian cells requires non-invasive, high-sensitivity RNA biosensors. Here, we present the adaptation of CHARGE, a catalytic RNA hairpin assembly system originally validated in E. Coli BL21 cells, for the targeted detection of a specific splice junction in human embryonic kidney (HEK293) cells. Re-engineering this platform for eukaryotic culture required iterative engineering optimization to overcome intracellular delivery and RNA stability constraints. We established optimal transfection parameters and constrained post-transfection imaging to a 4–6 hour window to account for cytosolic single-stranded RNA degradation. Live-cell fluorescence microscopy confirmed fluorescence upon fluorophore binding, validating target-driven intracellular Broccoli aptamer reconstitution at elevated hairpin doses. This experimental workflow was complemented by two custom computational tools (Strand Analyzer and Cell Analyzer) for thermodynamic sequence optimization and automated image quantification, establishing an adaptable framework for mammalian RNA biosensing.

### DETEX — *(no title)*
*GHA · None · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6534)

*(no abstract published)*

### Edinburgh — CondenSense: A Modular LLPS Biosensor for Early Drug Toxicity Detection
*GBR · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6407) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/edinburgh)

Standard drug toxicity assays (MTT, LDH, Annexin V) detect metabolic failure and cell death, but miss the earlier window when cells are already stressed and mounting damage responses — a gap linked to the ~30% of late-stage drug failures caused by unexpected organ toxicity. CondenSense detects this earlier signal using liquid-liquid phase separation. Our engineered protein couples an OxyR stress sensor to a FUS disordered region and sfGFP; under sub-lethal stress it condenses into fluorescent puncta within minutes before metabolic output changes. The readout is post-translational, so no new gene expression is needed. The design is modular: swap the sensor domain to target a different stress, keep the same fluorescent readout. We validate in E. coli, then translate to human liver and heart cell lines. All parts go to the iGEM Registry as reusable components.

### Eton-College-UK — ProteaSign: A Multiplexed Electrochemical Assay of Wound Protease Activity
*GBR · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6462) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/eton-college-uk)

Chronic wounds are cuts, sores and ulcers that fail to heal within four to twelve weeks, costing the NHS £8.3 billion annually and sometimes leading to amputation. Early diagnosis and close monitoring would significantly improve outcomes, but current methods are subjective and slow, and no test measures the biochemistry driving delayed healing. ProteaSign quantifies the activities of individual proteases in wound exudate, specifically MMP-9, which degrades the extracellular matrix, and neutrophil serine proteases, which degrade the growth factors needed for repair. Resolving both separately distinguishes the causes of non-healing, indicating relevant treatments. The device is electrochemical, using computationally modelled protease-cleavable peptides that release a redox reporter when protease activity is elevated. To confirm these peptides' per-enzyme specificity, we built recombinant sfGFP reporter proteins, quantified by Ni-NTA depletion and fluorescence. Delivered as a disposable cartridge for community wound care, ProteaSign turns wound assessment from a visual judgement into a measurement.

### GCGS — Dual-Response EcN: An Adjunct Therapy for Pseudomonas Infections in cystic fibrosis Patients
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6242) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/gcgs)

Patients with cystic fibrosis frequently experience recurrent drug-resistant infections due to chronic biofilm infections caused by Pseudomonas aeruginosa in the lungs, and there is a lack of effective targeted clinical treatments. We designed an EcN strain carrying a coupled Fur and RhlR sensing circuit to enable dual-specific recognition. The Fur system is only de-repressed in iron-deprived mucus at the site of infection, thereby enabling spatial localization; meanwhile, RhlR detects the AHL quorum sensing signals released by Pseudomonas aeruginosa. Only when both a low-iron environment and high AHL levels are simultaneously detected is the expression of alginate lyase and lysis E triggered. Lysis E cleaves E. coli, releasing alginate lyase that degrades the biofilm matrix of Pseudomonas aeruginosa, thereby enhancing the efficacy of antibiotics. This synthetic biology design integrates environmental and pathogen sensing functions, establishing a strategy to achieve targeted localization and in situ clearance of biofilms in chronic respiratory infections.

### Hamburg — CasSTAR - Cas-based Specific Testing for Antimicrobial Resistances
*DEU · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6324) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/hamburg)

Antimicrobial resistances (AMRs) are a prominent threat to modern medicine. Diagnosing them is important, both to enable the choice of a fitting antibiotic and to isolate people where necessary.  Currently, this is a lengthy and resource intensive process.  

 We developed a point of care test using LB-Cas12a to identify specific DNA sequences and gold nanoparticles combined with ssDNA based reporters for a visual output. We validated the target specificity, as well as the cis and trans cleavage of the LB-Cas12a and the minimal target concentration for detection. Additionally, we ran successful tests of the optical sensing hardware and obtained promising results for the Recombinant Polymerase Amplification. Besides the hardware design, which was heavily influenced by human practices, our dry lab work focused on research regarding available AMR sequence information. Outside of the lab we took part in numerous events to bring scientific education to interested people of all ages.

### HongKong-HS — AmpliRen - An at-home urine test for earliest molecular warnings of chronic kidney disease
*HKG · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6214) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/hongkong-hs)

Chronic Kidney Disease (CKD) affects approximately 10% of Hong Kong's population, with cases rising and driving high mortality and healthcare burdens. Conventional diagnostic methods like eGFR and uACR often fail to detect early-stage structural kidney damage. To enable accessible early intervention, our project introduces a non-invasive, home-based detection kit, AmpliRen, that targets microRNA (miRNA) biomarkers in urine. 



While initial designs utilized toehold switches with cell-free expression kit for precise sequence recognition, detecting low-abundance miRNAs required enhanced signal amplification. Addressing these limitations, we explored an enzyme-free Hybridization Chain Reaction (HCR). 



Further optimization replaced the initial G-quadruplex (G4)-ThT assay with a hemin-G4/TMB assay to enable colorimetric detection of the target miRNAs. Future work aims to eliminate false-positive background signals caused by spontaneous hairpin opening and non-specific G4 formation. 



This low-cost, enzyme-free kit is designed to achieve high sensitivity with minimal crosstalk, offering a reliable at-home diagnostic tool to improve early CKD management.

### ICT-Mumbai — SWASTh TB- Synthetic Whole-cell Antigen Sensing Test for Tuberculosis
*IND · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6386) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/ict-mumbai)

Tuberculosis, a disease that has plagued the planet for centuries, today stands as the world's biggest infectious killer, causing more deaths than malaria or HIV. Present diagnosis methods of TB are time-consuming, costly, and inaccessible to individuals in developing countries. Further, traditional treatment time ranging from 6-7 months and drugs with potential side effects, reduce the patient compliance, causing TB to develop resistance. It is imperative to develop faster, more efficient diagnostics. We strive to develop a whole-cell biosensor based on Escherichia coli to detect the Pst-S1 protein, an important phosphate-binding protein and a popular biomarker for tuberculosis. The genetically engineered E.coli cells shall detect the biomarker and through a curated two component system, release the enzyme β-Galactosidase. This will hydrolyze a common substrate X-gal to give a blue color for quick detection.

### IIT-Madras — Mycoflip: A Protease-Based Luminescent Biosensor for Intestinal Tuberculosis
*IND · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6075) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/iit-madras)

Intestinal tuberculosis is hard to diagnose because its symptoms overlap with those of other gut diseases. Current tests are invasive or slow, and the faster ones need equipment that rural clinics lack. Mycoflip is a protease-based detection system. An engineered probiotic E. coli expresses FlipNanoLuc, a split-luciferase reporter that an E5–K5 coiled coil holds inactive. In the gut, the engineered bacteria interact with *Mycobacterium tuberculosis*, whose surface proteases Hip1 cleave a site within the coil, allowing the reporter to assemble into active NanoLuc. Luminescence is read in stool after adding furimazine, using a low-cost hand-held silicon photomultiplier luminometer that we designed. Cleavage-site linkers were designed with a multi-objective genetic algorithm. The design was supported by constant-pH molecular dynamics and a reaction–diffusion model of intestinal transport. *M. smegmatis* serves as a BSL-1 surrogate for the target proteases.

### IOANNINA — LEDA
*GRC · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6248) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/ioannina)

Ionizing radiation damages DNA, directly and through the reactive species it creates, whether in one high dose during a radiological incident or in small amounts over years of medical and occupational exposure. The damage is invisible. Dosimeters record the dose that reached the badge, not what it did to the person, and that answer still takes a laboratory.



LEDA (Living Engineered Diagnostic Assay) reads it from a urine sample, using engineered cells and two biomarkers. 8-oxoguanine shows the injury to the DNA itself, detected by an RNA riboswitch that fluoresces red. Cell-free DNA shows the consequence, the cells that died, detected by receptors carrying human Ku70 and Ku80 that fluoresce green. Either alone is ambiguous, together they separate injury from cell death.



A small reader lets a smartphone photograph both, and the app returns a risk level.

### JLU-SPH — Byegerm: A Rapid CRISPR-Cas12a-Based Detection Platform for Upper Respiratory Pathogens
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6074) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/jlu-sph)

Respiratory infections can be caused by many different pathogens, but conventional diagnostic methods are often time-consuming, equipment-dependent, or difficult to apply outside well-equipped laboratories. Our project aims to develop Byegerm, a rapid and accessible detection platform for major upper respiratory pathogens. By combining recombinase polymerase amplification (RPA) with CRISPR-Cas12a-based nucleic acid detection, Byegerm is designed to identify pathogens such as Streptococcus pneumoniae, Bordetella pertussis, Haemophilus influenzae, respiratory syncytial virus, and human metapneumovirus with high specificity and sensitivity. We hope this system can support earlier diagnosis, more timely treatment decisions, and improved respiratory infection surveillance in clinical and resource-limited settings.

### Labi-Taiwan — BlueCatcher: An Integrated Cell-Free Biosensing Platform for Molecular Monitoring of Major Depressive Disorder
*TWN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6511) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/labi-taiwan)

Major Depressive Disorder (MDD) affects millions worldwide, yet its assessment still relies largely on clinical interviews and symptom reporting. BlueCatcher aims to complement these approaches with a portable, cell-free biosensor for detecting MDD-associated circulating microRNAs (miRNAs). We developed an integrated biomarker selection pipeline combining differential expression , miRNA-mRNA interactions, pathway and co-expression analysis, and literature evidence, narrowing transcriptome-wide signals to five miRNA candidates. For detection, target miRNAs bind to a Three-Way Junction (TWJ), releasing a trigger that activates a cell-free toehold switch (THS). This produces a nuclease that cleaves a reporter, converting miRNA recognition into a measurable electrochemical signal. We further developed machine-learning workflows to guide TWJ and THS designs and prioritize candidates to experimental testing. By integrating biomarker discovery, programmable nucleic acids, cell-free amplification, and portable electrochemical sensing, BlueCatcher provides a detection platform for future MDD screening, longitudinal monitoring, and treatment-response assessment alongside existing clinical evaluation.

### Lorraine University — EndoLore : Challenging diagnostic delay
*FRA · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6417) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/lorraine-university)

Endometriosis often remains undiagnosed for years, highlighting the need for earlier and more accessible screening methods. 



EndoLore is a saliva-based biosensor using engineered E. coli, miRNA-responsive toehold switches, genetic signal amplification, and a visual readout. The molecular sensor is complemented by a symptom-based machine learning pipeline. Together, these approaches explore how molecular detection and patient-reported symptoms could support earlier and more reliable endometriosis screening.

### NIS-Kazakhstan — ChemoGuard: A Toolkit for Pre-Treatment DPYD Genotyping to Prevent Severe Fluoropyrimidine Toxicity
*KAZ · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6493) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/nis-kazakhstan)

Fluoropyrimidines are the cornerstone of treatment for colorectal, gastric and breast cancer. In patients with low dihydropyrimidine dehydrogenase (DPD) activity, caused by inherited DPYD variants, standard doses become toxic and can be fatal. International guidelines and regulators now recommend DPYD genotyping before treatment, yet routine testing has entered practice only recently even in high-income health systems, and remains unavailable in Kazakhstan. We therefore developed a modular qPCR system for screening seven AMP Tier 1 DPYD variants. Seven locus-specific assays use allele-specific TaqMan hydrolysis probes labelled with FAM, HEX, ROX or Cy5. Thirty-six oligonucleotides were designed and synthesised: 14 primers, 14 core probes and 8 exploratory probes. Each assay was tested on pooled synthetic allele templates in three technical replicates at 63 °C, alongside a no-template control. All 66 expected probe signals were detected across the seven assays, with no signal in any NTC, establishing a functional prototype for clinical validation.

### NotreDame — VeraDose: Preventing Ethylene Glycol and Diethylene Glycol Contamination in Pediatric Medicines
*USA · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6298) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/notredame)

Pediatric medicines contain sugar syrups that help mask the bitter taste for children. In the process of generating these sugar syrups, toxic byproducts such as ethylene glycol and diethylene glycol can be generated. These byproducts have been the cause of accidental poisoning events in several LMICs due to lack of affordable detection metrics for manufacturers. VeraDose aims to use synthetic biology to identify the presence of these compounds with heightened sensitivity at a low cost, reducing the global risk of these accidental poisoning events.

### NU-Kazakhstan — LUMEN
*KAZ · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6476) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/nu-kazakhstan)

Our team built one pathway from three parts: a urine test, a measuring instrument, and software. A person gives a urine sample. DAScovery, an engineered Escherichia coli biosensor, turns N1,N12-diacetylspermine, a metabolite found at raised levels in the urine of patients with colorectal cancer, into green fluorescence. G-lighten, our 3D-printed reader, measures that fluorescence with the camera of an ordinary smartphone and reports a concentration. Below the threshold, the person goes home and comes back for routine monitoring. Above it, they are referred to a physician for colonoscopy. There ColoRisk reviews the recording of that examination a second time, so that fewer lesions are missed.

### Oneonta — OneHEART
*USA · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6206) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/oneonta)

Heart attack outcomes depend on timely diagnosis and response. Rural communities like ours in Otsego County, located in Central New York State, face barriers to accessing cardiac care. Our project, OneHEART, seeks to reduce barriers to timely, accurate heart attack diagnosis and improve outcomes in our community. Our strategy focuses on immediate-impact actions and long-term tool development to strengthen readiness for cardiac emergencies in New York State. Using synthetic biology, we worked on developing an affordable, portable lateral flow assay that detects cardiac troponin I (cTnI) and heart-type fatty acid-binding protein (h-FABP) using nanobody-based detection elements. Alongside diagnostic development, our Human Practices efforts promoted cardiac preparedness through CPR training, community education, and evaluation of cardiac emergency readiness in New York State schools.

### Puebla — NEUMA: amplification-free detection of lung cancer mutations with dCas9 on a phase-sensitive SPR biosensor
*MEX · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6265) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/puebla)

In Puebla’s public hospitals, most patients with advanced lung cancer never receive the molecular result that would decide their treatment. NEUMA is a biosensor that reads three actionable variants (EGFR exon 19 deletion, EGFR L858R and KRAS G12C) directly from plasma, using catalytically dead Cas9 on a phase-sensitive SPR chip, with no thermal amplification. We designed the recognition complex, validated its geometry against independent structural criteria and simulated it for one microsecond. A Quality-by-Design model carried the chain from plasmid to detection limit and ranked which measurement to make first. Whole-plasmid sequencing then showed that our bicistronic cassette had lost dCas9 through a 52 bp direct repeat, and the model established that mutation alone cannot account for the loss, so we redesigned with separate vectors and in vitro biotinylation. Twenty-five clinicians and three focus groups shaped the output format and the device’s placement in care.

### SenseBio — *(no title)*
*SAU · None · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6561)

*(no abstract published)*

### SRE-China — Prometheus Fire: A One-Pot Cancer Detection System via Sequential Thermal Activation
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6383) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/sre-china)

Current cancer diagnostics, such as qPCR, are costly, time-consuming, and complex, restricting their use in resource-limited settings. To detect ultra-low abundance biomarkers like miR-21, we introduce Prometheus Fire: A One-Pot Cancer Detection System via Sequential Thermal Activation. This portable platform seamlessly integrates Exponential Rolling Circle Amplification (E-RCA) and CRISPR-Cas12a within a single tube. Lysed clinical samples are added directly without purification. At 30 ℃, phi29 polymerase and a Zn2+-dependent DNAzyme drive exponential target amplification. Heating to 60 ℃ then dissociates a custom ssRNA blocker, precisely activating the Cas12a complex to cleave reporters and generate a robust visual signal. Achieving a limit of detection below 1 pM, this rapid, cross-contamination-free system provides a highly cost-effective solution for early cancer screening in primary healthcare facilities.

### SynFRET — *(no title)*
*BRA · None · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6540)

*(no abstract published)*

### SZPU-CHINA — Engineering a Yeast Biosensor for Influenza A HA Recognition and Environmental Risk Screening
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6373) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/szpu-china)

We aim to engineer Saccharomyces cerevisiae to detect influenza A hemagglutinin (HA), targeting future public-space environmental monitoring. Inspired by antigen-gated GPCRs, our design connects HA recognition to yeast MAPK signaling and FUS1-driven reporters.



Through iterative Design–Build–Test–Learn cycles, we developed four modules: reporters, FAR1/SST2 chassis modifications, humanized Gpa1, and antigen gating. Reporter and chassis-engineering modules were constructed and functionally characterized. Computational modeling prioritized nanobody R1a-B6 and proposed interaction-optimization hypotheses.



We tested hM1Dq- and Ste2-based gating designs. Ste2-based constructs retained α-factor-responsive fluorescence, but neither design demonstrated HA-dependent activation, highlighting gating and coupling challenges. We also developed a temperature-controlled optical-readout prototype and incorporated stakeholder feedback to refine application requirements.



Our contributions include reusable genetic parts, characterized engineering resources, and documented design iterations. HA-dependent sensing and air-sampling integration remain unvalidated; this work provides an experimental foundation rather than a deployment-ready detector.

### UBC-Vancouver — FAST - Fluorescent Antibiotic Susceptibility Test
*CAN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6279) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/ubc-vancouver)

Antimicrobial resistance is a major and growing public health threat, so clinicians need faster ways to identify which antibiotics will actually work for a given infection. Unfortunately, conventional antibiotic susceptibility testing can take 24-72 hours, too slow for many time-sensitive treatment decisions, so a rapid, bedside-friendly alternative is needed. To address this gap, we propose a point-of-care antibiotic susceptibility test that employs biosensors on a chip to rapidly determine antibiotic resistance or susceptibility via fluorescent reporter signals.

### UBrawijaya Indonesia — Surface-Displayed De Novo Nanobodies for Bacterial Diagnostics via Cell-Cell Adhesion
*IDN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6286) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/ubrawijaya-indonesia)

Bacteria are an integral part of our lives, playing roles that can be beneficial or harmful to human health. Among them, Enterococcus faecalis is a bacterium associated with opportunistic infections and possesses adhesion mechanisms that enables to bind with various bacteria. One of its adhesion-associated proteins, ACE, facilitates binding to host extracellular matrix components. Therefore, we designed a nanobody with the aim of achieving high affinity and specificity toward a selected target through computational de novo protein design. The design was evaluated through protein characterization and aggregation assays to assess the interaction between the engineered construct and the bacterial target. The results showed that the nanobody was successfully expressed in the transmembrane region when fused with intimin, a membrane-anchoring protein. Furthermore, microscopic analysis indicated an interaction between the engineered construct and the E. faecalis. These findings highlight the potential of computationally designed nanobodies for future applications in diagnostics.

### UFABC-Brazil — Patchlepsy: Biosensor for detecting synthetic tRNA-derived fragments (tRFs) related to epileptic seizures
*BRA · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6389) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/ufabc-brazil)

Epilepsy is one of the most common neurological disorders in the world, affecting almost 1% of the global population, mostly in low- and middle-income countries, such as Brazil. Patchlepsy emerges in this context as a tool to predict seizures in Temporal Lobe Epilepsy using a toehold switch-based biosensor that, when specific biomarkers are detected, changes its structural conformation and produces a colorimetric response. To our knowledge, Patchlepsy introduces the first toehold switches designed for tRNA-derived fragments recognition. The methodology followed the Design-Build-Test-Learn cycle: in Design, the toehold switches were designed in silico with NUPACK; in Build, the plasmids containing the toehold modules were commercially synthetized and Escherichia coli TOP10 and BL21(DE03) were transformed. The circuits were tested in a cell-free TX-TL system and as a whole-cell systems, and the fluorescence/absorbance were quantified. The whole-cell platform expected as idealize, being a initial proof of concept.

### UMA Malaga — Frida: Fast Rare Disease Identification via Detection Amplification
*ESP · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6119) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/uma-malaga)

Newborn rare disease diagnosis currently faces significant geographical disparities, meaning that early detection is often "a question of postcode, not biology". Acknowledging the profound impact of diagnostic delays on patients and families, our team introduces Frida: a scalable, exploratory platform designed to tackle the challenges of early detection and expand the scope of neonatal screening panels.

To systematically address this problem, the project is structured around three core pillars:

Neith (Weaving the platform): The physical diagnostic assay utilising SHERLOCK  and CRISPR-Cas13 technology to reliably detect target genetic sequences.

Diana (Designing the threads): The computational and bioinformatics foundation, this tool automates the diagnostic pipeline by analysing clinical mutation inputs to generate and rank optimised guide RNA candidate sequences for the CRISPR assay.

Athena (Understanding whom we protect): The human-centric approach that ensures our science is woven around a human need, evaluating the societal impact and engaging directly with stakeholders.

### UofGlasgow — Synwave: The Gut-Brain Axis Redefines Alzheimer's Biosensing
*GBR · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6255) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/uofglasgow)

Dementia is projected to affect 1 in 3 people born in Scotland today, yet Alzheimer’s is often diagnosed too late for disease-modifying drugs to work. The Synwave Screening Test targets detection up to 15 years before symptoms emerge. Leveraging the gut-brain axis, we developed a non-invasive biosensor from Lactobacillus gasseri, which adhered to Caco-2 intestinal epithelial cells with a mean adhesion of 1.7%. We modified a modular receptor platform using the transcription factor CadC, devising an scFv-IC16 receptor that binds Amyloid-β, an Alzheimer’s biomarker, coupling its detection to an amplified reporter signal (BBa_26453DYU). Amyloid-β was expressed in E. coli BL21(DE3) for in vitro testing. A nisin-inducible lysis device (BBa_265W9ZUC) doubles as signal release and biocontainment, ensuring a standardized screening period after which modified bacteria are cleared from the gut. Synwave offers a self-administered, non-invasive and quantitative test read from a stool sample, with potential to enable earlier intervention.

### WIST — Acoustic Probiotics: a two-input AND gate reporting colorectal cancer by ultrasound
*TWN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6062) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/wist)

Colorectal cancer is rising in people under 50, below the age at which screening begins. Taiwan's FIT programme starts at 45, so the fastest-growing group of patients sits outside it. We asked whether an engineered probiotic could report a tumour niche without a sample or a colonoscopy. Our E. coli fires only where lactate and nitrate occur together. Default-OFF sensors feed a σ54 HrpR/HrpS AND gate driving gas vesicles that scatter ultrasound, so the readout is a scan. The nitrate arm is characterised and clears an acceptance gate of ON/OFF ≥ 3×, fixed in writing two weeks before the ladder ran. Every construct we ordered was designed through Katana, our fail-closed build engine. With both inputs off the reporter sat at 76% and 59% of its both-on level, capping the fold at 1.31× and 1.69×. The circuit is bounded by its OFF state, where the next build starts.

### WUXI-DSAS — Whole-Cell Gentisic Acid Biosensor with Chemical Creatinine Assay for RCC
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6485) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/wuxi-dsas)

Renal cell carcinoma (RCC) is a common and aggressive urogenital cancer often diagnosed late due to a lack of early symptoms. Recent studies have identified gentisic acid, a phenolic acid elevated in the urine of RCC patients, as a potential non-invasive biomarker. Our project aims to develop a low-cost, easy-to-use early screening method by combining two parallel measurements: (1) a whole-cell bacterial biosensor for gentisic acid detection, based on the CnGtdRAT/PgtdA inducible system from Cupriavidus necator, which converts gentisic acid into the inducer 3-maleylpyruvic acid and triggers fluorescent protein expression; and (2) a chemical colorimetric assay for creatinine to normalize urine concentration. By measuring the gentisic acid-to-creatinine ratio, we can achieve reliable, rapid, and affordable RCC risk assessment using urine samples, suitable for routine screening in low-resource settings.

### XMars — *(no title)*
*USA · None · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6533)

*(no abstract published)*

### YiYe — MirUro: Non-Invasive  Bladder Cancer Diagnosis via miRNA Detection in Urine Using Toehold Switch Biosensors
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6139) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/yiye)

Bladder carcinoma is a common malignancy worldwide, causing around 220,596 deaths annually. Cystoscopy, the gold standard for diagnosis, is invasive, costly and not applicable for routine surveillance. Therefore, it is urgent to develop sensitive, convenient techniques for regular monitoring. Urinary miRNAs serve as stable, accessible biomarkers for bladder cancer. They show better sensitivity and specificity than urinary cytology for early detection, and are ideal for non-invasive screening and routine surveillance. Here, we establish a cell-free synthetic biology platform for non-invasive bladder cancer detection based on urinary miRNAs. Using custom-designed toehold switch RNA biosensors, the platform targets three cancer-specific miRNAs that are abnormally expressed and highly enriched in the urine of bladder cancer patients. Binding of target miRNAs activates the toehold switch and induces EGFP expression, generating quantifiable fluorescence signals. This system allows convenient non-invasive quantification of urinary miRNAs in clinical samples.

### ZJUT-China — G-SCOUT （Gut-specific Synthetic Cellular Optimized Unit for Tumor-detection）
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6382) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/zjut-china)

CRC is a high incidence of malignant tumors in the world. It has been a severe pubic health issues that patients in the middle or late stage do not recover well after surgery, and the cost of treatment has risen sharply.Conducting early diagnosis and risk warning of diseases are key measures to improve the survival rate of patients, lower the social medical burden and promote the pattern of disease treatment.Currently, the mainstream clinical screening methods have defects such as strong invasiveness and low compliance of the population.Our project is based on synthetic biology technology to transform engineering probiotics, integrates functional modules such as colonization, signal perception, report color development, and build a novel non-invasive screening system.Aiming to develop a convenient and budget-saving early tumor detection technology, providing a brand-new plan for clinical diagnosis for early CRC.

### ZZU — ARGO-ZZU: PfAgo Dual-Target Magnetic Bead-Aptamer Displacement Platform for AD Early Screening
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6217) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/zzu)

ARGO-ZZU is a dual-mode AD early screening platform that integrates magnetic bead-aptamer displacement with PfAgo signal amplification for ultrasensitive detection of p-tau181 and p-tau231. It provides fluorescence assays for hospital settings and lateral flow strips for community-based screening. Designed to address the three barriers of invasiveness, high cost, and limited reach, the platform is developed in collaboration with clinicians, patients, and industry partners, and is cost-effective, accessible, and deployable in resource-limited settings.


## Fashion & Cosmetics (28)

### Baltimore — KeraTINT: The colorful cure to hair damage
*USA · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6329) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/baltimore)

Most hair dyes are harmful for both hair and skin. These toxic chemicals are environmentally damaging and include oxidative chemicals that remove the hair’s cuticle layer. KeraTINT is a safer, environmentally friendly hair dye that both dye and repair hair. KeraTINT uses highly specific keratin-binding peptides (KBP) fused to chromoproteins and expresses the fusion in bacteria. While other hair dyes harm hair structure, KeraTINT actually restores damaged hair because the KBP penetrates into the hair structure, binds keratin, and repairs the damaged inner layers. To provide a wide range of colors, Escherichia coli were engineered to produce various colors, such as blue (aeblue and amilCP chromoproteins), pink (anm2CP), magenta (mScarlet-i), red (mkate), yellow (amilGFP), and green (sfgfp). The proteins were successfully expressed and purified and can be used to treat hair samples. We developed unique assays to test our dye and its ability to color a range of hair types.

### CAFA-LZU — PHOTOSYNTHETIC FRAGRANCE: A Light-Controlled Yeast Fragrance Synthesis System
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6209) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/cafa-lzu)

To bridge the gap between bio-art and laboratory biological research, we launched the PHOTOSYNTHETIC FRAGRANCE project this year. In this project, we designed and developed an engineered yeast strain capable of synthesizing linalool and limonene under the induction of red and blue light, respectively. Additionally, through our unique "Articulation" initiative, we connected scientific research with artistic expression, curating a series of bio-art exhibitions and works.

### Cataverse-Union — A Multicolor Cultural Relic In Situ Restoration System Based on CBM Fusion Enzyme Technology
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6181) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/cataverse-union)

Polychrome artifacts, such as those found in the Beijing Forbidden City, represent an important cultural heritage of human civilization. However, prolonged exposure to environmental factors such as light causes these binding materials to gradually age, leading to pigment fading, powdering, or flaking and detachment.We heterologously expressed key enzymes involved in the biosynthesis of different pigments in Escherichia coli, and employed in vitro enzymatic catalysis to convert cheap and readily available precursors into high-value biopigments. To address the issue of poor photostability, we innovatively fused a cellulose-binding module with the pigment synthases, enabling the produced pigments to be directionally deposited and stably bound to the surface of paper or silk fibers through the anchoring function of the enzymes. This project not only revives ancient colors but also breathes new life into traditional pigments through engineering approaches.

### China-HUBU-Wuhan — Green Biosynthesis of Fragrance Isoambrettolide by Candida tropicalis
*CHN · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6050) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/china-hubu-wuhan)

Isoambrettolide is a valuable macrocyclic musk fragrance widely used in high‑end perfume and personal‑care products. Conventional plant extraction suffers from unstable supply, while chemical synthesis involves complicated procedures, massive solvent consumption and heavy environmental burdens. In this project, we constructed a Candida tropicalis cell factory with palm oil as renewable feedstock for isoambrettolide production, adopting an intracellular‑in vitro decoupled modular strategy. Competing metabolic pathways were knocked out to redirect carbon flux. Functional genetic elements were screened and fermentation conditions optimized for intracellular precursor accumulation. The challenging macrolactonization step was completed by lipase‑mediated in‑vitro catalysis to reduce host metabolic burden. This biomanufacturing route offers a green alternative for macrocyclic musk fragrances. Furthermore, the chassis can be extended to produce other macrocyclic‑lactone aroma chemicals. Further process optimization will be performed to enhance production performance.

### Concordia-Shanghai — SCOPE - Sustainable Cellulose: Optimizing Products for Everyone
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6361) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/concordia-shanghai)

Our project rests on two pillars: sustainability for the planet and accessibility in the lab. Bacterial cellulose (BC) is prized for its strength, purity and biocompatibility; environmental economists we consulted identified BC-based beauty masks as a near immediate commercial application. Life cycle analysis against four substrates found BC used the least water (6.3 mL per functional unit) but had higher carbon impacts. Upscaling BC therefore rests on yield per unit energy and feedstock - allowing us to set engineering goals.
