Native Komagataeibacter strains grow slowly and mutate into non-producers, so we pursued E. coli recombinant synthesis: overexpressing phosphoglucomutase and UDP-glucose pyrophosphorylase across constitutive, inducible and cold-inducible promoters to relieve the precursor bottleneck, and modeling alanine substitutions at BcsA’s Glu370–Arg583 salt bridge to predict a c-di-GMP-independent synthase. In parallel we built HALO, a wheelchair-mounted modular lab organizer co-designed with wheelchair-using scientists, removing a physical barrier to participation in synthetic biology.

### Cornell — Klothos: Engineering Anti-Fibrotic Wound Healing through Electrospinning with Arachne
*USA · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6122) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/cornell)

Skin is central to our story and self-expression. However, after injury, the body often fails to restore its original appearance, as overexpressed repair pathways can end in permanent fibrotic scarring. This excessive accumulation of scar tissue impacts skin texture, self-esteem, and mobility for over 100 million people each year. Team Cornell proposes Klothos, a novel platform to divert pathways from fibrosis while ensuring sustainable and accessible delivery. We developed a gated probiotic system that releases anti-fibrotic peptides when wound-site molecules indicate unchecked remodeling. Engineered probiotics are spun into an alginate nanofiber matrix with our novel coaxial electrospinner, enabling localized, sustained peptide delivery in a smart bandage. Klothos is a scalable platform for adaptive wound care that redefines the cosmeceutical market with clinical-grade scar care at a consumer-friendly price point. We hope to advance cosmetics towards a future where uncontrolled scarring is no longer an outcome of skin injury.

### GreatBay-SCIE — Expelliodor: Protein-based Targeted Deodorant
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6133) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/greatbay-scie)

Axillary odor is a common concern influenced by genetic variations. People with certain variants of ABCC11 gene express a transporter that carries the odor precursor into the axilla, where Staphylococcus hominis metabolizes this odorless precursor Cys-Gly-3M3SH to release 3M3SH, the major contributor to axillary odor. To reduce precursor availability of uptake without broadly supressing skin microbiota, we developed three complementary solutions: a de novo small-molecule-binding protein to sequester the precursor, a de novo protein-binding protein designed to inhibit conformational changes in the transporter PepTSh, and an enzyme to modify the precursor and reduce its recognition by the transporter. Beyond the engineering, Human Practices led us to recognize that underarm odor is a natural physiological variation, yet often burdened by social stigma. While science can offer ways to manage it, we believe the decision should remain a matter of personal choice, rather than be dictated by social expectations.

### HKUST-GZ — BioWipe: Engineered Bacillus subtilis for Rinse-Free Scalp Care and Hair Conditioning
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6143) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/hkust-gz)

Scalp discomfort makes washing difficult for postoperative, postpartum, traveling, and bedridden users. BioWipe is a waterless finger-sleeve applicator with separate dry and wet components. It uses engineered *Bacillus subtilis* to rebalance the scalp microbiome. Stored dry and activated upon use, it uses an LCFA-responsive sensor to detect excess sebum and trigger Penetratin-mediated antifungal activity, while constitutive K31-PepG supports hair repair. A xylose- and population-density-responsive biosafety switch limits bacterial survival after the product leaves the scalp. User interviews and industry research led us to replace a wipe with a finger sleeve for better access through hair and helped define regulatory boundaries for live products. Dry Lab promoter prediction, protein structural analysis, and population and storage modeling inform the design. GEMR Studio supports collaboration among iGEM teams. We propose phased commercialization through Basic (non-viable), Advanced (postbiotic), and LivePlatform (self-limiting live) products for care between washes.

### HongKong-UCCKE — Phoeniveil
*HKG · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6241) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/hongkong-uccke)

Post-inflammatory hyperpigmentation (PIH) triggered by UV exposure affects over 70% of second-degree burn patients and over 60% report suffering from permanent scars, leaving a lasting mark that can negatively affect their emotional well-being.

Phoeniveil proposes a solution which eliminates the need for expensive and painful treatments by utilizing the UV absorbing property of Mycosporine-like Amino Acids (MAAs) to prevent UV exposure to wounds. We devised a cheaper alternative to extracting and chemically synthesizing MAA, by using synthetic biology to synthesize them using enzymes native in corals. To optimize the yield of MAA, we overexpressed the transketolase-encoding gene in E. coli to increase the availability of sedoheptulose-7-phosphate (S7P), a key precursor of MAA. The biologically synthesized MAA, encapsulated in chitosan nanoparticles prepared by ionic gelation, are then applied to the first layer of the three-layered bandage system of burn wound patients.

### HUST-China — IRIS
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6284) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/hust-china)

Dian Cui, a traditional Chinese art renowned for its brilliant lake blue structural color, has historically relied on kingfisher hunting. Ethical and ecological concerns surrounding feather sourcing call for animal-friendly substitution of this cultural art.

Thus, IRIS was born. We established an integrated biofabrication pipeline transforming fashion waste into optical materials. Structural color was achieved through the self-assembly of cellulose nanocrystals (CNCs). By combining redundance recycling, bacterial cellulose production, and structural-color film fabrication, IRIS explores how waste can become a valuable resource for fashion innovation.

Meanwhile, a controlled-atmosphere spin-coating system was developed to improve fabrication consistency and parameter controls.

So far, IRIS has developed a sustainable alternative to kingfisher-feather-based ornamentation. Beyond reproducing the visual appeal of Dian Cui, IRIS envisions a future in which fashion waste becomes a resource for innovative materials, and traditional craftsmanship inspires more ethical, sustainable, and meaningful design.

### IITDelhi — DermaSense:A living, air pollution-responsive moisturiser
*IND · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6159) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/iitdelhi)

Delhi’s annual PM2.5 has hovered near 100 µg/m³ since 2021, about twenty times the WHO guideline. Particulates and ozone drive reactive oxygen species that harm the skin. Conventional moisturisers use one fixed dose. DermaSense is a living alternative. We are engineering Staphylococcus epidermidis, a skin-commensal, so that a redox-sensitive regulator opens a synthetic operon under oxidative stress. The operon makes pulcherrimin, an antioxidant that also absorbs UV, together with xylolipid and lyso-ornithine lipid, biosurfactants that emulsify and hydrate at the barrier. We established proof of concept for lyso-ornithine lipid biosynthesis in E. coli, paving the way for its potential integration into a skin-associated microbial chassis. Our software integrates live air-quality data across the globe to estimate pollution-induced damage and potential protection offered by these molecules. Our vision is skincare that no longer sits on the skin but lives within it: responsive, self-regulating, and sustained by microbiome itself.

### JLU-NBBMS — TetraxSkin:DNA Tetrahedra-Mediated High-Value Utilization of Ginseng By-products via S.cerevisiae toward Multidimensional Skincare Products
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6073) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/jlu-nbbms)

To address the high costs, low utilization, and resource waste of traditional ginseng-derived cosmetic ingredients, this project utilizes synthetic biology to engineer Saccharomyces cerevisiae as a sustainable platform for producing Compound K (CK)—a highly bioactive ginsenoside with anti-inflammatory, antioxidant, and anti-aging properties. Concurrently, ginseng polysaccharides are enzymatically broken down into low-molecular-weight oligosaccharides to act as efficient moisturizing agents. To optimize skin delivery and stability, CK is loaded onto DNA tetrahedral nanostructures (tFNA). The ultimate goal is to formulate a high-performance facial mask combining tFNA-CK for advanced skin barrier repair and ginseng oligosaccharides for deep hydration. By integrating synthetic biology, nanotechnology, and green biomanufacturing, this project delivers a sustainable, highly efficient approach to producing high-value skincare ingredients, showcasing the potential of advanced biotechnology in next-generation cosmetics.

### MIT-MAHE — MAHEK
*IND · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6145) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/mit-mahe)

Aromatic compounds are used in a diverse range of personal care and household products, yet awareness regarding their unsustainable production is lacking. Cuminaldehyde, responsible for cumin’s warm and spicy signature fragrance, is a high-value compound across perfumery and cosmetic formulations. Conventionally, it is obtained either by extracting essential oils from cumin seeds, generating substantial biomass waste, or by energy-intensive petrochemical synthesis, which contributes to greenhouse gas emissions. MAHEK provides a sustainable, bio-based alternative for synthesising cuminaldehyde using Pseudomonas putida, a bacterium that naturally produces the compound as a metabolic intermediate in its native p-cymene degradation pathway. Through targeted CRISPR-Cas9 gene editing, the cymC gene will be knocked out to arrest the further metabolism of cuminaldehyde, creating an engineered strain optimised to synthesise the compound as the primary end product. MAHEK aims to deliver an industrially relevant fragrance ingredient while curbing greenhouse gas emissions and promoting sustainable manufacturing using synthetic biology.

### NENU-China — CottonStatic
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6458) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/nenu-china)

Static electricity poses fatal fire risks in low-humidity environments like gas stations and operating rooms, yet 83% of people wrongly believe cotton is reliably antistatic. In reality, below 30% humidity, cotton charges like polyester. Current solutions fail: chemical finishes wash out, and conductive fibers sacrifice comfort and breathability.

To address this, NENU-China 2026 developed CottonStatic, a bio-based treatment using a CBM3a-Linker-PaPilA fusion protein. CBM3a anchors to cotton, while PaPilA self-assembles into a humidity-independent conductive nanowire network. Through three DBTL cycles, computational and wet-lab validations optimized the design, achieving reproducible conductivity while preserving cotton's softness and breathability.

By embedding safety into natural fibers, CottonStatic provides a sustainable, durable antistatic solution for skin-contact garments, transforming a widespread misconception into a concrete, market-ready application.

### PROFUTURE-CHINA — Furflouris: Engineering Purine Metabolism for Sustainable Allantoin Production in Pet Skin and Coat Care。
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6406) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/profuture-china)

Growing demand for multifunctional pet grooming products highlights the importance of skin health, coat maintenance, and animal comfort. However, conventional chemical synthesis and plant extraction of functional ingredients face challenges in waste management, raw material variability, and production efficiency.



Furflouris employs synthetic biology to integrate skin care, hair repair, and fragrance into a unified system. Using engineered Escherichia coli BL21, we constructed an allantoin biosynthesis pathway by extending endogenous purine metabolism with XdhABC and PucLM modules. Through promoter optimization, molybdate supplementation, and temperature adjustment, allantoin production reached 68.14 ± 4.06 mg/L. Additionally, we designed a PepG–EGF fusion module to explore hair-fiber and epidermal repair, alongside a vanillin biosynthesis pathway involving QuiC, OMT, CAR, and Sfp for fragrance applications.



By combining microbial biosynthesis with specialized functional modules, Furflouris establishes a bio-based ingredient production framework for integrated pet skin and coat care.

### Qingdao-China — Yeauty: Engineering a Common Yeast for Endotoxin-Free Hyaluronic Acid Production
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6230) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/qingdao-china)

Hyaluronic acid (HA) is widely used in cosmetic and biomedical applications, with different molecular weights exhibiting distinct biological properties. However, conventional bacterial fermentation faces challenges including endotoxin contamination, costly purification, and difficulties in controlling HA molecular weight. Yeauty aims to develop a safer and more efficient HA production platform using the yeast Komagataella pastoris. We engineered K. pastoris to produce HA by introducing a heterologous hyaluronic acid synthase (HAS) and key enzymes involved in precursor biosynthesis. Through Design–Build–Test–Learn cycles, we integrated genome-scale metabolic modeling with experimental validation to identify metabolic bottlenecks and guide pathway optimization. Our modeling highlighted the importance of balancing two precursor-synthesis enzymes, leading us to develop a P2A peptide-based co-expression strategy that increased HA production. By integrating synthetic biology, metabolic engineering, and computational modeling, Yeauty offers a yeast-based strategy for safer, more efficient, and potentially more sustainable HA production while avoiding bacterial endotoxin contamination.

### QingdaoAcademy — UPkeep: Sustainable Cosmetic Production via Upcycling of Ulva prolifera
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6244) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/qingdaoacademy)

Ulva prolifera blooms cause green tides, leaving biomass that can harm coastal environments when allowed to accumulate. This biomass also offers a source of valuable products. UPKeep aims to convert it into ulvan-derived oligosaccharides as potential cosmetic ingredients through a two-stage enzymatic process. First, a designer cellulosome uses a cellulose-binding module and configurable enzyme assembly to promote cooperative cell-wall degradation and release ulvan. An INPNC-displayed ulvan lyase then breaks down ulvan into oligosaccharides. Inspired by consolidated bio-saccharification (CBS), we plan to use biomass hydrolysates to support microbial growth and enzyme production, and recover cellulosomes with residual solids for reuse. Oligosaccharides were selected for potential solubility and bioavailability advantages. Stakeholder input helped shape the design. Ultimately, our project is expected to transform green tide "waste" into a sustainable resource, mitigating the ecological hazards while generating commercial revenues that can offset the financial losses caused by green tides.

### SPC — BioScyde
*HKG · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6104) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/spc)

Chemical sunscreen runoff accelerates coral bleaching, driving an urgent need for reef-safe alternatives. BioScyde addresses this crisis by engineering Escherichia coli as a sustainable production chassis for scytonemin, a cyanobacterial pigment with potent UV-absorbent and antioxidant properties. 



In wet lab, we attempted the synthesis of scytonemin monomer and extensively modified the periplasmic dimerization enzyme ScyE, integrating computational modeling to optimize its translocation, folding, and structural stability. 



Along the way, we built two software tools, the DNA Design Suite to eliminate greedy codon repeats and a protocol safety module.  We also made custom hardware for downstream pigment extraction. 



Integrated Human Practices informed our engineering through stakeholder and sustainability analyses to satisfy environmental standards. These outcomes were taken into account in an entrepreneurial strategy evaluating technoeconomic feasibility, accompanied by educational initiatives promoting synthetic biology and marine conservation.

### Squirrel-Shanghai — Sustainable biosynthesis of cis-abienol as an alternative for ambergris.
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6126) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/squirrel-shanghai)

Cis - abienol is a crucial precursor for ambrox, a key ingredient in fragrances and pharmaceuticals. As traditional ambergris from sperm whales is scarce and raises conservation issues, developing sustainable synthesis routes is vital. Currently, cis - abienol is obtained via plant extraction or chemical synthesis, which are complex, low - yielding, and environmentally burdensome. In this study, Escherichia coli was chosen as the host. By optimizing the isopentenol utilization pathway (IUP), an efficient and cost - effective microbial production system for cis - abienol was built.

### SSES — Temperature-Controlled Smart Odor-Degradation System Based on Engineered Microorganisms
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6414) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/sses)

Odor pollution is prevalent in daily life. Foot odor, pet waste, and garbage piles release volatile short-chain fatty acids, with isovaleric acid as the core sour and rancid odor substance—even trace amounts cause strong unpleasant smells. We constructed basic and composite genetic parts in Escherichia coli and built recombinant plasmids. Complete isovaleric acid degradation requires four key proteins—FadL, FadD, AidB, and MccAB—since partial expression may cause insufficient substrate activation or toxic intermediate accumulation; we thus designed a degradation module with three plasmids requiring co-expression of all four genes. To prevent ecological risks from strain escape, we introduced the pTRIP temperature-dependent quorum-sensing dual-regulation system and a lethal module (three plasmids). The suicide pathway stays off at 37 °C fermentation but triggers lysis and lethality via AHL signaling at low temperatures. This project assembles a degradation pathway and temperature-controlled suicide module into E. coli, creating safe, efficient odor-removing engineered bacteria.

### TecCEM — MONARCH: A SynBio approach to the future of skincare
*MEX · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6358) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/teccem)

Skin inflammation, whether driven by chronic conditions or by daily stressors, affects both physical health and psychological well-being, and in Mexico, high solar exposure and urban stress intensify this burden. MONARCH by iGEM Tec CEM, is a serum based on multifunctional peptides designed to reduce inflammation at the molecular level while addressing the barriers that limit peptides in skincare: stability, targeted delivery, and skin penetration. The multipeptides were designed and studied in silico, whilst the serum formulation was capable of maintaining BSA stability as a preliminary test of its peptide-preservation capabilities



The team proposes the GRAS organism Lactococcus lactis as a production chassis, since its safety status and secretion capacity simplify purification; however, its lactic acid bacteria metabolism poses specific challenges, which the team addressed by building a metabolic model of its network. Throughout the project, interviews with multidisciplinary stakeholders guided its development and shaped our initial business proposal.

### Tsinghua-T — Glabridin biosynthesis via AI-designed LLPS Architecture with Temporospatial-controlled Enzyme Assembly
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6308) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/tsinghua-t)

Glabridin is a high-value plant-derived skincare ingredient, yet its microbial biosynthesis suffers from severe bottlenecks: premature off-pathway demethylation diverts intermediates and lowers yield. Instead of altering intrinsic enzyme catalytic properties, GALATEA tackles this issue by spatially reorganizing two key enzymes, OC and DMT, inside an engineered liquid-liquid phase-separated (LLPS) condensate. This design enforces ordered reactions: OC catalyzes ring-closure first, followed by terminal demethylation by DMT, suppressing intermediate leakage.

Our dry-lab pipeline combines structural compatibility, phase-separation characteristics and pathway kinetics to guide wet-lab iteration, generalized into a software platform with an adaptive AI agent and a literature retrieval algorithm. In wet-lab experiments, we truncated OC's ER anchor, equipped enzymes with recruitment tags, and constructed the FUSN-SH3-PDZ-FTH1 scaffold as reusable composite parts, testing both cytosolic and ER-anchored LLPS architectures. We established an evidence-driven evaluation framework aligned with SDG 3, 9 and 12, covering safety profiling and resource-balance modeling, enabling sustainable glabridin biomanufacturing.

### Tsinglan — you NAILed it
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6190) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/tsinglan)

The global nail art market is projected to reach 4.034 billion USD by 2034. Traditional nail products cause health risks and plastic pollution. Our team developed a synthetic biology system with three modules: biodegradable artificial nail tips, biological adhesive, and nail polishes fabricated by fluorescent protein and chromoprotein.  We mutated gfasPurple chromoprotein, expressed it in pET-28α, and verified its good thermostability. We also screened more proteins for diverse colours. Our nail patches use hydroxyapatite-reinforced alginate crosslinked by calcium chloride. The bio-glue combines mussel foot proteins and spider silk proteins.  This fully biodegradable alternative protects users and reduces environmental burdens, proving synthetic biology enables safe, high-performance sustainable cosmetics.

### TU Darmstadt — UNRAVEL- Unlocking New Resources And Value from End-of-Life textiles
*DEU · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6356) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/tu-darmstadt)

The fashion industry generates 6,900,000 tons of waste every year in the EU alone. Blended fabrics remain especially difficult to recycle using conventional methods, which is why our team aims to UNRAVEL the potential of thermophilic bacteria for the degradation of mixed textile waste. We have investigated five different enzymes for degrading PET and nylon and attempted to make them more thermostable through engineering to reach higher conversion rates. Integrating these into an organism that can already degrade cotton would enable the degradation of mixed textiles without the need to laboriously separate the various materials found in common textiles. This led us on a journey discovering how to handle thermophilic, anaerobic organisms as potential chassis, building our own equipment and modelling biological systems. With our research we work towards turning the waste of today into the resource of tomorrow.

### Usherbrooke — Bioproduction of poly glutamic acid
*CAN · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6262) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/usherbrooke)

Our 2026 project develops an alternative to hyaluronic acid, widely used in cosmetics and biomedicine: the microbial production of poly-γ-glutamic acid (γ-PGA), a biopolymer with excellent moisturizing properties, biocompatibility, and biodegradability. Produced by several Bacillus species, γ-PGA is highly versatile thanks to its wide polydispersity.



Despite a growing market, industrial production remains concentrated in Asia, raising concerns about availability, reliability, and environmental impact for North American manufacturers that depend on imports. iGEM UdeS aims to develop a sustainable, local production process for Canada.



We selected Bacillus subtilis var. natto for its native γ-PGA biosynthesis, its safety profile, and its accessibility. Our strategy targets a single gene in the γ-PGA pathway, either overexpressing a promoter of production or knocking out a limiting one, to outproduce the wild type.

### Westlake — AI-Powered Personalized Fragrance via Synthetic Biology
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6498) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/westlake)

The traditional fragrance industry relies on environmentally damaging plant and animal extraction methods or polluting chemical synthesis, while consumers constantly face a frustrating trial-and-error process to find their perfect scent. To solve this, we are developing a closed-loop, personalized fragrance platform that combines artificial intelligence with synthetic biology. First, our AI algorithm analyzes a user's specific preferences to design a unique, digitized scent recipe. Next, we introduce plant and bacteria genes into engineered yeast to build our "green factories", regulate MVA pathways and produce terpenoid fragrant molecules, which is a more sustainable and environment-friendly strategy. Finally, our custom-built automated hardware precisely blends these bio-manufactured ingredients, delivering a fully customized, eco-friendly perfume directly to the consumer.

### Worldshaper-HZ — BlackNova: A Safe and Sustainable Polydopamine Bio-Hair Dye
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6496) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/worldshaper-hz)

Hair graying often causes appearance anxiety, yet conventional chemical dyes rely on harsh ingredients like p-phenylenediamine that can cause severe scalp allergies. To solve this, we created a safe, eco-friendly hair dye kit based on polydopamine, a natural dark pigment with excellent body compatibility. Using engineered harmless bacteria as green factories, we biologically produce dopamine from simple amino acids under mild conditions. We also designed a special bio-peptide that acts like glue, helping the natural pigment stick firmly to hair proteins for long-lasting color. Packaged as a practical, easy-to-use kit, our product replaces toxic chemical treatments with a gentle bio-cleansing routine, offering a healthy and commercial solution to graying hair.

### ZQT-Nanjing — EctoBloom: Green Biomanufacturing of Ectoine for Sensitive Skin Repair and Facial Mask Application
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6236) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/zqt-nanjing)

Our project focuses on the sustainable biomanufacturing of an Ectoine–Bacterial Cellulose (BC) facial mask, integrating synthetic biology with bio-based materials for greener skincare solutions.



Ectoine is a natural compound with moisturizing and cell-protective properties. However, conventional production relies on high-salinity fermentation, causing equipment corrosion and wastewater challenges. To address these issues, we engineered Escherichia coli with the EctABC biosynthetic pathway, optimized carbon and nitrogen sources, and introduced the LysC T352I mutation to enhance ectoine production. We also explored wheat straw hydrolysate as a renewable fermentation substrate.



For product development, we investigated bacterial cellulose as a biodegradable facial mask substrate, combining it with silk fibroin and purified ectoine to improve moisturizing performance.



By integrating metabolic engineering, renewable resources, and material design, we aim to develop sustainable skincare products.


## Food & Nutrition (33)

### Aalto-Helsinki — DAHLIA: Making Food Safety Visible
*FIN · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6201) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/aalto-helsinki)

Is your food safe to eat? Earlier this year, Finnish broadcasters exposed issues with the storage of meat and fish in supermarkets. The Aalto-Helsinki iGEM team presents DAHLIA, a test strip that detects the histamine emitted by foods like meat and fish as they spoil. In our biosensor, Diamine Oxidase (DAO) reacts with histamine, producing H2O2, and Horseradish Peroxidase (HRP) reacts with H2O2 and changes the color of a substrate. DAO and HRP are each joined to a cellulose-binding module (CBM) to adhere to the cellulose-based test strip. Our team successfully designed, transformed and expressed both fusion proteins and developed assays for prototype testing. To ensure the positive impact of the product on our community, we interacted with consumers, food vendors, and experts from different fields. We especially strived to educate the community about food safety and histamine - for a future with less food waste and more food safety.

### Anatolia College-GR — Engineering Chlorella Vulgaris to Enhance Non-Heme Iron Bioavailability
*GRC · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6210) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/anatolia-college-gr)

Iron deficiency continues to be one of the most prevalent nutritional concerns worldwide, particularly impacting populations that depend on plant-based diets abundant in non-heme iron, which has low bioavailability. Our initiative, Engineering Chlorella vulgaris to Enhance Non-Heme Iron Bioavailability, tackles this issue through the application of synthetic biology by genetically modifying the microalgae Chlorella Vulgaris to overexpress the VTC2/GGP gene, a crucial enzyme in the L-ascorbic acid biosynthesis pathway. By significantly increasing the production of vitamin C within the organism, we seek to naturally improve iron absorption in the same context. Transgenic strains will be created using electroporation, and their effectiveness will be assessed through in-vitro digestion models. Ultimately, our aim is to develop an affordable and sustainable algae-based capsule supplement, bridging the divide between metabolic engineering and global nutritional security.

### BOKU-Vienna — Algaeneering
*AUT · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6090) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/boku-vienna)

Micronutrient deficiencies affect billions worldwide. Our project aimed to establish a proof of concept for vitamin overproduction in microalgae by engineering Chlamydomonas reinhardtii to increase vitamin C and folate production through overexpression of GGP, GTPCHI, and ADCS.

Through expert and stakeholder interviews, we questioned our approach, its potential benefits, and the challenges of genetically modified foods. They also highlighted that technology can only deliver real impact when paired with trust and public acceptance. This strengthened our focus on education and public engagement.

Computational models complemented our laboratory work by exploring biological processes relevant to vitamin production. Experimentally, we confirmed reporter expression and obtained promising preliminary results for transformation with our GGP construct, while successful transformation of the folate constructs remains unconfirmed.

Although further research is needed, our project provides a starting point for developing vitamin-enriched microalgae and demonstrates the importance of combining experimental research, computational modeling, and public engagement.

### Bonn — PotyGuard⁺ - A bodyguard for your vegetable yard
*DEU · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6455) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/bonn)

PotyGuard⁺ is a modular, protease-responsive biosensor designed to detect viral infection in plant cells and convert viral protease activity into a programmable cellular response. The system combines split proteins, Npu DnaE inteins, and inhibitory peptide cages that prevent spontaneous reconstitution. Protease-mediated cage removal enables the split components to reassemble and restore protein function.



We demonstrated the functionality of this architecture using split eGFP as a fluorescent proof of concept in Nicotiana benthamiana. The same principle can be transferred to functional effectors, such as split Cre recombinase, linking protease activity to downstream cellular responses.



By separating sensing from output, PotyGuard⁺ can be adapted to different proteases and effectors. This enables applications ranging from fluorescence and recombinase activity to transcriptional or signaling responses. Such outputs could conditionally constitute a defense against viral replication or restrict viral spread in plants, while the underlying architecture provides a broadly applicable platform for protease-responsive synthetic biology.

### Cataverse-Beijing — A Portable Rapid Bacterial Detection System Mediated by Cerium-Based MOF Nanozymes
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6180) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/cataverse-beijing)

Foodborne pathogens can be transmitted through contaminated media such as vegetables, causing a range of illnesses from mild diarrhea, abdominal pain, and vomiting to severe fever and even hemolytic uremic syndrome. These pathogens pose a persistent threat to public health security. Therefore, the development of rapid and sensitive detection methods for foodborne pathogens is of great practical significance for early warning and effective prevention of foodborne diseases. To address this need, we have developed a novel rapid bacterial detection platform based on magnetic bead–phage receptor-binding protein coupling technology, a cerium-based MOF nanozyme (Ce-UiO-66) with alkaline phosphatase-like activity, and a smartphone application. This platform provides promising technical support for food safety monitoring, primary-level public health protection, and on-site rapid diagnosis.

### Cataverse-Shanghai — Reshaping the Future of Food: Unlocking the Nutritional Secrets of Novel Plant Proteins
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6134) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/cataverse-shanghai)

With the growth of the global population, rising health awareness and increasing demand for sustainable development, plant proteins have become a key development direction in the food industry due to their advantages of abundant resources, environmental friendliness and balanced nutrition. In the field of plant based meat products, plant proteins can mimic the texture, mouthfeel and flavor of animal meat through protein modification, compounding technologies and processing process optimization, meeting consumers’ demands for health, environmental protection and diversification. This project selects potato glycoprotein Patatin, hemp seed protein Edestin 1 and cassava 11S globulin as core raw materials for improving the mouthfeel of plant based meat. Heterologous expression is performed using the Pichia pastoris platform, aiming to provide safe and controllable high quality protein raw materials and replicable synthetic biology solutions for sustainable food systems.

### CAU-China — Fungal Sentinel
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6426) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/cau-china)

Globally, approximately 3% of grain is lost to mold annually, with China losing 42 billion kg in storage. Since visual inspection and laboratory tests typically detect contamination only after fungal growth has occurred, CAU-China 2026 introduces Fungal Sentinel, a yeast-based platform designed to sense fungal volatile organic compounds (VOCs) —specifically 1-octen-3-ol, a key biomarker of mold—before visible contamination appears.

Our sensor rewires the yeast mating pathway: an olfactory receptor (OR) binds the VOC, a Gpa1–Gαolf chimera relays the signal, and the FUS1 promoter drives ymStayGold, converting ligand binding into a measurable green fluorescence output.

For readout, OptiX, a phone-based fluorimeter costing about $15 in materials, resolves the reporter spectrum from RAW images down to nanomolar levels. Together, the living sensor and the pocket reader transform invisible, early-stage contamination into a measurable signal long before mold becomes visible.

### Chalmers-Gothenburg — CHYMERA: Cell-Based Hybrid Yeast system for Multi-analyte Engineered Receptor Assays
*SWE · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6471) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/chalmers-gothenburg)

Biogenic amines such as histamine and tyramine accumulate during microbial food spoilage and represent important indicators for example scombroid poisoning. The aim of this project was to create a yeast-based biosensor, detecting histamine and tyramine, in order to facilitate the detection of spoiled fish prior to consumption. The human GPCRs, H3HR and TAAR1 as well as the honeybee (Apis mellifera) orthologues amTAR1 and amTAR2 were cloned using MoClo assembly and expressed in S. cerevisiae. The receptor genes were introduced into Ste2/Ste3 (-mating factor receptor) knockout strains. The strains contained a modified MAPK cascade together with Gαi3/Gpa1 chimeric G protein α -subunit and Ste12LexA chimeric transcription factor with a GFP reporter gene coupled to the LexO6xLEU2 promoter. Membrane localization of BFP-fused receptors was studied using fluorescence microscopy indicating secondary vacuolar and ER localization. Functional analysis of ligand interaction indicated that the biosensor was non-functional under the tested conditions.

### China SUIS GB — Fish Spoilage Biosensor
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6411) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/china-suis-gb)

Seafood spoilage generates significant global food waste, primarily driven by fish degrading into volatile organic compounds like trimethylamine (TMA). To combat this, we developed a biosensor engineered for cold-chain environments (0–4°C) that detects gaseous TMA.

Our system utilizes a two-strain Shewanella oneidensis MR-1 co-culture. Strain 1 contains a cold-inducible promoter PcspA, expressing periplasmic TMM, converting incoming gaseous TMA to TMAO. Strain 2 senses extracellular TMAO through its native periplasmic TorT/TorS machinery, initiating a phosphorylation cascade that activates the Ptor promoter to express the blue chromoprotein AmilCP.

Across two engineering cycles, we conducted double digestion (PstI/SacI) and ligation molar ratios (5:1 insert-to-vector) using plasmids pDSK519 and pBBR1MCS-1. PCR cross-validation confirmed successful strain construction and plasmid maintenance under selective antibiotics. Finally, we integrated this co-culture into a dual-layer product featuring an outer gas-permeable cellulose acetate shell and inner porous agarose microbeads for bacterial immobilization.

### DEPU — Degradation of Acrylamide in High-Temperature-Processed Foods Using Immobilized Dual-Enzyme Systems
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6451) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/depu)

Acrylamide, a probable human carcinogen, is widely produced in thermally processed foods via the Maillard reaction between asparagine and reducing sugars. Natural enzymes suffer from low thermostability and fail to work efficiently under high-temperature food processing conditions. To solve this and the limitations of single-enzyme approaches and high costs, we build an immobilized dual-enzyme system of L-asparaginase and glucose oxidase. LEA peptides are applied to greatly boost enzyme thermostability, and food-grade crosslinkers enable co-immobilization to remove two key precursors. This eco-friendly, recyclable solution effectively reduces acrylamide in fried and baked foods and improves public food safety.

### HKSSC — Waste-to-watt: Engineering PrbA for Enhanced Food Waste Bioenergy Production
*HKG · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6053) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/hkssc)

While industrial anaerobic digestion converts tonnes of global food waste into renewable bioenergy, a critical hidden bottleneck lowers efficiency: synthetic preservatives like parabens accumulate in waste streams, paralyze functional microbes. So we, HKSSC introduces Waste-to-Watt, a targeted solution designed to overcome this industrial crisis. By engineering E. coli to express the paraben-degrading enzyme prbA, our system neutralizes chemical inhibitors to accelerate microbial digestion. Grounded in rigorous engineering design, modeling, and wet-lab validation, Waste-to-Watt bridges molecular innovation with municipal green infrastructure, maximizing renewable energy yields and establishing scalable blueprint for sustainable global waste valorization. We have charactereized 16 new parts, including 14 new basic parts and 2 new composite parts. Furthermore, through our structured iCycle Integrated Human Practices framework and extensive community outreach in all age groups, including school workshops, interactive games, and public surveys, we engaged diverse stakeholders to guide project optimization while inspiring the next generation of synthetic biologists.

### Hongwen School — BrewXOS: A Solution for Transforming Brewer's Spent Grain into High-Value Prebiotics
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6174) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/hongwen-school)

Brewing generates approximately 38 million tonnes of brewer’s spent grain (BSG) annually, largely used as low-value animal feed. Converting its fibre into potential prebiotic ingredients could create greater economic value through functional food applications. However, complex fibre structures and separate enzyme-production steps complicate conversion.

BrewXOS engineers Bacillus subtilis with three complementary enzymes to combine enzyme production and BSG conversion in one fermentation process, without adding purified enzymes. After 12 hours, our best strain achieved a control-adjusted sugar yield of 107.9 mg xylose equivalents per gram of dry BSG, with further evidence of oligosaccharide-like products. We also built a compact fermentation-monitoring prototype and incorporated stakeholder feedback into ingredient development.

Future work will identify and recover the products, validate safety and prebiotic function, and evaluate food compatibility and production economics—advancing spent grain toward ingredients with nutritional and economic potential.

### Jilin-China — NutriTrace: A Living DNA Recorder for Fermentation
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6456) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/jilin-china)

Fermented products including beer, wine, soy sauce, cheese and many yeast-derived ingredients are manufactured in large stirred tanks. Poor mixing and uneven mass transfer form gradients of nutrients, oxygen and metabolites, generating local microenvironmental stresses that reduce yield. Conventional detection averages tank conditions into one value and fails to capture these stresses. We constructed a living DNA recorder in Komagataella phaffii. Cells permanently record their experienced stresses into DNA, and this stress history can be read out by fluorescence after fermentation. Each recording channel combines a stress-responsive promoter and a site-specific recombinase; the target stress is defined by the promoter, making the recorder adaptable to different industrial processes. Encapsulated in calcium-cross-linked alginate beads, engineered cells are recoverable for post-run readout. Unlike real-time probes showing instant fermenter status, our recorder records each cell’s past exposure, providing engineers with information for process adjustment.

### JZIB-QDHS — A New Herbal Approach to Weight Loss: Functional Foods Targeting SULT Enzymes
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6198) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/jzib-qdhs)

This project targets human sulfotransferases (SULT2B1, SULT1E1, SULT2A1) involved in metabolic regulation. Using a fluorescence-based high-throughput screening platform, natural compounds are screened via molecular docking and validated for SULT inhibition. Synergistic inhibitor combinations are identified using Chou-Talalay analysis. A dual-enzyme biosynthesis system (UGT88D4 + UGD) is engineered in E. coli to produce bioactive flavonoid glycosides. The final formulation is tested in 3T3-L1 adipocyte differentiation models for reduced lipid accumulation and adipogenic gene expression. This work provides a functional food strategy for weight management.

### KCIS-Xiugang-Taipei — Herculyeast
*TWN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6103) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/kcis-xiugang-taipei)

Taiwan is ending food waste pig feeding due to African Swine Fever policies, with a final transition deadline in December, 2026, leaving stored waste that can clog plumbing, produce odors, and contribute to climate change through methane release. To address this, we engineered Saccharomyces cerevisiae to secrete two digestive enzymes: Proteinase A (PEP4) for protein degradation and sterol esterase (TGL1) for lipid degradation. Fusion to the MFα1 prepro-leader sequence is intended to redirect both enzymes into the culture medium, under inducible and constitutive promoters. Enzymes collected from the medium are intended to power two products: a two-chamber reactor that sequentially degrades lipids and proteins and an enzyme-based drain unblocker that targets grease buildup. Because both products use enzyme preparations rather than live engineered yeast, they are intended to treat food waste at its source as pretreatment before conventional treatment. We hope this pretreatment reduces storage needs and eases environmental impacts.

### KhanLab-BayArea — The Cluck is Ticking
*USA · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6080) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/khanlab-bayarea)

Globally, meat waste accounts for approximately 100 billion USD of lost value annually. A major driver of this waste is the reliance on static expiration dates, which disregard real-world variability during storage and handling. Consequently, meat is often disposed of prematurely at every level of the supply chain. More importantly, spoiled meat is consumed unintentionally posing serious individual and public health risks. 



To address this issue, we are engineering an E. coli based biosensor to detect cadaverine, a metabolic byproduct of bacteria that accumulates in meat over time. Our biosensor will produce more chromoprotein in response to increased concentrations of cadaverine. In parallel, we will develop a mathematical model to predict live-response meat expiration dates, allowing consumers, producers, and sellers to make clear choices about food conservation and consumption.

### Kyoto — OiLipase
*JPN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6399) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/kyoto)

Rice bran rapidly degrades after milling due to lipase-driven free fatty acid (FFA) production. We developed OiLipase, a two-part system to combat this: KANNAME, a spatiotemporally controlled protease system that selectively degrades the lipase at the oil-water interface and self-inactivates via a timed split-protease mechanism; and NIINAME, a cell-free FFA biosensor adapted from the ROSALIND platform. Together, they enable heat-free control and monitoring of rice bran rancidity.

### Latvia-Riga — TRACE – Trichinella Rapid Assay for Capture-to-Consumption Evaluation.
*LVA · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6431) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/latvia-riga)

Trichinellosis persists as a zoonotic barrier at the intersection of wildlife ecology and human food safety. The standard regulatory method, artificial pepsin digestion, is tethered to centralized laboratories, requiring specialized equipment and extensive processing. This dependency creates a diagnostic void for remote populations and hunters consuming wild game without veterinary oversight. Project TRACE bridges this gap with a decentralized, field-ready screening platform. Its architecture leverages Bacillus subtilis spore display, fusing passenger proteins and biotin acceptor-peptides to native CotB, CotC and CotG coat proteins, with sfGFP as a size placeholder for VHH-nanobodies against the T. spiralis serine protease. This yields a durable, shelf-stable bioreagent, while an Fe-EDTA-ethanol protocol renders spores non-viable before release. Bypassing hazardous acids, sample preparation uses a detergent-buffer and mechanical maceration to isolate antigens into a liquid exudate. Detection is via lateral flow assay, visualizing spore-antigen complexes with streptavidin-coated carbon nanoparticles.

### NPSI Singapore — ProbioRegulon
*SGP · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6094) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/npsi-singapore)

As diabetes becomes one of the world’s largest health challenges, interest has grown in food-based approaches to managing blood glucose. Bitter gourd (Momordica charantia) has long been used for this purpose, and contains mcIRBP-19, a peptide reported to act at the insulin receptor. But the amount present in the plant varies with variety, ripeness and preparation, and the natural sequence is under patent. In our project, we redesigned the peptide and had the coding sequences synthesised, then verified the resulting constructs in bacteria, working toward secretion from Lactobacillus paracasei, a food-grade probiotic. Delivering the peptide from bacteria living in the gut avoids the main obstacle facing peptides taken by mouth, which are largely broken down before they can act. Computational modelling guided every design decision. In the future, Mordilac could become a palatable probiotic drink, offering a familiar food format for people managing or at risk of type 2 diabetes.

### NTHU — AeroSense: A Modular Biohybrid Olfactory Platform for Agricultural Screening
*TWN · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6460) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/nthu)

Fungal deterioration causes substantial crop losses, highlighting the need for accessible tools for early, on-site detection. Our project, AeroSense, is a modular biohybrid olfactory platform designed for agricultural screening, with banana production as its intended first application. The platform brings together a cell-based sensing array expressing Drosophila odorant receptors and ORCO in HEK293 cells, GCaMP6f fluorescence reporting, a four-channel optical reader, and a fly-inspired neural network for odor-pattern decoding. We documented reporter expression and conducted exploratory measurements of fluorescence kinetics. We built and tested the optical reader using fluorescent standards, with transport and optical modeling informing its design. Computational studies evaluated odor-pattern decoding on external sensor datasets. Discussions with agricultural researchers and potential users shaped the proposed screening workflow and a shared-access deployment strategy. With reusable analysis code, hardware documentation, and educational resources, this work lays the groundwork for an accessible screening tool to guide follow-up inspection and confirmatory testing.

### NYCU-Taiwan — BITE: Biosensing for Instant Target Pathogen Evaluation
*TWN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6306) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/nycu-taiwan)

Foodborne pathogens threaten public health, yet conventional detection often requires specialized equipment, trained personnel, and time-consuming procedures. To make preliminary food safety screening more accessible, we developed BITE (Biosensing for Instant Target Pathogen Evaluation), a synthetic biology-based platform designed for intuitive visual detection.



Using Salmonella invA DNA as a proof-of-concept target, BITE combines magnetic bead-based DNA enrichment, CRISPR-Cas12a molecular recognition, and G-quadruplex–hemin (G4–hemin) DNAzyme colorimetric readout. Magnetic beads capture and concentrate target DNA, Cas12a provides sequence-specific recognition, and the G4–hemin system generates a visible blue signal through TMB oxidation.



We investigated these modules experimentally and used mathematical modelling to study detection processes. Industry consultations provided technical and product-development perspectives, while educational outreach promoted synthetic biology. By integrating molecular recognition with a simple visual readout, BITE explores a portable approach to preliminary foodborne pathogen screening.

### Okinawa — pHlex
*JPN · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6155) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/okinawa)

Antimicrobial resistance (AMR) is a critical problem in human and veterinary medicine, driven largely by antibiotic overuse. We focused on two bacteria for which resistance is becoming serious. Brachyspira hyodysenteriae, the agent of swine dysentery, is endemic in Okinawa, Japan, where pigs are often given multiple antibiotics over long periods, disrupting the gut microbiota and weakening immunity; simple antibiotic-free control measures are therefore needed on farms. Helicobacter pylori infects 4.4 billion people and causes gastric cancer, with high-risk strains common in Japan, China, and South Korea, and resistant strains are emerging.



We aim to develop phage therapy against these gastrointestinal pathogens. Because phages are inactivated by gastric acid after oral administration, we constructed an original AI platform to predict genetic sequences conferring acid resistance. Guided by these predictions, acid-resistant phage strains will be engineered into new preparations effective against B. hyodysenteriae and H. pylori.

### OUC-China — Pro-TPSP : Engineering a Programmable and Tunable Platform for Tailored Pullulan
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6179) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/ouc-china)

Pullulan is a biodegradable microbial polysaccharide with potential applications in edible films, food packaging, nutritional products, and animal-free capsule shells. These applications require different film-forming properties and viscosities, both closely related to molecular weight.

Pro-TPSP aims to develop a programmable platform for tailoring pullulan properties in Aureobasidium melanogenum P16. We target AmyR, a shared regulator of three hydrolase genes, through two complementary approaches: a Tet-On system enabling externally induced transcriptional control, and uORF-mediated translational regulation guided by sequence-based models and evaluated through two rounds of GFP reporter assays. These approaches explore control over pullulan molecular weight and viscosity. Preliminary results showed that combining the selected A1444 uORF with amyR increased pullulan molecular weight relative to parental P16. By connecting expression regulation with material requirements, our project offers a strategy for developing customized biomaterials for future food and nutrition applications.

### RooseveltSATX — Amisense
*USA · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6051) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/rooseveltsatx)

One of the challenges in preventing food-related illnesses from fish stems from their rapid decomposition—which can occur before there are any sensory cues such as unpleasant odors and visual changes. Microbial degradation and autolysis starts on the molecular level with the production of compounds through chemical reactions—such as the bioamine 'histamine'. Our team seeks to address this global food safety issue by creating an accessible and authentic product that detects contamination before reaching the consumer. By engineering DNA, our team has created a color changing biosensor that detects rising histamine levels before any physical manifestations show.

### SCU-China — DunaTerp:  A Programmable Carotenoid Metabolic Platform
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6212) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/scu-china)

Terpenoids supply most natural food pigments and flavors, yet plant extraction suffers from low yield and high cost, while synthetic alternatives raise safety concerns. We developed DunaTerp, a food-grade terpenoid platform using Dunaliella salina — the only industrially cultivated microalga for natural β-carotene. D. salina already possesses a complete endogenous MEP pathway and efficiently expresses LCYB, natively accumulating β-carotene up to 14% of dry weight and bypassing costly precursor engineering.

We introduce CPP transcription factors that simultaneously activate β-carotene synthesis and suppress competing branches, redirecting flux toward a shared β-carotene hub. Building on this hub, modular pipelines produce astaxanthin (BKT), β-ionone (CCD1), crocetin (GjCCD4a/GjALDH2C3), and β-citraurin (CitCCD4). For biocontainment, a salt-dependent mazE-mazF switch triggers cell death upon escape into low-salt environments. Interchangeable enzyme cassettes allow flexible adaptation to diverse terpenoid products, making DunaTerp a generalizable chassis for sustainable terpenoid supply.

### SHSBNU-China — BLISS: Bioengineered Low-calorie Innovative Sweetener Synthesis
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6387) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/shsbnu-china)

Current sugar substitutes often compromise taste and deprive people joy of eating. The sweet protein Brazzein, from Pentadiplandra brazzeana, combines a clean, sugar-like taste with promising stability during processing. However, difficulties with protein folding and purification in recombinant microbial expressions limit its production for broader commercial use. BLISS addresses these challenges through a novel active inclusion body strategy in Escherichia coli. Using the SAP peptide (L6KD) and partner adapter tags (Sumo and MtuI), we demonstrated improved Brazzein production and simplified its recovery. With AI-assisted protein engineering, we identified variants with potentially better stability and sweetness. We also evaluated expression in yeast for scalable, food-grade production. Through iterative engineering, we develop PrintScope, a figure-sized miniature microscope, to promote accessible biology education. Ultimately, BLISS seeks to create a sweet future without the associated health burden.

### SSBS — Enabling “Slow Absorption” of Starch: Reconstructing Low-Glycemic Index (GI) Resistant Starch via a Multi-Enzyme System
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6192) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/ssbs)

With the widespread prevalence of metabolic diseases such as type 2 diabetes and obesity worldwide, their rapid digestion and high glycemic index (GI)—have become increasingly prominent. Resistant starch (RS), as a food ingredient that combines the benefits of a low GI with dietary fiber, has emerged as a key focus in healthy dietary interventions. Based on the principles of synthetic biology and multi-enzyme synergistic catalysis, this study established a three-enzyme synergistic modification system comprising hydrolysis, chain elongation, and debranching (α-amylase, 4-α-glucan transferase, and pullulanase). to specifically reconfigure the molecular structure and crystalline properties of starch. The modification effects were evaluated through experiments such as SEM analysis and resistant starch (RS) content determination. Ultimately, a low-GI resistant starch preparation process suitable for daily staple foods such as noodles and bread was developed, providing a feasible dietary intervention strategy for patients with metabolic diseases and individuals seeking weight management.

### Tec-Chihuahua — BruSafe: mRNA platform for bovine brucellosis prevention
*MEX · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6344) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/tec-chihuahua)

Bovine brucellosis, caused mainly by Brucella abortus, remains one of the most widespread neglected zoonoses, endemic in Mexico, where 57.4% of national territory is still in the control phase. Current vaccines rely on live attenuated strains: S19 interferes with serological diagnosis, while RB51 is abortifacient, shed in milk, virulent and rifampicin-resistant in humans, and less protective than claimed. We present BruSafe, a non-replicating vaccine platform in which an mRNA encoding a multiepitope antigen derived from conserved Brucella outer membrane proteins (OMP10, OMP25, OMP25b) is packaged into a 27 nm self-assembling MS2 bacteriophage virus-like particle produced in Escherichia coli BL21(DE3). The particle contains no live organism, no smooth lipopolysaccharide and no antibiotic-resistance marker, making it DIVA-compatible, safe in pregnant cattle and cold-chain tolerant. Subcutaneously delivered, it targets dendritic cells to elicit the CD4⁺ Th1/IFN-γ response required for protection, and its modular cassette is retargetable to other pathogens.

### Texas — Engineering Probiotics to Combat Malnutrition
*USA · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6231) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/texas)

Malnutrition affects millions worldwide, and a subset of children fail to respond to microbiota-directed complementary foods (MDCFs). Recent work links this to absence of Faecalibacterium prausnitzii, whose FAAH enzyme hydrolyzes oleoylethanolamide (OEA), a lipid messenger signaling satiety and suppressing food intake. We engineered Escherichia coli Nissle 1917 to express a transmembrane-domain-deleted FAAH fused to YebF. Unexpectedly, YebF localized it to the bacterial membrane instead of secreting, reconstituting FAAH to it’s native compartment. We confirmed membrane localization and demonstrated OEA hydrolysis by the engineered strain. In parallel, MICOM community metabolic modeling reproduced the gut’s known cross-feeding network from genome content alone and found OEA absent from shared bacterial metabolism, consistent with a host-derived signal. Integrated human practices with researchers, clinicians, NGOs and community leaders, plus webinars, $3 meal plans and a gated deployment route, grounded the work. This offers a route to relieving premature satiation, an under-addressed barrier to nutritional recovery.

### Thailand-RIS — BetterSweet: Reimagining Sweeteners through Mogroside Pathway Reconstruction
*THA · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6099) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/thailand-ris)

Novel low-calorie and high-intensity natural sweeteners are needed to address the global overconsumption of sugar that leads to severe health consequences. Monk fruit, or Siraitia grosvenorii, belongs to the cucumber family (Cucurbitaceae), which is native to southern China. It has a special metabolic pathway that produces Mogrosides, a group of triterpene glycosides. The final product of this pathway is Mogroside V, a compound 200-400 times sweeter than table sugar with no calories. However, the production of Monk Fruit remains limited by slow growth and restricted cultivation. Our project aims to reconstruct key steps of the Mogroside biosynthetic pathway in Nicotiana benthamiana, a fast-growing and easily engineered plant host. By establishing an alternative plant-based production system, we hope to make natural sweeteners more accessible, scalable, and environmentally sustainable.

### UFRGS-Brazil — 2,4-Detector: development of a fluorescent biosensor for detecting 2,4-D herbicide in agricultural systems
*BRA · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6130) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/ufrgs-brazil)

The widespread use of 2,4-D in soybean farms causes herbicide drift, threatening neighboring crops. In southern Brazil’s Campanha Gaúcha, this drift devastates viticulture. Containing the issue requires proving contamination, currently a major bottleneck since existing tests demand expensive HPLC equipment, trained personnel, and long-distance sample shipping. To solve this, and guided by consultations with farmers, experts, and technicians, our project developed a portable fluorescent biosensor for 2,4-D. Utilizing the naturally occurring regulatory pair CadR/pCadA, our system recognizes the herbicide and induces an RNA aptamer to emit fluorescence. To optimize this interaction, we employed structural bioinformatics, integrating structure prediction, molecular docking, and dynamics simulations. The mechanism operates entirely within a cell-free system to ensure biosafety and containment. Paired with custom field-reading hardware and a digital reporting forum, 2,4-Detector provides a fast, cost-effective, on-site tool for immediate agricultural monitoring.

### UM-Macau — Guarding the Fridge: A Molecular Photocontrolled Biosensing Toolkit Discriminating Viable Listeria monocytogenes
*MAC · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6101) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/um-macau)

Listeria monocytogenes is a major foodborne pathogen capable of proliferating at refrigeration temperatures, posing significant risks to food safety and public health. To enable rapid detection of viable Listeria, we developed a dual-input AND-gate biosensing platform targeting Listeriolysin O (LLO), a virulence factor secreted only by living pathogenic cells. LLO-mediated membrane disruption serves as the first input, while a blue-light-responsive LOV system provides the second input, triggering split-sfGFP complementation or TEV-dependent reporter activation for signal amplification. By integrating fluorescence-based sensing with mathematical modeling, our system enables sensitive quantification of viable bacteria while distinguishing live cells from dead contaminants. Designed with input from food safety stakeholders, the platform is modular, portable, user-friendly, and capable of delivering results within one day, offering a practical solution for real-time food safety surveillance.

### Zhulu-Union — Persephone: PresERvation and Shelf-life Extension of fruits through PostHarvest Optimization by multi-eNgineered Enzymes
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6287) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/zhulu-union)

Persephone is a modular postharvest fruit preservation project targeting ethylene-driven ripening, oxidative damage, and fungal infection. It integrates four modules: ACC deaminase to reduce ethylene biosynthesis, ethylene receptor inhibitory peptides to interfere with ethylene signaling, MnSOD to scavenge superoxide radicals, and chitinase to inhibit fungal growth. ACCD and MnSOD are fused with the cell-penetrating peptide BP100 to improve access to intracellular substrates.

Through computational screening and experimental validation, we combined functional components ACCD–BP100, MnSOD–BP100, B03, and chitinase in a chitosan matrix. Under the tested conditions, this formulation delayed banana peel yellowing by approximately three days and showed less visible mold growth than the 1-MCP treatment group.

The intended product is a powder containing no live microorganisms, reconstituted before use and applied as a spray within existing postharvest workflows.It complements the cold chain, particularly during long-distance fruit transportation and at stages with insufficient cold-chain coverage.


## Foundational Advance (48)

### Austin-UTexas — RAD to the Future
*USA · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6163) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/austin-utexas)

Deinococcus radiodurans is a highly resilient bacterium capable of surviving ionizing radiation, severe desiccation, and extensive DNA damage, making it a promising chassis for synthetic biology in harsh environments such as space and hazardous waste sites. Our project aims to make D. radiodurans easier to engineer by expanding its genetic toolkit. We developed new parts and adapted existing parts for Golden Gate Assembly to improve modularity and standardization. We also explored TnpB, a compact Cas9-like system derived from mobile genetic elements, as a potential method to improve homologous recombination in the polyploid D. radiodurans genome. Additionally, we developed a modular gene repression system using catalytically inactive TnpB (dTnpB) and explored its potential for future predictive modeling. Together, these approaches expand the genetic tools available for engineering D. radiodurans and help lower barriers to using this resilient organism in synthetic biology.

### Bologna — PHAGED-IT: PHage Assissted Gene EDiting
*ITA · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6470) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/bologna)

Gene editing can rewrite DNA precisely, but it cannot choose which cell to enter. Because surface markers can be shared between pathological and healthy tissue, single-receptor targeting is not selective enough. PHAGED-IT addresses this issue with two engineered M13 bacteriophages that split the editing system between them: one, retargeted to EGFR, carries a the guide RNA; the other, retargeted to FRα, carries the Cas9-derived base editor. Neither edits alone, so the cell surface itself becomes a molecular AND-gate and editing occurs only where both receptors are present. We are building the system in parts. Our cell models, HEK293 and MDA-MB-231GFP, are established and their transfection optimized; several base editor and guide RNA combinations have been tested; our first phages have been produced and purified, and the EGFR and FRα targeting components are in preparation. The architecture is modular: changing the receptors aims the same platform at different cells.

### CalPolySLO — Synthetic Cells: An Accessible Platform for Education and Engineering
*USA · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6405) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/calpolyslo)

Synthetic biology and cell-free protein synthesis (CFPS) offer alternatives to engineering biology in living cells, but cost and technical complexity often keep these platforms out of undergraduate hands. Our team developed a modular enzyme-characterization toolkit and undergraduate curriculum that make cell-free and synthetic-cell systems accessible to non-experts. CHEM 471, “Engineering Synthetic Cells,” teaches synthetic-biology skills while using student outcomes to refine the platform and educational materials. We extended this work to Cold Spring Harbor’s Synthetic Biology Program, contributing protocols, hardware, and training. Our measurement toolkit centers on class III polyphosphate kinases (PPK2s), which regenerate nucleotide triphosphates from NDPs and NMPs. Its real-time assay measures consumption of multiple substrates to classify new PPK2s and characterize enzyme kinetics. PPK2-driven NTP regeneration strengthens cell-free systems as an alternative platform to living cells. Low-cost emulsion hardware enables reproducible synthetic-cell production in classrooms. Open designs, protocols, and data give future iGEM teams a reusable framework.

### Claremont CA — Phagenomics
*USA · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6317) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/claremont-ca)

Phage therapy's bottleneck is host range: each phage binds a specific receptor on a specific strain, so matching phage to target means screening, not design. We isolated our own system from soil at the California Botanic Garden: Pseudomonas capeferrum and an unnamed relative in the Pseudomonas putida group, with their co-occurring phages. Phage Phi8 is novel: 62,339 bp, 80 CDS, no database match, no virulence or resistance genes. Modelling then redirected the project. Structure prediction and protein language models showed the recognition step is not protein-protein: the receptor-binding apparatus is branched, and one accessory protein is a depolymerase degrading the bacterial polysaccharide layer. The key interaction is protein-sugar. We therefore asked whether ancestral depolymerases were more generalist, reconstructing ancestors of the phage's GH5 glycoside hydrolase, scoring them with an enzyme-conditioned language model, and expressing modern and ancestral enzymes in E. coli, reporting what worked and what did not.

### Columbia NYC — Project SENTINEL
*USA · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6410) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/columbia-nyc)

Stroke remains a leading cause of disability and death worldwide, with therapeutic efficacy declining rapidly post-onset. Although circulating biomarkers offer early diagnosis, the design of high-affinity biosensing binders remains constrained by the opacity of generative protein models. These models operate as black boxes, which obscures the features that drive high affinity and specificity. SENTINEL addresses this bottleneck by introducing a mechanistically-interpretable framework for de novo protein binder engineering. By integrating Sparse Autoencoders with ESM-based protein models, we extract human-interpretable features associated with molecular recognition to steer towards high-affinity binding. We benchmark SENTINEL against leading design architectures and, as proof-of-concept, validate candidate binder pairs for clinically relevant stroke biomarkers (UCH-L1, VILIP-1, and B-FABP) through split reporter survival assays. Through coupling resolved AI design principles and synthetic biology, SENTINEL aims to establish mechanistic interpretability as a universal accelerant for rational protein binder design—adaptable to any disease where early detection saves lives.

### Concordia-Montreal — BiblioTK: Engineering a Gram-Positive Toolkit System
*CAN · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6091) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/concordia-montreal)

Across different iGEM projects, the BiblioTK team noticed a clear trend: Gram-negative bacteria dominate, favoured for their well-characterised nature and familiarity.  Their Gram-positive counterparts, however, remain underutilised despite strong and relevant applications. To bridge this gap, we are expanding the existing Gram-positive genetic engineering platform, SubtiToolKit (STK), by developing functional tools for other under-researched strains. Our approach centres around two new modular parts: Part E, providing modular origins of replication as independently exchangeable components within STK’s Golden Gate grammar and pGRAMLIN, a split-marker shuttle vector that couples antibiotic selection to correct recircularisation, improving transformation efficiency in Gram-positive hosts. Alongside the wet-lab, we developed Project Bookmark, a statistical analysis tool that identifies candidate promoter regions across bacterial sequences, streamlining genetic part discovery. Together, these tools make established Gram-positive chassis for synthetic biology and biomanufacturing, extending a shareable, cross-species-transferable parts standard to organisms with therapeutic and industrial promise beyond traditional model organisms.

### Duke — PACERS: Refining PACE with RNA Switch Control
*USA · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6487) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/duke)

Phage Assisted Continuous Evolution (PACE) accelerates protein engineering by linking protein activity to bacteriophage propagation. However, leaky expression of the infectivity protein pIII can reduce host cell susceptibility to infection and allow suboptimal variants to propagate. To address this challenge, Duke iGEM developed PACERS (Phage Assisted Continuous Evolution with RNA Switch), a strategy incorporating RNA toehold switches to regulate pIII translation. These synthetic RNA structures repress translation without a trigger RNA and activate it when the trigger is present. By placing the toehold switch upstream of gene III and encoding its trigger sequence in the selection phage, PACERS aims to minimize unintended pIII production while enabling activation following infection. With this controlled expression system, PACERS has the potential to improve continuous protein evolution reliability and support applications in medicine, agriculture, and biotechnology.

### DUT-China — SHUFFLE: Self-iterative High-throughput Unlocked Flexible Frequency Loci Editor
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6328) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/dut-china)

Improving microbial cell factories requires precise, diverse mutations across multiple loci, yet conventional genome-editing methods are limited by insufficient targeting control, restricted substitution spectra, or double-strand-break toxicity. We developed SHUFFLE (Self-iterative High-throughput Unlocked Flexible Frequency Loci Editor), a targeted N-to-N base-editing platform combining dual-activity TadDE, Cas9 nickase, and paired guide RNAs. Iterative engineering expanded PAM recognition with nSpCas9-NG, incorporated AlkA and CWBEmini modules, and evolved TadDE using EvolvePro to obtain A158R. The optimized TadDE_A158R–nSpCas9_NG–AlkA editor achieved up to approximately 50% editing efficiency at tested loci and generated diverse nucleotide substitutions. SHUFFLE enabled diversification of global regulators to identify strains with improved growth and polyhydroxybutyrate production on ethanol, and editing of the accA ribosome-binding site to improve malonyl-CoA availability, as indicated by flaviolin screening. SHUFFLE provides a versatile approach to targeted diversification for microbial strain engineering.

### ETH-Zurich — preCISe: modular and targeted protein delivery into mammalian cells using extracellular contractile injection systems
*CHE · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6289) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/eth-zurich)

Protein therapeutics offer unique advantages as inhibitors and enzymes, making them promising candidates for treating a wide range of diseases. However, their inability to efficiently cross cell membranes generally limits their access to intracellular targets. preCISe addresses this challenge with a platform for target-specific protein delivery based on extracellular contractile injection systems (eCISs), bacterial nanosyringes that inject cargo directly into the cytosol. The platform comprises a modular eCIS design that can be retargeted to new receptors by exchanging their binding domains, preCISion reporter cell lines that quantify intracellular delivery, and our deCISion software which finds cell-specific receptors and binders and returns ready-to-order DNA sequences. Because receptors unique to one cell type are rare, we initiated a dual-targeting strategy: the cargo is split between two eCISs addressing two receptors and reassembles only in cells displaying both. Our software OpenSplitProtein ranks split sites that preserve folding and spontaneous reassembly.

### Evry-Paris-Saclay — ORBIT - Orthogonal Ribosome Bioengineering Iterative Toolbox
*FRA · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6063) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/evry-paris-saclay)

The ribosome builds every protein in every living cell and it is one of the hardest machines in biology to engineer - a change that suits an engineered purpose usually cripples the host.
