Orthogonal ribosomes sidestep this by reading only their own messages. ORBIT set out to establish the system that can evolve it.



Three advances had never been combined: a tethered ribosome whose subunits cannot swap with native ones, an evolved 16S rRNA that translates faster and the cleanest orthogonal binding-site pair described. We built every combination - 16 ribosome plasmids and nine reporters, all sequence-verified and obtained E. coli strains surviving solely on tethered ribosomes. The improvements do not transfer between architectures, so a tethered ribosome must be evolved on its own terms. 



ORBIT delivers a characterized toolkit for tethered-ribosome evolution: strains, parts, readouts, software and a clear account of what each of them does.

### Example — Best year yet
*FRA · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6039) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/example)

Maybe this is our year?

### FHNW — AI-Assisted Nanobody Discovery for AAV Analytics
*CHE · None · withdrawn* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6205) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/fhnw)

Adeno-associated viruses (AAVs) are widely used viral vectors in gene therapy, enabling the delivery of genetic material into target cells. As their importance in biomedical research and therapeutic development continues to grow, there is an increasing need for efficient and reliable methods to monitor and optimize AAV production.

Our project aims to develop a nanobody-based approach for AAV analysis. Nanobodies are small antibody fragments that combine high target specificity with simple production, making them valuable tools for biotechnology and diagnostics.

To achieve this goal, we integrate computational design with experimental validation. Potential nanobody candidates are designed and optimized in silico before being tested and characterized in the laboratory. By combining computational and wet-lab approaches, we seek to establish a proof-of-concept system for the detection and analysis of AAV particles.

### FHS-China — ProLac: Characterization of a Probiotic and In Silico Optimize Lactose Consumption for Lactose Intolerance Relief
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6448) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/fhs-china)

Lactose intolerance is a highly prevalent digestive disorder. Over 90% of adults in Asia lose lactase persistence, and many of these people suffer from symptomatic lactose intolerance. Distinct from previous iGEM teams that aimed to construct new engineered strains, we launched ProLac, an observation-first synthetic biology project. We characterized an acid- and oxygen-tolerant probiotic *Lacticaseibacillus paracasei* subsp. paracasei CICC 6109 with growth curve assays and plasmids sequence analysis. With COBRApy, we performed in silico *manM* knockout simulation on *Lacticaseibacillus paracasei* ATCC 334 based on a published genome-scale metabolic model to redirect carbon metabolism for preferential lactose utilization. To address the wet-lab experimental challenges, we managed to develop a multifunctional fluorometer. We also built an open-source tool for campus biology map for science outreach and released our campus map as a demo.

### Fudan — GlycoGarden: A Programmable Platform for Glycoengineering
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6293) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/fudan)

Glycans are essential biomolecules involved in protein function, immune recognition, and disease, yet they remain difficult to study and engineer because their biosynthesis is non-template-based, heterogeneous, and highly dependent on intracellular localization. GlycoGarden aims to bridge this gap by developing a yeast-based synthetic biology platform for programmable glycoengineering, in which engineered intracellular glycosylation pathways are rationally rewired to produce defined, controllable glycan outputs on yeast surfaces. Based on this platform, GlycoGarden facilitates glycosylation mechanism studies, cancer-associated glycoantigen therapy development, high-throughput screening of glycan-binding molecules, and production of glycan-based bioingredients for biomedical and food-related applications. Ultimately, GlycoGarden seeks to establish a modular synthetic biology platform that makes glycan research and glycoengineering, with long-term public value.

### GTIIT-China — Self-Exciting Quantum Dots: Bioluminescence-Driven Excitation of Yeast-Biosynthesised CdSe
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6483) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/gtiit-china)

Quantum dots are bright fluorophores whose colour is set by particle size, but they need an external light source, which also excites the surroundings. We built a quantum-dot system that excites itself. The yeast Yarrowia lipolytica was engineered to synthesise cadmium selenide inside the cell from selenite and cadmium salts, and to express a fusion of the CdSe-binding peptide CdSeBP to NanoLuc luciferase. Supplied with furimazine, NanoLuc emits blue light and transfers energy to the bound particle, which then emits in the red without illumination. Absorption spectroscopy tracked cadmium sulfide formation, fluorescence spectroscopy compared selenium and cadmium combinations, peptide binding was measured in vitro, and confocal microscopy showed that emission originates inside cells and requires both precursors. Finally, an extra copy of MET25, a selenium-pathway gene, raised particle emission several-fold. Biosynthesised quantum dots can therefore be excited by an enzyme rather than by a lamp.

### Heidelberg — VOYAGE: Programmable Gas Vesicles for Acoustic Observation and Organization
*DEU · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6249) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/heidelberg)

We present VOYAGE, a modular platform that enables gas vesicles to become a programmable interface between ultrasound and synthetic biology. These genetically encoded protein nanostructures scatter acoustic waves and collapse at defined pressures, enabling their use for measuring and manipulating cells. We engineered gas vesicle variants for multiplexed in vivo imaging and coupled enzyme activity and the presence of soluble biomarkers to acoustic signals. Beyond observation, our toolbox enables precise spatial organization, and sorting of distinct cell types. Our computational model, ATLAS, built alongside experimental data, unites simulation, generative protein design, and deep-learning image analysis into a framework. Inside and outside the lab, our Fail Forward initiative builds an open culture around setbacks in science through education and by addressing challenges at both individual and systemic levels. Uniting our toolbox, computational modeling, and a culture of learning from failure, VOYAGE transforms how we explore, understand, and engineer biology using acoustics.

### HKUST — Developing a promoter toolkit for Clostridium butyricum
*HKG · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6213) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/hkust)

Out team aims to develop a set of reliable, standardized promoters for Clostridium butyricum. Ultimately, we want to contribute to the broader characterization of C. butyricum. Human practices runs alongside these efforts, providing feedback that directly informs our design, shapes how we communicate risks and benefits, and guides our education initiatives.

### HongKong-PuiChing — Conditional Localized Assembly of Synthetic Proteins (CLASP)
*HKG · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6088) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/hongkong-puiching)

Within living cells, functions arise from conditional spatial assembly of molecules. Synthetic biology enables gene-expression editing, yet post-translational spatial control remains limited by chemical or light methods that leak and lack geometric precision.



CLASP (Conditional Localized Assembly of Synthetic Proteins) overcomes this with a modular scaffold using a rigid CTPR backbone, customizable sensors, payloads, split-luciferase reporting, and optional covalent locking. It enables programmable nanometer-precision proximity: recruit, report, retain, and react in response to chosen signals.



ORIGAMI, a physics-based active-learning tool, models structure-proximity relationships to guide closed-loop CLASP design.



CLASP can be applied in salinity-responsive enzyme assembly and biomarker diagnostics as it is a universal, geometrically programmable platform that turns induced proximity into deliberate engineering.

### IITKGP — dSyPhER: dynamic Synthetic Phosphorylation via Effector Response
*IND · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6162) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/iitkgp)

Chronic inflammation and the lack of precise, tunable control over cell signaling remain key challenges in engineering therapeutic cell systems. DySPHER (Dynamic Synthetic Phosphorylation via Effector Response) addresses this using modular synthetic receptors in HEK293T cells that convert extracellular TNFα and rapalog binding into programmable, ligand-specific phosphorylation cascades. By coupling receptor dimerization to PUmHD-zipper interactions and IRES-eIF4E-mediated translational control, our platform enables precise, user-defined activation of gene expression, offering a generalizable framework for sensing disease-relevant and chemically inducible signals and translating them into controllable cellular outputs, with applications in inflammation biosensing and cell-based therapeutics. Guided by extensive stakeholder engagement, school and community outreach, and a clear entrepreneurship roadmap, DySPHER reflects our commitment to science that is both technically rigorous and grounded in real-world relevance and responsibility.

### Imperial College — reGELerate - structured, functionalised, prion hydrogels
*GBR · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6377) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/imperial-college)

The goal of our project was to develop a tunable biomaterial where protein fibrils are treated as discrete mechanical struts as opposed to a tangled aggregate. Non-infectious prions and amyloids have long been used by iGEM teams in everything from bio-wires to gene switches but we chose to focus on HET-s from Podospora Anserina which is an attractive biomaterials candidate due its lack of structural polymorphism. Alongside our wet lab work we produced dry lab modelling for every step using in silico construct design, molecular dynamics and aggregation modelling. Alongside our human practices interviews we chose to focus specifically on biosecurity and bioethics producing frameworks examining how our work intersects with diverse stakeholders. We hope these resources could also be applied more broadly for future synthetic biology innovations. Finally, through our education campaign we engaged every level of society from primary school up to experienced scientists.

### JLU-CP — DigiCAT：A Multi-Threshold Biosensor for Monitoring of Feline Diabetic Ketoacidosis
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6234) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/jlu-cp)

Converting continuous molecular signals into programmable genetic decisions remains a challenge in synthetic biology. We developed a modular multi-threshold biosensing platform using β-hydroxybutyrate (BHB), a biomarker associated with feline diabetic ketoacidosis, as a proof of concept. BDH converts BHB into acetoacetate (AcAc), which is sensed by the AtoSC two-component system to regulate the expression of Bxb1 and phiC31 integrases. By tuning integrase expression through ribosome-binding sites (RBSs) and integrating mathematical modeling to guide threshold design, our system aims to convert continuous molecular inputs into discrete genetic states and fluorescent outputs. This work provides a modular framework for programmable multi-threshold biosensing.

### Keio-Japan — One Direction
*JPN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6488) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/keio-japan)

The development of intercellular adhesion technologies that enable the formation of specific topologies is essential for advancing synthetic multicellular systems. However, existing cell adhesion platforms have primarily focused on controlling the selectivity of cell-cell adhesion, without achieving control over the spatial orientation of cells. In this project, we aim to develop a foundational technology that enables the design of cell orientation in multicellular systems. We propose a genetic design that localizes adhesion machinery to both poles of rod-shaped Escherichia coli, enabling control over the spatial orientation of adhering cells.

### KIT — ENCORE: Engineered Nanocompartments for Capture, Optimization and Reactor Engineering
*DEU · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6296) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/kit)

No standardized cloning system previously existed for expressing programmable protein nanocompartments, encapsulins, together with customizable cargo in a eukaryotic host. To address this, we developed the ENCORE toolKIT: a Golden Gate-based, modular cloning system for combining encapsulins, cargo proteins, targeting peptides, linkers, and promoters in Saccharomyces cerevisiae. Built on a domesticated shuttle vector, the toolKIT organizes parts into five-part cassettes across a three-level cloning hierarchy. We extended the yeast MoClo standard by splitting its single coding sequence position into separate modules, so that shells, targeting peptides, linkers and cargo can be exchanged independently rather than cloned anew for every combination. To support accessible construct design, we developed ENCORE Builder, a web-based software tool for in silico construct planning. We demonstrate the platform through a nanoreactor enabling oxygen-free biocatalysis, the confinement of a hydrogen peroxide-dependent enzyme cascade, and an mRNA capture system preserving transcriptomic snapshots of stress responses for retrospective analysis.

### Leiden — Vascular Engineered Self-assembling Scaffold for Living Organoids)
*NLD · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6204) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/leiden)

Testing new drugs and treatments in reliable preclinical models is an essential step before they reach patients. Commonly used models, however, do not fully reflect how human tissues behave. Organoids offer a promising alternative because they form more complex and mature structures, and represent human biology more accurately than traditional models. Organoids face one major limitation: the lack of blood vessels (vascularization). With VESSL, we aim to address the challenge of vascularization by developing a peptide-based scaffold to support the formation of vascular-like structures in 3D bioprinted organoids. We designed peptides that self-assemble into fibres, forming a network that endothelial cells can attach to and migrate along. Through computational modeling, we assessed different designs to identify candidates predicted to form stable coiled-coil structures and efficiently self-assemble. Five promising peptide candidates were then selected for production using the bacterium E. coli, the fungus Aspergillus niger, and cell-free protein synthesis (CFPS).

### Marburg — CLiPPR - A Toolbox for Flexible, Inducible Gene Expression in Chloroplasts
*DEU · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6350) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/marburg)

Plants account for around 99% of Earth's new biomass. Through photosynthesis they convert light, water and CO₂ into valuable biomolecules, possibly making them the cheapest biofactories we have. Yet for biocompound production we almost always reach for microbes; not for plants, and least of all their chloroplasts.

Chloroplasts combine photosynthesis with high-level recombinant protein production. But growth and production compete, limiting yield. In microbes, dynamic control decouples the two; currently equivalent chloroplast tools are limited. Plants themselves offer a solution: they regulate chloroplast gene expression post-transcriptionally via nuclear-encoded RNA-binding proteins.



We introduce CLiPPR (Chloroplast Library for Inducible PPRs), a modular toolbox for on-demand control of chloroplast gene expression. Synthetic Pentatricopeptide Repeat proteins (PPRs) bind engineered 5'-untranslated regions placed upstream of any gene of interest, while nuclear inducible promoters provide temporal control over PPR production.



Testing in cell-free extracts, Chlamydomonas reinhardtii and Nicotiana benthamiana, CLiPPR aims to advance resilient chloroplast-based production.

### Munich — Primetheus
*DEU · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6111) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/munich)

While nuclear genome engineering has advanced rapidly, mitochondrial gene editing remains fundamentally unexplored, current methods being limited to specific single nucleotide substitutions. Prime editing is a gene editing system consisting of a Cas nickase fused to a reverse transcriptase (RT) and a prime editing guide RNA (pegRNA) used as a template to synthesize a new, edited strand without introducing double-strand DNA breaks. Our project Primetheus adapts this system for mitochondria through separate import of the fused Cas-RT complex and the pegRNA into mitochondria. To overcome the technical challenge of verifying intra-mitochondrial RNA delivery, we are also developing a novel, genetically encoded RNA-based readout system to enable continuous live-cell RNA monitoring. Primetheus will enable researchers to introduce a wider range of edits, including all base substitutions, as well as longer insertions into mitochondrial DNA, allowing in-depth studies of the mitochondrial genome and unlocking new therapeutic approaches.

### NAU-CHINA — CellApp: Compartmentalization via Engineered Liquid-Liquid Phase Separation for Achieving Programmable Protein Regulation
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6419) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/nau-china)

Synthetic biology has long sought to improve metabolic efficiency through compartmentalization, yet engineering dynamic and reversible compartments in prokaryotes remains a major challenge. Traditional rigid protein scaffolds often impair enzyme activity and lack orthogonal control. To address these challenges, our project developed a tunable dynamic membrane-less organelle toolbox in E. coli based on liquid-liquid phase separation. Specifically, we designed several orthogonal modules that respectively enable long-term static sequestration, reversible condensation in response to rapid light-controlled aggregation, temperature and pH, and intein-mediated repeated enzyme swapping. These modules collectively provide programmable, reversible and orthogonal control of compartmentalization. Our toolbox offers a versatile platform for regulation in synthetic biology, promoting efficient biomanufacturing.

### NUDT-CHINA — Ubik: Programming Degradation with AI-Designed Ubiquitin Codes
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6307) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/nudt-china)

Cells naturally dispose of unwanted proteins by tagging them with a "destroy me" signal called ubiquitin. Targeted protein degradation tries to hijack this system to eliminate disease-causing proteins, but current methods like PROTACs are limited because they must recruit a handful of natural E3 ligases, cellular machines that attach the ubiquitin tag, leading to a shortage of suitable drug targets, poor drug-like properties, and unpredictable tissue effects. We take a different approach: instead of recruiting natural E3 ligases, we use AI to design entirely new, programmable “ubiquitin codes”, miniature artificial modules that directly catalyze the ubiquitin tagging of any chosen disease protein. By creating these synthetic degradation signals from scratch, we bypass the E3 bottleneck entirely, making the system more predictable, more broadly applicable, and more drug-oriented. This transforms targeted protein degradation from a hunt for natural E3 ligands into a programmable, AI driven engineering discipline.

### Patras-Med — ICHNOS: Trace the Invisible
*GRC · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6459) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/patras-med)

Every cell has a history that its present state may not reveal. As transient stress responses fade, information about their intensity and timing becomes difficult to recover, complicating assessments of experimental reliability and reproducibility.

ICHNOS addresses this challenge through a modular synthetic biology platform in Saccharomyces cerevisiae, combining stress-responsive sensing, a TetR-based circuit and a tandem fluorescent timer. ICHNOS-Vail, our software tool application, analyses fluorescence signals through image analysis and mathematical modelling to estimate past stress events.

At the heart of our project lies modularity: interchangeable sensing elements connect different biological inputs to a shared processing and reporting architecture. This design, combined with insights from stakeholder discussions, led us to explore potential future applications in the fields of biomanufacturing, cosmetics, food and nutrition and agriculture.

Together, ICHNOS and ICHNOS-Vail provide a foundation for adaptable cellular recording, helping researchers investigate both a cell’s current state and the experiences that shaped it.

### Princeton — SymPleco: Engineering a High-Surface-Area, Light-Regenerable, Bio-Safe Phage Platform for Precision Toxin Sequestration
*USA · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6295) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/princeton)

SymPleco is a precision sequestration toolkit that opens the door to novel cancer therapeutics, a last-line-of-defense cure for neurological diseases, and safe environmental bioremediation for the world’s waterways. We modified the M13 bacteriophage chassis to support orthogonal sortase ligation of proteins for molecular sequestration. Combined with our software SymPlify, which designs high-affinity de novo binder proteins for globular proteins and small molecules, the SymPleco toolkit provides a foundational advance in building tunable capture technologies for any target. Our functionalized phages also proved to be ingested and digested by murine bone marrow-derived macrophages, allowing for their biosafe removal. Our immediate future direction of incorporating photo-switchable opto-monobody domains will introduce light‑dependent affinity changes, allowing renewable release without chemical elution. Altogether, we built a novel, highly programmable, scalable yet specific sequestration platform pivotal to environmental and medical remediation that bridges the intractable trade-off between capacity and specificity in current adsorption technologies.

### Queens-Canada — MOSAIC: A Modular Operator System for AND-gated Input Coupling in engineered Escherichia coli
*CAN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6228) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/queens-canada)

Engineered bacterial biosensors enable programmable detection of molecular signals, with applications across healthcare, technology, and environmental monitoring. Yet many existing systems remain constrained to single-target detection and application-specific designs, limiting their adaptability where cellular context is dictated by multiple stimuli. MOSAIC is a proof of concept architecture for constructing customizable dual-input biosensors in E. coli, integrating pairs of interchangeable two-component regulatory systems (TCSs) with a genetic AND logic gate. In silico and in vitro work provide foundational data for characterizing individual parts and their integration into functional biosensors, supporting a modular toolkit of three functional layers: wild-type and variant TCSs for sensing, split T7 RNA polymerase for signal integration, and a choice of reporter modules for measurable output. We present a standardized and flexible framework for engineering dual-input AND-gate biosensors in E. coli, enabling interchangeable sensing architectures that can be adapted and expanded by future iGEM teams and researchers.

### Rice — DigiActivate: Chemically Induced, Tunable Gene Activation
*USA · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6250) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/rice)

Cell fate is governed by fluctuations in gene expression over time, yet CRISPR transcriptional tools are binary, holding a gene ON or OFF at a fixed level over a fixed period. Engineering that timing and magnitude is what therapies for haploinsufficiency or T cell exhaustion require. DigiActivate achieves this by fusing an array of inducer gated switches onto dCas9, where each switch controls the recruitment of a different transcriptional activator. We tested multiple levels of activation on a two switch array through flow cytometry, where we observed distinct 1.3, 1.7 and 1.9 fold increases in eGFP mean fluorescence intensity corresponding to inducer additions of B, A and A+B respectively. Finally, we used RFdiffusion to design compact de novo binders replacing the natural, bulky E3 ligase domains. The result is a modular part collection in which the inducer or the effector can be changed by swapping a single part.

### ShanghaiTech-China — MOSAIC: Modular Orthogonal Stochastic Automated Integrated Consortia
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6334) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/shanghaitech-china)

MOSAIC is a genetic toolkit for constructing multicellular communities with diverse cell states and tunable population ratios. It uses Cre-lox recombination to generate different DNA configurations and Bxb1-att recombination to convert them into distinct cellular states. These communities are measured through reporter signals and evaluated using our hardware and software workflow to identify suitable population compositions. Once a desired ratio is selected, the reporter system can be switched to functional outputs, allowing different cell types to cooperate in research, medical, and industrial applications.

### TAU-Israel — CERNAL: Compiler-like Engine for RNA-based Logic
*ISR · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6196) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/tau-israel)

Synthetic biology aims to program cells to sense and respond to various signals; however, we currently lack a universal framework that allows these conditional actions to be easily programmed. Since cells already respond to environmental and internal changes through shifts in their gene expression, harnessing that into actionable inputs enables enhanced programming. To achieve this, we present CERNAL: a computational platform that transforms transcriptomic data into universal, RNA-based logic circuits. CERNAL is a computational compiler designed to evaluate differential gene expression profiles and construct RNA-based logic circuits. The CERNAL pipeline identifies specific genes to act as inputs, computes the corresponding logic gates (e.g., toehold switches and CRISPR sgRNAs switches), assembles potential circuit architectures, and outputs the optimal configuration based on predictive modeling. We demonstrate the versatility and entrepreneurial viability of the CERNAL platform through validation of two applications: a prokaryotic PFAS biosensor and a therapeutic strategy for eukaryotic Endometriosis.

### Tianjin — Genome Revival
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6404) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/tianjin)

Genome Revival asks whether a yeast genome can regain independent growth through temporary support from another cell. We explored protoplast fusion and developed a genetic route using Saccharomyces eubayanus spores lacking the essential gene CDC15. Mating with Saccharomyces cerevisiae provided temporary support. We then restored CDC15 and used HAnDy-derived tools to remove the helper chromosomes. Parental-marker PCR, whole-genome sequencing coverage and growth assays supported recovery of the S. eubayanus lineage. The sealing-wax system combines red colony colour with a DNA watermark for lineage identification. We organised chromosome-elimination components into the CENSOR Collection and developed the Genome Revival Toolkit for DNA design. Public engagement and comment analysis shaped our communication and ethical reflection, leading to SBTI, an interactive exploration of bioethical perspectives. Together, these efforts connect experimental genome revival with reusable tools and public discussion.

### Toulouse-INSA-UT — CHIRALYS: Aptamers through the looking glass
*FRA · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6390) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/toulouse-insa-ut)

Our team iGEM Toulouse has designed next-generation DNA aptamers to bind proteins, pathogens or cancer cells with unprecedented stability. Current aptamers built from “right-handed” nucleotides, the natural chirality found in biology, are prone to degradation in the human body and in a physiological environment. We propose to create mirror-image, “left-handed” aptamers that will evade natural degradation mechanisms, enhancing their long-term binding capabilities. We devised experimental and in silico strategies to design, produce, and characterize mirror aptamers targeting peptides and proteins. Moreover, we engaged in the debate about the risks and ethical issues of creating a “mirror life”.

### Tsinghua — NucleArk—— A Nuclear Delivery System Based on Phase-Separating Condensates
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6195) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/tsinghua)

Efficient nuclear delivery into slowly dividing cells remains a major challenge, particularly for large nucleic acids such as plasmids. Yet, many such cells—including retinal cells, cardiomyocytes, and neurons—are critical therapeutic targets in the human body. This delivery bottleneck has significantly hindered genetic therapies targeting these cell types. By leveraging the high biocompatibility, low biotoxicity, and efficient cargo encapsulation capacity of phase-separated droplets, we aim to establish a safe and effective platform for plasmid nuclear delivery.

### TU-Dresden — MOCHI - Making OCH1 Controlled Humanization Inducible
*DEU · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6468) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/tu-dresden)

We use a tool called RNA interference (RNAi), a natural mechanism for regulating gene expression, that lowers the production of the targeted gene’s protein by repressing the translation of the complementary mRNA. Our work builds upon the work of Ruan et. al. (2025) that showed that RNAi can be established in Komagataella phaffii when the Argonaut 1 enzyme from Naumovozyma castelli is introduced together with a double stranded RNA (dsRNA) precursor. This publication points out a limitation: the silencing might weaken over time. Our proposed solution is creating a stronger dsRNA signal by introducing an RNA dependent RNA polymerase that constantly supplies new dsRNA molecules. We want to achieve this by introducing QDE-1 ΔN from Neurospora crassa. The result we hypothesize to be more templates for further silencing and the creation of a self-enhancing loop.

### USTC — TEMPO: Timed Expression and Suicide Module with Programmable Oscillator-Coupled Binary Counter
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6202) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/ustc)

Biological functions depend not only on what cells do, but also on when they act. Many existing genetic timing strategies couple timekeeping directly to a specific downstream function, so adapting them to a new task often requires redesigning the timing circuit itself. TEMPO instead separates timekeeping from execution, providing a modular internal clock that can connect to different downstream programs. It integrates three modules: an oscillator-based CLOCK that produces periodic internal signals, a reversible MEMORY that records successive signals as binary DNA states, and an ACTUATOR that links selected counts to specific outputs. Binary encoding enables multiple stages to be represented without adding a complete regulatory layer for every new step. By tuning the clock period, target count, and output gene, the architecture provides a general framework for staged biomanufacturing, sequential research perturbations, timed therapeutic programs, and programmed elimination for biocontainment.

### UTDallas — SPARC (Signal Protease Amplification Reaction Cascade)
*USA · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6253) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/utdallas)

Both natural and synthetic genetic circuits amplify inputs through gene expression, whereas protein-based circuits promise faster response times than genetic circuits. We constructed SPARC, a synthetic protein circuit that uses a series of synthetic proteases to rapidly amplify input signals and produce custom secreted protein outputs. First, we engineered the SPARC input module by using rapamycin-activated proteases. The activated protease then cascades to activate another synthetic protease using antiparallel coiled coils, which can be reconstituted only upon proteolytic cleavage. To complete SPARC with a custom output module, we engineered protein secretion using secreted embryonic alkaline phosphatase (SEAP) that can be activated by the protease cascade module. Combining these modules will establish a foundation for a fast, programmable cell signaling pathway at the protein level.

### Utrecht — EVerse - Reaching Beyond Biological Barriers
*NLD · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6138) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/utrecht)

Biological barriers protect our organs from harmful substances, yet they can also prevent medicines from reaching the tissues that need them. EVerse explores a way around this delivery problem using extracellular vesicles (EVs) and aims to provide researchers with a ready-to-use, non-immunogenic vehicle that can be easily modulated to target and endocytose any cell in the body. An EV-associated scaffold protein presents a SpyCatcher on the surface of the vesicle, which can be conjugated to a SpyTag-equipped nanobody. By exchanging nanobodies, it is possible for the vesicle to recognise different receptors and proteins without rebuilding the entire carrier. We chose the blood-brain barrier (BBB) as our proof of concept to validate the engineered system through cell-based characterisation, transwell assays, and zebrafish embryos. These experiments are an early test of the platform's design and capabilities, with further work needed to establish reliable transport and functional cargo delivery.

### Vilnius-Lithuania — BMClick: One Shell to Click Them All
*LTU · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6043) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/vilnius-lithuania)

Cells provide organisation at the cost of control, and test tubes offer precise conditions but lack order. Neither approach keeps pace with what we ask of biomolecules in today’s rapidly advancing scientific world. Confronted by this problem, we drew inspiration from bacterial microcompartments (BMCs), aiming to bring a part of cellular organisation to the tube. By modifying a single BMC component - the trimer - we were not only able to create a microscopic box capable of encapsulating and displaying novel cargo, but also an environment where that cargo is protected. And if a shell is not what one needs, the trimer can be converted into a functional material that holds cargo on an open surface, be it active enzymes or even whole microorganisms. All these options define BMClick as a modular toolbox that can find its purpose in industry, therapy, and fundamental science.

### Virginia — ChronoContain
*USA · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6256) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/virginia)

Engineered organisms are increasingly being deployed outside the laboratory setting, creating biosafety risks if they remain or spread in an open environment. Current biocontainment systems often rely on environmental triggers, which can fail under changing real-world conditions. Needed is a biological device that enables controllable and predictably timed cell death, allowing cells to function normally for a defined time before autonomously activating a lethal lysis mechanism. Our solution to this problem is a modular, time-controlled genetic kill switch that enables predictable and autonomous activation of cell death after a defined period of time. We are engineering a modular timed kill switch in E. coli that leverages bacteriophage-derived holin-antiholin systems to enable precisely timed cell lysis. Our controllable timed lysis system provides predictable, autonomous containment, enabling safer deployment of engineered microbes. Our timed self-lysis system is a foundational advance that will enhance the opportunities for deployment of synthetic biology devices.

### Washington — HedgehogSense: A Platform for Tuning Ciliary Receptor Abundance
*USA · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6229) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/washington)

The Hedgehog (Hh) signaling pathway plays an essential role in embryonic development, tissue homeostasis, and cell differentiation. Dysregulation of this pathway is associated with developmental disorders and several cancers. A key component of Hedgehog Sinaling, Smoothened (SMO), is regulated by the MGRN1–MEGF8–MOSMO (MMM) complex, which controls SMO ubiquitination.



HedgehogSense aims to engineer a modular platform for controlling ciliary receptor abundance by repurposing and disrupting endogenous regulatory machinery. Our system explores two complementary approaches: first, we use chemically induced dimerization to recruit the E3 ubiquitin ligase MGRN1 to SMO, with the long-term goal of extending this recruitment strategy to other ciliary membrane receptors. Second, we are developing a reproducible computational protein design pipeline to engineer miniprotein disruptors that interfere with endogenous MMM complex interactions, providing an opposing mechanism to prevent overdegradation. Ultimately, HedgehogSense seeks to move beyond SMO-specific regulation toward a modular platform capable of tuning ciliary receptor abundance.

### WHU-China — Differphase
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6220) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/whu-china)

Differphase is a synthetic biology platform for programmable cellular differentiation through liquid–liquid phase separation (LLPS)-mediated asymmetric plasmid partitioning. Founder cells establish polarized intracellular organization via LLPS and engineered c-di-GMP signaling gradients, biasing plasmid segregation during division to generate distinct cell lineages through intermediate buffer states. Orthogonal plasmid partitioning systems (ParB/parS and SopB/sopC) enable irreversible lineage diversification by physically separating genetic modules, while a light-responsive dedifferentiation module partially restores the founder state to maintain long-term population stability. Coupled with a toxin–antitoxin stability module, Differphase provides a modular framework for engineering dynamic, self-maintaining multicellular behaviors in bacteria. By overcoming key limitations of conventional co-culture systems, it offers a generalizable strategy with potential applications in tumor-targeted therapies, programmed microbial consortia for biomanufacturing, environmental remediation, plastic-degrading biofilms, and distributed multicellular logic systems.

### William-and-Mary — BRO[K]EN: Building Resilient Optimized Engineered Networks
*USA · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6243) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/william-and-mary)

Synthetic biology has enormous potential to address real-world challenges, however engineered biological systems must do more than work initially: they must work reliably and safely over time. Like all engineered systems, genetic circuits can break. Mutations, selection, burden, and environmental stresses can compromise function. Systematic failure analysis are not yet routine across synthetic biology, particularly in realistic operating environments. To address this challenge,  William_and_Mary  iGEM  developed BROKEN: Building Resilient Optimized Engineered Networks, bringing failure-analysis principles from mature engineering disciplines into synthetic biology. Our project integrates three goals: DETECT, MITIGATE, and PREDICT circuit failure. We performed longitudinal experiments on four engineered circuits in laboratory and simulated environmental conditions; developed mathematical and machine-learning approaches; engineered detection and prevention systems; and used multitiered functional and molecular measurements to investigate deterioration. BROKEN seeks to move synthetic biology beyond circuits that work initially toward systems that remain predictable, dependable, and safe throughout their intended use.

### XJTLU-China — dogmaOS: Translation-Folding Control for Protein Expression in Escherichia coli
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6096) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/xjtlu-china)

From recombinant insulin to modern biopharmaceuticals, prokaryotes have been widely used for protein production due to their low cost, rapid growth, and mature genetic toolkits. However, many eukaryotic proteins remain difficult to express in soluble and functional forms in prokaryotes, as differences in translation dynamics and cellular environments can affect protein folding. To address this challenge, we introduced dogmaOS, a programmable translation operating system built in E. coli. It explores codon optimization and tRNA pool engineering as a co-design problem, enabling programmable control of translation and protein folding. Our model integrates promoter activity, tRNA availability, elongation rate, and co-translational folding into a unified framework to guide coding sequence design. Based on model predictions, dogmaOS tunes elongation dynamics to better match protein folding requirements. By treating translation as a programmable biological operating system, dogmaOS enables precise control over protein synthesis, opening new possibilities for controllable protein production in prokaryotes.

### XMU-China — STING: Small TnpB-based Innovative Nuclease toolkit for bacterial Genome editing
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6059) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/xmu-china)

STING develops a compact bacterial genome-editing toolkit based on ISHahl1 TnpB. The system achieved gene deletion in E. coli (4.2–100%) and P. putida (30–44%), alongside transcriptional repression and cytosine base editing. Structure-guided evolution expanded recognition to five noncanonical TAMs. Computational modelling integrated Protenix, Gaussian-mixture scoring, and molecular dynamics simulations to optimize TAM recognition. Integrated Human Practices incorporated expert and stakeholder feedback to improve functionality and biosafety. A self-targeting biocontainment circuit reduced bacterial survival by up to five orders of magnitude. Core parts include ISHahl1 TnpB (BBa_26F4MI62), programmable reRNA, inactive TnpB variants, and base-editing modules, establishing a versatile miniature genome-engineering platform.


## Infectious Diseases (13)

### Aachen — Adesonica
*DEU · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6065) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/aachen)

Antibiotic-resistant bacteria are a leading global health crisis. To address this, we propose a novel antibiotic strategy.

We designed a delivery system to transport universal cargo into Gram-negative bacteria. Tobacco-mosaic-virus (TMV) coat proteins functionalized with the cell-penetrating peptide TAT were reassembled in vitro and encapsulated RNA.

Our antibacterial mechanism targets the dynamic regulation of bacterial DNA methylation. We hypothesize that interfering with methyltransferase expression could disrupt the timing information in methylation patterns during replication. To prove our concept, we developed the Python package adesomics that uses transcriptomic data to predict growth rates, and an ML model that predicts the transcriptome from the methylome to reduce measurement costs. To automate TMV production, we engineered the bioreactor system M.A.R.M.A.L.A.D.E. (Modular Automated Reactor for Mixing And virus-Like-particle Assembly for DElivering cargo) and built software to control it. Bacteria produce coat protein subunits in the first part, and assemble TMV in the second.

### Boston-BOSLab — Epidermighty: One Mighty Commensal against Tick-Borne Diseases
*USA · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6274) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/boston-boslab)

Tick-borne diseases, including Lyme disease, babesiosis, anaplasmosis, and Powassan virus disease, pose a growing concern as cases rise and new pathogens emerge. Epidermighty developed two approaches using the skin bacterium Staphylococcus epidermidis: an adaptable topical vaccine displaying nonfunctional antigens to target current and emerging pathogens, and protein binders intended to inhibit Dae2, an antimicrobial tick protein. After building vaccine plasmids, we paused this approach for future work and focused on Dae2. We hypothesized Dae2 inhibition could affect tick survival and reduce transmission of multiple diseases; this remains untested. We computationally generated 90 candidate designs and tested five in bacterial and cell-free two-hybrid assays. Results guided improvements, but binder–Dae2 interaction remains unconfirmed. Interviews with 16 stakeholders across patient, clinical, laboratory, environmental, bioethics, and commercial perspectives informed project design and safety. Epidermighty is early-stage research; its safety and effectiveness have not been established.

### Hopkins — Oralis
*USA · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6281) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/hopkins)

Oralis is a needle-free, oral mucosal vaccine that delivers antigen to the junctional epithelium via a floss-like device, inducing localized immune activation at the site of pathogen entry. The platform pairs an engineered bacterial chassis with virus-like particles displayed through SpyTag/SpyCatcher conjugation. This modularity allows antigens to be swapped without redesigning the delivery system, enabling rapid extension to other norovirus strains and mucosal pathogens. Oralis' room-temperature stability removes cold-chain dependence, and self-administration removes the need for trained personnel, thus positioning it as a new class of stable, accessible vaccines that protect at the point of entry.

### IISc-Bengaluru — MOSKATEER
*IND · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6506) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/iisc-bengaluru)

Malaria continues to impose a disproportionate burden on communities across India and Sub-Saharan Africa, and the rapid spread of insecticide resistance threatens the vector control strategies on which these regions depend. CRISPR-based gene drives offer a transformative approach to mosquito suppression, yet ecological irreversibility, regulatory uncertainty and limited public acceptance remain substantial barriers. MOSKATEER offers a paratransgenic strategy that circumvents these limitations. We engineer symbiotic bacteria to express double-stranded RNA targeting mosGILT, a gene required for Anopheles fertility. A fusion protein comprising tandem MS2 bacteriophage coat protein repeats, a (GGGGS)₄ flexible linker and a functional peptide module packages the dsRNA for stable delivery and RNAi-mediated gene silencing upon ingestion. The platform supports both horizontal spread among adults and vertical inheritance through progeny, amplifying suppression across generations without permanent genomic alteration. Bacteria are immobilised in a sugar matrix baited with acetoin, producing a low-cost, field-deployable sugar trap for resource-limited settings.

### NCKU-Tainan — Capture: A Dual-Action Biosensor and Inhibitor for Pseudomonas aeruginosa
*TWN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6379) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/ncku-tainan)

Pseudomonas aeruginosa is one of the  leading causes of healthcare-associated infections on ventilators, catheters, and open wounds. Severe ICU infections carry 20% to 40% mortality, and comes with sever antibiotic resistance. Clinical treatments often fail because long time of designating the pathogen, imprecise treatment, which gives bacteria time to form biofilm and secret toxin via AHL.

In Project Capture, we engineered an Escherichia coli platform targeting AHL through two systems. First, our biosensor detects AHL when Pseudomonas aeruginosa starts to expand, generating a visible fluorescent readout for rapid, low-cost screening. Secondly, our inhibition system utilize the retron system to produces ssDNA aptamers that specifically bind the AHL of Pseudomonas aeruginosa. By intercepting bacterial communication before biofilms mature, Capture can inhibit the swarming, pyocyanin, and most importantly, biofilm formation, of Pseudomonas aeruginosa. Combining the two system, we hope Capture can provide a new solution to support the medical system.

### Ohio State — SprayAway: Redoseable Extracellular Vesicle (EV) Gene Therapy to move towards an HIV Cure
*USA · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6515) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/ohio-state)

HIV (Human Immunodeficiency Virus) persists in infected individuals despite antiretroviral therapy (ART) through latent integration into long-lived cellular reservoirs. Eliminating this reservoir is the primary barrier to functional cure. Current gene therapy approaches suffer from immunogenicity constraints that limit redosing, low target specificity, and gene cargo constraints.



SprayAway addresses these limitations through a redosable reservoir depletion strategy combining extracellular vesicle (EV) delivery technology with novel eukaryotic-origin CRISPR systems completely unknown to the immune system. 



The EV payload consists of a plasmid encoding a nuclease and gRNAs targeting three ultraconserved positions in the HIV long terminal repeat (LTR) that were identified via Shannon entropy. EV's will display engineered surface proteins for high tropism to CD4+, the same proteins HIV uses, and endosomal escape mechanisms for high transduction. Experimental validation will use a J-Lat disease model to demonstrate HIV silencing.

### PeptiForge-UDEM — *(no title)*
*MEX · None · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6545)

*(no abstract published)*

### SUSTechOcean — The biosynthesis of lactosylarchaeol-based vaccine adjuvant ingredient in E. coli
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6314) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/sustechocean)

Infectious diseases still threaten millions of families, yet many vaccines require cold-chain storage that is hard to maintain worldwide. Sulfated lactosyl archaeol (SLA) is a promising thermostable adjuvant that strongly boosts immune responses, but current production depends on slow-growing archaea and complex chemical steps.

Our project, SUSTech-OCEAN, engineers Escherichia coli to biosynthesize the lactosyl-archaeol core of SLA. We establish an archaeol chassis and drive glycosylation primarily with MTH_884 from Methanothermobacter thermautotrophicus—the first successful expression and purification of this enzyme in truncated form. A light-controlled module selectively removes host phospholipids while preserving the ether-linked product. Wet-lab experiments are combined with computational enzyme mining and modeling to optimize the pathway.

SUSTech-OCEAN aims to create a scalable, room-temperature-stable vaccine adjuvant and a modular platform for producing other archaeal lipids.

### UChicago — GENELOCK: a new antibiotic mechanism based on programmable DNA sequestration
*USA · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6120) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/uchicago)

Antimicrobial resistance is an emerging threat to public health, motivating the search for novel bacterial inhibition mechanisms. Since recent work demonstrated that synthetic polymers can sequester bacterial DNA through phase separation, inhibiting transcription and inducing cell death, we investigated genetically encodable protein condensates as a programmable alternative. We computationally designed a modular condensate-forming protein with high-affinity DNA binding domain, intrinsically disordered region to drive condensation, and oligomerization domain to increase valency. To determine whether DNA sequestration interfered with gene expression, we used an in vivo ParB–parS reporter to assess localized DNA sequestration and transcriptional repression. Finally, we quantified bacterial growth and viability using optical density measurements and colony-forming unit assays to evaluate antimicrobial activity and proved protein expression via GFP-tagged protein. Together, these experiments investigated whether programmable protein condensates could compact bacterial DNA, inhibit transcription, and provide a novel antimicrobial strategy.

### UiOslo-Norway — HePatch
*NOR · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6422) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/uioslo-norway)

We present HePatch, a synthetic biology project aimed at engineering thermostable variants of the hepatitis B surface antigen (HBsAg) and integrating them into dissolvable microneedle patch systems for cold-chain-free vaccine delivery. Our strategy combines rational computational protein engineering, site directed experimental mutagenesis, and advanced biomaterial formulation to produce vaccines stable at ambient temperatures exceeding 37–45°C for 28 days or longer.

### Vironics AI — *(no title)*
*PAK · None · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6549)

*(no abstract published)*

### Waseda-Tokyo — SUNRISE: A New Dawn in the Fight Against Antimicrobial Resistance
*JPN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6144) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/waseda-tokyo)

Antimicrobial resistance (AMR) is one of the most urgent public health challenges facing modern medicine. As resistance spreads, effective antibiotic options continue to narrow, leaving patients with fewer treatment options. To overcome this challenge, we propose SUNRISE (Safety-enhanced UNconventional Resensitization through Injection and Selective Elimination), a system designed to restore susceptibility to existing antibiotics. This system combines a Cargo Protein Injection (CPI) module and a Targeted Protein Degradation (TPD) module. The protein-based Injector introduces the Adaptor directly into the target bacterium. Once inside the cell, the Adaptor binds to the antibiotic resistance protein through its Binder and recruits the endogenous ClpXP protease complex for degradation. Thus, this system aims to reduce resistance protein levels and restore susceptibility to existing antibiotics. By independently redesigning the Adaptor's Binder and Injector's tail fiber, SUNRISE can be rapidly adapted to diverse resistance proteins and bacteria, bringing a new dawn to the fight against AMR.

### Waterloo — Biofilm Busters: Targeting Pathogenic Gut Biofilms Through Curli Inhibition
*CAN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6251) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/waterloo)

Pathogenic gut biofilms can contribute to persistent infections by protecting bacteria from antibiotics and immune defences. In many Enterobacteriaceae, curli amyloid fibres formed by CsgA and CsgB provide structural support. This project explores an engineered biotherapeutic that secretes CsgC, a natural amyloid chaperone, to inhibit curli formation without directly killing bacteria.



The initial construct uses an N-terminal YebF carrier to support CsgC secretion in E. coli DH5α. The proposed platform uses Lactococcus lactis with inducible expression and biological containment. Bioinformatics surveyed curli genes in the human gut to identify potentially affected taxa, while mathematical modelling supported investigation of biofilm formation and treatment strategies.



Human Practices helped assess clinical relevance, safety, host selection, and practical implementation through stakeholder engagement. By considering patient needs and effects on the gut microbiome alongside technical feasibility, the project aims to develop a targeted approach that could complement existing treatments while limiting disruption to beneficial bacteria.


## Oncology (18)

### Bist-China — PyrLac Glow: A dual-biomarker platform for early cancer screening.
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6263) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/bist-china)

Many cancers alter the body’s metabolism, changing the levels of lactate and pyruvate in the blood. Current screening methods can be invasive, expensive, or lack sensitivity. Our project aims to develop fluorescent probes that detect blood lactate and pyruvate levels quickly and accurately. By measuring the ratio of these two metabolites, we hope to provide a simple, low-cost blood test for early cancer screening. This approach could offer a non-invasive, accessible tool to flag individuals who need further diagnostic follow-up.

### BNU-China — CABOMB: Calcium-based Acid-responsive Biodegradable Osteosarcoma-targeted MNAzyme Biocarrier
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6354) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/bnu-china)

Osteosarcoma is an aggressive primary malignant bone tumor prone to metastasis that predominantly affects children and adolescents. Current treatments, mainly surgery combined with chemotherapy, offer limited selectivity, and the heterogeneity of osteosarcoma makes tumor cells difficult to target, leaving the prognosis of advanced patients unsatisfactory. To address these challenges, we propose CABOMB, a targeted therapeutic strategy integrating a delivery module and an effector module, in which nanoscale calcium carbonate serves as the delivery carrier and a multicomponent nucleic acid enzyme (MNAzyme) functions as the effector, enabling tumor-targeted delivery and selective killing. By integrating molecular recognition, conditional activation, and targeted delivery, CABOMB enables multi-site intervention, holds potential for point-mutation discrimination, and can be extended to other cancers. This innovative design is expected to break through the selectivity bottleneck of current treatments, expand the boundaries of cancer therapy, and provide a new platform for tumor-targeted therapy.

### Caltech — Cervixa: A homologous recombination-mediated biosensor for cervical cancer
*USA · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6211) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/caltech)

Cervical cancer is the leading cause of cancer-related deaths among women in over 37 countries. Paradoxically, the World Health Organization estimates that 99% of these deaths are preventable by early screening. Traditional methods, such as Pap smears, carry drawbacks: Human Papillomavirus (HPV) infection, the cause of nearly all cervical cancers, may incubate for over 15 years before Pap-smear detection is feasible. We address this gap with a homologous-recombination-based biosensor that detects HPV DNA in menstrual fluid, permitting an at-home, self-administered screen free from the sociopolitical barriers of current exams. Further, our biosensor detects infection years before tumorigenic cells appear. We also present a rate-kinetic model of the homologous-recombination-mediated genetic circuit producing our readout. Because the model is built from ordinary differential equations, it may be integrated with other genetic components to form sequence-specific DNA logic gates. In summary, we present a biosensor with socioeconomic and biomedical advantages over traditional exams.

### CSW-UD — Engineering Barley Stripe Mosaic Virus-Like Particles as Drug Delivery Vectors for Triple Negative Breast Cancer
*USA · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6186) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/csw-ud)

The CSW-UD iGEM team addresses a gap in specific and effective treatments for Triple-Negative Breast Cancer (TNBC) by engineering a novel rod-shaped plant Virus-Like Particle (VLP) platform, based on the Barley Stripe Mosaic Virus (BSMV), into a biocompatible drug carrier to improve the specificity of Doxorubicin, a cancer drug known as the “red devil,” and reduce toxicity. 



To address this, we modified and engineered the inner channel of BSMV using cysteine mutations to conjugate Doxorubicin with a thiol-Michael reaction, preventing it from indiscriminately attacking healthy cells throughout the body. We further modulated BSMV’s surface to display ligand receptors and endosomal escape peptides to specifically target TNBC and trigger intracellular drug release. We successfully formed a BSMV rod therapy presented with ligands and conjugated with Doxorubicin. Through these efforts, we took meaningful steps towards building a novel drug delivery platform to address the challenges and gaps of current TNBC treatment options.

### ECNU-China — PREVAIL: Pre-Existing Viral Immunity–Enabled Universal Bacterial Tumor Vaccine
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6380) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/ecnu-china)

PREVAIL (Pre-Existing Viral Immunity–Enabled Universal Bacterial Tumor Vaccine) is a broad-spectrum bacterial vaccine platform that leverages the widespread pre‑existing immunity against SARS‑CoV‑2 (from prior infection or vaccination) in the human population to activate antitumor responses. The vaccine uses engineered bacteria as carriers to present SARS‑CoV‑2 epitopes within the tumor microenvironment, thereby “hijacking” established COVID‑19‑specific memory T cells to rapidly elicit potent and durable antitumor immunity. It also overcomes inter‑individual immune heterogeneity and enables universal application across diverse tumor types.

### HBUT-China — Intelligent Genetically engineered Neo-IL-2-armed oHSV-2 for Tumor microenvironment remodeling and Enhancement of antitumor immunity
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6330) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/hbut-china)

MSS-type colorectal cancer (CRC) is a classic "cold tumor": most immunotherapies fail in this population, and systemic treatments such as chemotherapy carry severe side effects. To address this unmet need, we engineered oHSV2-Neo-IL-2 — an oncolytic herpesvirus type 2 armed with an AI-designed attenuated interleukin-2 variant. Knockout of ICP34.5 and ICP47 confers tumor-specific replication and restores MHC-I antigen presentation. Neo-IL-2 retains CD122/CD132 binding while losing CD25 affinity, activating effector T cells without expanding immunosuppressive Tregs. The project was guided by our HOPES human-practices framework, which integrates patient interviews with clinical-translation planning, and the SHIELD safety framework, spanning molecular design to cross-team biosafety resources. Combining computational screening with in vitro and in vivo validation, we aim to deliver a tolerable, effective therapy for MSS CRC patients who currently have limited treatment options.

### HIT-SLS — GLYCO‑TAM：Glycosylation‑engineered Tumor‑Associated Macrophage for Universal Cancer Immunosensitization
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6499) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/hit-sls)

Despite advances in tumor immunotherapy, macrophage-driven immunosuppression and tumor immune resistance greatly limit treatment outcomes for solid tumors. Conventional immunotherapies partially suppress tumor growth yet lack universality, stable phenotypes and clinical accessibility. Our GLYCO-TAM project develops a synthetic biology strategy to remodel glycosylation of tumor-associated macrophages (TAMs). After seven DBTL cycles, MGAT5 was confirmed as the key target to construct anti-tumor macrophage phenotypes, with broad efficacy verified in liver and colorectal cancer models. We optimized an LNP-mRNA delivery system to reprogram TAMs toward the M1 phenotype and boost anti-PD-1 effects in mice. Combined with policy, safety, ethics research and bilingual science outreach, this work proposes a scalable approach to reverse tumor immunosuppression. Validated only in cell and animal models without human trials, our strategy represents a promising alternative to conventional tumor immunotherapy.

### HKPOLYU — Engineered Lactobacillus as a Novel Targeted Delivery and Therapeutic Agent for Colorectal Cancer
*HKG · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6226) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/hkpolyu)

Our project developed a living, locally active oral biotherapeutic platform for treating colorectal cancer. Rather than infusing cytotoxic agents throughout the entire body, our system leverages an engineered, food-grade probiotic strain Lactobacillus casei ATCC 393 to produce and secrete the human cytokine IL-37. By modifying the bacterial surface with a cadherin-targeting peptide, it specifically targets colorectal cancer cells while sparing healthy tissue. Delivering therapeutics locally within the gut lumen avoids the widespread off-target tissue destruction characteristic of systemic chemotherapy, and probiotic-based delivery systems offer an inherently scalable, low-cost chassis that could eventually transition from clinic-bound infusions to accessible, shelf-stable oral formulations. Beyond delivering an engineered strain, our project provides the iGEM community with documented protein engineering protocols, functional assays, characterizations of failed constructs, and design rationales for both bacterial expression and chemical surface modification.

### IZJU-China — Bifidobacterium longum elicits anti‑tumor immunity in pancreatic cancer
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6113) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/izju-china)

Impaired antigen presentation can help pancreatic cancer cells evade immune recognition. X-BAT addresses this problem through an engineered Bifidobacterium longum platform designed to display Galectin-8 (Gal8) on its surface and promote xenophagy—the selective autophagic degradation of intracellular bacteria. By enhancing bacterial processing, we aim to increase the presentation of bacterial antigens by tumour cells and support immune recognition. The design brings together tumour targeting, cellular entry, immune activation and safety control, with bacterial-antigen vaccination proposed as a complementary strategy.

### JLU-FBH — CirCure: A Multi-Epitope Circular RNA Vaccine Platform for Triple-Negative Breast Cancer
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6056) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/jlu-fbh)

Triple-negative breast cancer (TNBC) is hard to treat: its tumors are heterogeneous, recur often, and escape single-target therapies. CirCure is a modular circular RNA (circRNA) vaccine platform that broadens immune recognition beyond a single TNBC-associated antigen. A computational pipeline screens antigen fragments, or epitopes, for MHC-I binding, immunogenicity, and structural compatibility, prioritizing candidates with strong potential to activate CD8+ "killer" T cells. The selected epitopes are encoded into a single engineered circRNA. Inside dendritic cells, the circRNA acts as a durable instruction set, supporting antigen production and presentation so CD8+ T cells can recognize and attack tumor cells with different antigen profiles. We experimentally validate circRNA circularization, antigen expression, dendritic cell maturation, and T-cell-mediated tumor cell killing. Input from clinicians and other stakeholders guides our iterative design. CirCure offers a scalable, "plug-and-play" framework for RNA vaccines against cancers in which tumor diversity limits single-target treatments.

### NIS Almaty-Medeu — MiRVOX: Two Approaches, One Oncological Challenge
*KAZ · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6523) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/nis-almaty-medeu)

Lung cancer caused approximately 1.8 million deaths worldwide in 2022. Early detection remains limited by diagnostic uncertainty, invasive tissue sampling, and difficulties detecting low-abundance biomarkers.

MiRVOX investigates two approaches to lung cancer sensing: Pulmora, a synthetic biology platform for microRNA detection, and OncoNose, an electronic-nose system for breath analysis. Pulmora proposes a five-miRNA panel (miR-21-5p, miR-155-5p, miR-223, let-7c-5p, and miR-486-5p) to investigate NSCLC-associated molecular signatures. Its design combines conventional and reverse-logic toehold switches to address increased and decreased biomarker expression. Using synthetic reference miRNA, we investigated stem-loop reverse transcription, PCR amplification, RNA-trigger generation, and cell-free fluorescent reporting.

OncoNose integrates metal-oxide gas sensors with computational analysis to investigate volatile organic compound patterns in exhaled breath. Together, these approaches investigate programmable molecular detection and accessible, non-invasive breath sensing. Both remain research-stage technologies requiring further experimental and clinical validation.

### Penn State — TRIDENT: Yeast-Derived Exosomes as a Delivery System for Apoptosis-Inducing Plasmid Payloads
*USA · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6315) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/penn-state)

Advanced stage carcinomas are difficult to target for a myriad of reasons, ranging from complications in tumor identification to targetability. TROP-2, a transmembrane glycoprotein, is overexpressed in epithelial cancers with poor prognoses. Furthermore, miRNA34𝛼, a small, non-coding RNA molecule downstream of the tumor suppressor p53 pathway, shows promise as a cancer therapeutic. Our project engineered a novel delivery platform, a miR-34𝛼 payload in S. cerevisiae exosomes coated with anti-TROP-2 nanobodies, to assess the effectiveness of exosome delivery mechanisms in an in-vitro pancreatic cancer model.

### PLKLFC — COLOPRINT: Personalized Bioprinted Probiotic Hydrogels for Colorectal Cancer
*HKG · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6092) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/plklfc)

Colorectal cancer (CRC) treatment faces three major hurdles: antigen heterogeneity, gastrointestinal physiological variation, and prohibitive costs. To address these limitations, we designed COLOPRINT: a non-invasive, localized biological delivery platform that releases therapeutic compounds directly at tumor sites while minimizing systemic side effects.By integrating patient genomic profiling, microenvironment modeling, and 3D bioprinting with engineered probiotics, COLOPRINT processes patient data via custom software to fabricate tailored alginate hydrogel pills. These shield live E. coli Nissle 1917 during gastric transit until the alkaline colon environment triggers polymer deprotonation and swelling. Released bacteria selectively bind tumor cells via an Lpp-OmpA nanobody-display module. A double-criteria biosensor system (sensing hypoxia and elevated formic acid) tightly regulates localized therapeutic payload expression, while an L-arabinose-inducible kill switch ensures strict biosafety, creating an accessible, decentralized personalized oncology platform.

### SEHS — FusionDrive: Engineering and Functional Evaluation of an Anti-TIGIT–IL-2 Variant Fusion Protein for Gastric Cancer Immunotherapy
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6177) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/sehs)

Dietary habits, Helicobacter pylori infection, smoking, and alcohol exposure contribute to Shandong’s high gastric cancer burden. Treatments remain limited by chemoradiotherapy toxicity, low anti-PD-1 response rates, and IL-2-associated systemic toxicity and off-target immunosuppression. We designed FusionDrive, fusing an anti-TIGIT antibody with an engineered IL-2 variant. Across five engineering cycles, we expressed and purified proteins in CHO cells and evaluated target binding and signaling activity. The variant retained TIGIT binding (EC50: 1.155 nM). In a PHA-induced Jurkat model, it reduced pSTAT5 signaling more strongly in CD25⁺ cells than in CD25⁻ cells relative to the wild-type fusion, while retaining CD25⁻ signaling activity, supporting reduced CD25-associated response bias. Human Practices integrated stakeholder feedback into project iteration, while our business plan outlined a translation pathway guided by research milestones and industry collaboration.

### SUDA-ICPI-China — FLAME: An Engineered Bacterial Platform for Precision Colorectal Cancer Therapy
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6428) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/suda-icpi-china)

Colorectal cancer therapy requires effective tumor-cell killing while limiting damage to healthy tissues. FLAME engineers the probiotic Escherichia coli Nissle 1917 to regulate GSDMD-N, a pore-forming fragment of gasdermin D. A regulatory circuit combines hypoxia and lactate sensing with LuxI/LuxR quorum sensing to favor expression under tumor-associated conditions. Reporter experiments showed the strongest response under combined low oxygen and lactate. Comparing untagged, TAT-fused, and SsrA-tagged effectors in CT26 and L929 cells revealed differences in tumor-cell and non-tumor-cell cytotoxicity, supporting the preliminary prioritization of the SsrA-tagged construct based on safety considerations. Mathematical modeling informed promoter selection and identified constraints on selectivity and lesion size. Stakeholder interviews explored acceptance, while education evolved toward age-appropriate participation and dialogue. Together, these results provide an experimental foundation for investigating controlled bacterial effector delivery.

### Thessaloniki — AETh3R: A Targeted, E3 Ligase-Harnessing Therapeutic Approach for Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer
*GRC · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6118) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/thessaloniki)

AETh3R is a novel peptide-PROTAC (PROteolysis TArgeting Chimera)-based strategy for the treatment and management of Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer (NSCLC), the most common type of lung cancer, challenged by treatment resistance and relapse. Our project targets two key oncogenic drivers: mutant KRAS, which promotes uncontrolled cell proliferation, and MDM2, a negative regulator of the tumor suppressor p53. We aim to degrade both proteins simultaneously, suppressing oncogenic signaling while restoring p53-mediated tumor suppression and apoptosis. This approach employs two heterobifunctional peptide-PROTACs, each comprising a target-binding peptide, a linker, and a von Hippel–Lindau (VHL) E3 ubiquitin ligase-recruiting ligand, triggering target ubiquitination and proteasomal degradation. A targeted lipid nanoparticle delivery system with an optimized, DOTAP-containing formulation favors lung biodistribution, while incorporated anti-Integrin αvβ6 antibody fragments enhance tumor cell-specific uptake. Through computational modeling and experimental validation, AETh3R aims to improve therapeutic targeting and offer a promising, less resistance-prone alternative to conventional inhibition-based therapies.

### UNC-Chapel Hill — CRYPTIC: Colorectal Reinforcement through a Probiotic Therapeutic for Intestinal Cancer
*USA · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6277) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/unc-chapel-hill)

Colorectal cancer (CRC) is the third leading cause of cancer, and its prevalence in young adults has soared since 1990. Probiotics are widely used to enhance normal gut health. We propose an engineered probiotic therapeutic using E. coli Nissle 1917 (EcN), which localizes to hypoxic cancerous environments in the gut, to intervene during CRC development. Our hypothesis is that shifting tryptophan metabolism from the proliferative, immunosuppressive kynurenine pathway (KP) to the protective indole pathway (IP) can reduce the immunosuppressive conditions that drive healthy colon cells toward cancer. To accomplish this, we engineered EcN to convert tryptophan into indole-3-acetic acid (IAA) and indole-3-propionic acid (IPA), along with expressing enzyme KynU to break down kynurenine, the major tumorigenic KP metabolite. This work lays the foundation for a CRC-specific preventative probiotic that can be used alongside current treatments. Beyond the lab, we provide preventative measures to reduce CRC risk and promote STEM accessibility.

### Zhulu-China — Acid-responsive self-assembling RGD-ES/KS peptides for tumor Invasion suppression under weak acid conditions
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6288) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/zhulu-china)

Lung cancer remains the leading cause of cancer death. We engineered a genetically encoded peptide that recognizes the tumor surface and assembles into a physical barrier specifically in the mildly acidic tumor microenvironment (pH ≈ 6.0) by fusing an RGD targeting motif to a pH-responsive polypeptide (ES) scaffold.



Through five DBTL cycles, we prioritized the RGD-6ES construct. It demonstrated storage stability, significantly reduced A549 cell viability and migration, reversed EMT markers, and suppressed YAP signaling, while remaining safe to normal bronchial epithelial cells at 0.5 mg/mL.



Our Integrated Human Practices refined this trajectory, narrowing the application to NSCLC surgical margins and establishing a preclinical safety package. Addressing dual-use AI risks, we developed PRISM, a locally deployed screening layer. This work delivers a modular, pH-selective platform for tumor microenvironment intervention, with barrier imaging and in vivo validation as next steps.


## Software & AI (15)

### Al-Nahrain-IRAQ — SYNOVA: AI-Guided Prediction of Yeast Fermentation for Bioethanol from Iraqi Date Palm Waste
*IRQ · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6520) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/al-nahrain-iraq)

SYNOVA (Synthetic Yeast Next-generation Organism Valorization Application) is a software platform, four apps in all, that helps a team decide what to test for bioethanol production by Saccharomyces cerevisiae from Iraqi date waste, a sugar-rich residue now mostly discarded. Instead of trial and error, SYNOVA combines a kinetic model of one batch fermentation (Engine 1, not yet shown to predict real batches), a ranking of genes that changed in RNA-seq experiments (Engine 2), strain comparison across 17 published conditions, and tools for a lab's own measurements (Lab Data). The wet lab used a species-specific PCR workflow to identify local yeast isolates; RT-qPCR of shortlisted genes is the next step.
