We ran workshops for children, students and the public, and consulted agricultural, business and AI specialists, whose feedback changed what we built.SYNOVA: Helping Choose Strains and Conditions to Test for Bioethanol from Iraqi Date Waste

### BostonU — Cirrus: Creation and Optimization of Cloud Laboratories for Synbio
*USA · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6432) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/bostonu)

Many synthetic biology researchers could benefit from a high-throughput, remotely accessible cloud lab. However, there is no standard or organized method for integrating cloud accessibility into the synthetic biology lab space. We created Cirrus to support both new and existing labs in their transition to optimized cloud labs. Users specify a target and maximum budget, identify the equipment they own, define their laboratory space within an interactive sandbox, select the protocols they wish to run, and indicate the weekly frequency of each protocol. Users may also prioritize either maintaining proximity between related equipment or minimizing walking distance. From these inputs, Cirrus generates a build-ready cloud lab blueprint. Each custom plan comprises a curated bill of materials, standardized protocols, an optimized floor plan, and a cloud lab interface for workflow management. Implementing Cirrus will allow your lab to become a node in a network of remotely accessible cloud labs.

### ChronoSplice — *(no title)*
*SWE · None · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6548)

*(no abstract published)*

### Denmark-GA — ECLIPSE - Evaporatively Cooled LOCKR Integration for Peptide Screening & Evaluation
*USA · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6112) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/denmark-ga)

Designing a functional peptide binder for LOCKR, a programmable protein-switch platform, requires extensive GPU usage and months of wet-lab experimentation, since most design software predicts candidates without a level of accuracy needed for evaluating functionality. Malaria illustrates why this limitation matters. Current diagnostic tests target a gene subject to deletion, resulting in 2 in 10 cases going undiagnosed. This is a deficiency the WHO has formally acknowledged, and closing that gap requires a faster, more reliable design pipeline. Our tool, LATTE, meets this need by analyzing binders for LOCKR, through charge scanning and thermodynamic modelling. Our scoring function, HybridDock-Pep complements it, rapidly evaluating protein-peptide binding affinities and selectivity. These tools produce ECLIPSE, a PfLDH-based malaria diagnostic, demonstrating a scalable, computation-first approach to protein diagnostic design, applicable wherever speed and accuracy determine outcomes in many diseases.

### DKU — PeptiCraft: AI-Powered Multifunctional Fusion Protein Design Platform for Skincare and Medical Aesthetics
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6064) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/dku)

Skincare and medical aesthetics present needs for multifunctional biomaterials. We built PeptiCraft, an AI-powered web platform that proposes recombinant fusion proteins based on users’ chosen product formats and functions, together with recommended expression strategies. Refined over iterations, the pipeline screens 849 million public protein sequences through 9 filtering rounds, with safety applied as a hard veto. Negative controls help assess screening performance. The deployed platform supports screening within a 20.2-million-peptide search space and provides 496 precomputed candidate constructs across four functional directions. As a proof of concept, we tested an antioxidant silk-fibroin construct for a topical moisturizer. Preliminary antioxidant and cell-based assays supported initial feasibility and informed model improvement. Our Human Practices work explored societal needs and ethical questions around beauty and AI through outreach and communication. Together, these efforts support computational design and early-stage evaluation of multifunctional protein biomaterials.

### EdgeSci-THU-China — (E)vidence-driven (Co)-administration (P)redictive Analysis for (I)nvestigational New Drugs
*CHN · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6433) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/edgesci-thu-china)

ECoPI is an AI-Agent system for evidence-driven predictive analysis of co-administration risks involving investigational new drugs. By coordinating specialized agents for pharmacokinetics, pharmacodynamics, toxicology, and patient education, ECoPI transforms biomedical evidence into interpretable mechanism–effect–risk chains. The system provides early warnings for researchers and accessible safety explanations for patients.

### HelioSynth Research — *(no title)*
*IND · None · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6547)

*(no abstract published)*

### NEBS-VCA — Mechanism-Guided PETase Engineering for Environment-Aware Polyester Recycling
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6420) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/nebs-vca)

Polyethylene terephthalate (PET) persists in waste streams because enzymatic breakdown is limited by polymer accessibility and catalytic performance. We investigated FAST-PETase using two complementary engineering routes: structure-aware mutation discovery and active-site-focused design. PET-film assays identified Y87Q and S187Y as beneficial variants, while molecular dynamics, pre-reaction-state analysis, and quantum chemistry helped explain their different effects on catalytic geometry and reaction energetics.

Building on these results, we present an environment-aware design strategy informed by EnzOracle, a framework for predicting enzyme melting temperature, optimal catalytic temperature, and optimal pH. Studies of photoprotective PET-based copolyesters and chemically recyclable polyethylene-like polyesters provide material-design context and suggest future enzyme–substrate compatibility experiments. These extensions remain to be directly validated.

Our project highlights how experimental evidence and mechanistic interpretation can guide more reproducible PET hydrolase engineering.

### NJU-China — Starase Atlas
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6337) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/nju-china)

Discovering enzymes for specific reactions remains challenging, as protein sequence space is vast and known enzyme–reaction relationships are incomplete. To address this, NJU-China 2026 developed ATLAS, integrating COMPASS, a reaction-guided AI agent; BRIDGE, a protein retrieval model; and EDGE, a graph-based knowledge base of enzyme–reaction relationships. From a user-defined reaction, COMPASS uses BRIDGE to search broad protein space, apply taxonomic constraints, and combine functional, structural, mechanistic, and enzyme-family evidence to prioritize candidates, while EDGE stores and visualizes validated knowledge. To evaluate ATLAS, we applied it to the conversion of farnesyl diphosphate to β-farnesene. The top-ranked candidate, UniProt protein M5AW86, previously unannotated with this activity, was experimentally confirmed to catalyze the reaction, revealing a new enzyme function. Through iterative prediction, validation, and inclusion, ATLAS progressively expands the metabolic knowledge network and enables interpretable enzyme discovery for synthetic biology.

### SJTU-Software — Owl-Eyezyme
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6375) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/sjtu-software)

Finding natural enzymes for target reactions is challenging because databases and prediction tools are fragmented, and results are difficult to reproduce. Owl-Eyezyme is a multi-agent virtual laboratory that transforms a reaction description into a ranked, traceable shortlist of enzyme candidates. Large language models coordinate a principal investigator, four domain specialists, a scientific critic, and a reporter to plan tasks, operate bioinformatics tools, evaluate evidence, and summarize findings. The platform integrates reaction classification, protein retrieval, structural analysis, and enzyme–reaction matching through flexible research workflows and reproducible fixed pipelines. Staged execution and checkpoints support limited computing resources, while recorded inputs, intermediate results, failures, and uncertainties preserve the evidence chain. Owl-Eyezyme supports candidate selection and experimental planning, helping researchers move from chemical targets toward laboratory validation.

### Tsinghua-A — mRNA Vaccines for Feline Immunocastration: An AI-Driven Platform from Sequence to Application
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6271) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/tsinghua-a)

mRNA technologies offer programmable biological expression, but sequence performance remains difficult to optimize. Existing RNA language models and translation predictors can score candidates, yet many do not generate and iteratively refine host-adapted designs, while cross-species transfer remains uncertain. Using feline GnRH immunocontraception as a case study, we developed an AI-assisted platform for mRNA optimization.

For 5′UTRs, we implemented an ERNIE-RNA-guided hybrid genetic algorithm and an Optimus-based directed-mutation route. For CDSs, our exploration progressed from a conditional VAE, through an interpretable multi-objective genetic algorithm, to a DiT-based structure-aware Refiner that preserves proteins while learning patterns from natural sequences. We integrated these methods into RNA Optimization Studio, a local CPU application supporting validation, five workflows, whole-construct assembly, ranking, visualization, and export.

Representative UTR runs increased mean predicted MRL by 33.1% and 42.8%. Reporter measurements revealed both useful signals and remaining uncertainty, establishing a foundation for future wet-lab-guided optimization.

### USP-Brazil — S(H)ARP - Streptomyces Hidden Antibiotic Regulated Pathways
*BRA · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6333) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/usp-brazil)

The global dissemination of multidrug-resistant pathogens listed by the World Health Organization poses a major public health challenge and highlights the need for investment in the research and development of new therapies. Actinobacteria are widely recognized for their ability to produce secondary metabolites; however, most of their Biosynthetic Gene Clusters remain silent under laboratory conditions. To accelerate the discovery of novel antibiotics, we aim to integrate synthetic biology and bioinformatics approaches to uncover new pathways for the production of bioactive compounds. Our project is structured around three complementary pillars. First, we are developing a bioinformatics tool for the semi-automated identification of novel BGCs in actinobacterial genomes. Second, we are constructing a modular Golden Gate-compatible cloning system for Streptomyces spp., to be used for the first time in iGEM. Finally, through our Human Practices initiative, we will engage the public in the search for novel actinobacteria across different regions of Brazil.

### XJTLU-Software — AMPilot
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6219) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/xjtlu-software)

AMPilot is an AI-powered research workbench that helps users develop antimicrobial peptides from an initial idea to experimental planning. Users describe their goals and requirements in natural language, and AMPilot searches relevant literature, generates candidate sequences, and evaluates their properties, predicted activity, and potential safety concerns. It helps researchers compare candidates, explore formulation and production options, and prepare editable reports and experimental handoff materials. Throughout the workflow, evidence sources and prediction limitations remain visible, while key decisions stay with the researcher. By bringing these tasks into one workspace, AMPilot helps users organize complex research and prepare for laboratory validation.


## Space (17)

### AEI Prep-Taiwan — synPETic
*TWN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6200) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/aei-prep-taiwan)

Plastic waste is a growing burden on manned spacecraft. The ISS, for example, produces about 2,500 kg of plastic waste per year, and resupply costs $10,000–$20,000 per kilogram. Additionally, traditional industrial PET recycling methods require temperatures of 240–285 °C and use metal catalysts, neither of which are currently feasible in space. SynPETic engineers a PETosome, a scaffold-based two-enzyme system produced by engineered E. coli, to depolymerize PET at 37–50 °C. We expressed and purified three functional proteins and measured PET film depolymerization by HPLC. Our human practices include expert consultations and a community survey on attitudes toward GMO-based recycling. For entrepreneurship, we have outlined a pre-commercial pathway serving space agencies, commercial station operators, and remote Earth sites, monetized through development contracts, pilot services, and licensing. Our process is compatible with biological repolymerization. Our unique contributions include a new composite part and reusable measurement protocols.

### AFMU-China — NEXO: Engineered Exosome-Producing Microneedle Patches for Microgravity-Induced Muscle Atrophy
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6216) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/afmu-china)

Long-duration spaceflight poses challenges for maintaining skeletal muscle and supplying repeated treatments. NEXO explores a living-cell microneedle platform for sustained delivery of engineered exosomes. We engineered HEK-293T cells with modules for SMAD7 mRNA loading, skeletal-muscle targeting, and enhanced exosome production, and encapsulated them in layered GelMA microneedles. Under simulated microgravity, experiments supported increased exosome yield and sustained encapsulated-cell viability; co-culture studies provided initial evidence of reduced myotube atrophy. Models investigated nutrient supply, secretion dynamics, molecular interactions, and tissue exposure to inform design and further validation. Our modular Registry collection and interactive modeling resources support adaptation by other teams. Stakeholder engagement shaped usability, safety, and research-ethics priorities, while education activities developed reusable resources through participant feedback. NEXO remains an in vitro prototype: in vivo delivery, containment, long-term safety, and performance in actual spaceflight require further investigation.

### Barcelona-UB — Guard’s Up: A temporary, reversible and non-integrative genetic protection against space radiation
*ESP · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6349) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/barcelona-ub)

As humankind prepares to conquer the stars, radiation remains one of the greatest challenges to long-duration human spaceflight. Current protection strategies rely mainly on physical shielding, heavy and limmited against high-energy cosmic radiation. 



Synthetic biology offers an alternative: engineering cells to better withstand radiation-induced DNA damage. The Guard’s Up system combines the tardigrade-derived Damage Suppressor Protein (Dsup) with a DNA targeting system to provide broad DNA protection while reinforcing selected radiation-sensitive genomic regions. 



The system is maintained episomally, without any modification of the genome, and can be switched off and progressively cleared once it isn’t needed simply by taking a doxycycline pill. By combining targeted protection with temporary, non-integrative genetic engineering, Guard’s Up aims to provide an additional layer of cellular radioprotection for safer long-duration human space exploration.

### BGCFullerton — MycoVela: Protecting Mycorrhizal Fungi for Future Space Agriculture
*USA · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6475) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/bgcfullerton)

Arbuscular mycorrhizal fungi (AMF) can improve plant nutrient acquisition and stress tolerance, but oxidative stress associated with lunar regolith may reduce fungal viability and disrupt plant-fungal symbiosis. MycoVela is a project designing a cell-free antioxidant carrier system designed to protect Rhizophagus irregularis spores and hyphae during early exposure to lunar regolith simulant. The formulation uses pyomelanin produced through heterologous HPPD expression in Escherichia coli, which is recovered and incorporated into a protective carrier near AMF propagules. We also designed a novel catalase optimized for activity in regolith. The deliverables can even be applied here on Earth, as improved AMF symbiosis has the potential to increase the yield for many crops. Proposed experiments test the catalase root-zone treatment and compare pyomelanin-containing carriers with ascorbic acid, carrier-only, and stress-only controls while measuring spore viability, hyphal growth, and oxidative-stress response. This work aims to improve AMF resilience in lunar-regolith-based plant growth systems.

### BIT-LIFE — Mars Magnet
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6285) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/bit-life)

Iron monitoring matters in space; detection stays in the lab. BIT-LIFE (iGEM 2026) asked: what if the detector were alive? On E. coli's Fur switch, three tracks close one loop. Trained on 112,955 promoters, a CNN screener (P@20 = 1.00) and CNN–Transformer scorer (R² = 0.5684) narrowed 51,203 candidates to 25. Five designed promoters drove Cycle3GFP across 0–30 µM iron; S3 and S5 fell over 0–10 and 0–15 µM. A sealed pocket detector takes it off the bench.

### BNDS-China — OrbitColi
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6115) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/bnds-china)

During long-term space missions like Artemis, astronauts are exposed to cosmic radiation and microgravity, leading to oxidative stress that conventional antioxidant supplements have limited efficacy due to instability and fixed dosing. We designed OrbitColi, an engineered Escherichia coli probiotic platform that detects and mitigates oxidative stress in the astronauts’ gut. It senses glycochenodeoxycholic acid (GCDCA), a biomarker indicating food intake, and an oxidative-stress-responsive signal, with both inputs integrated through a split T7 RNA polymerase AND gate to ensure responses only occur when both conditions are present. Its activation triggers the production of trehalose, protecting proteins against oxidative damage, and riboflavin (vitamin B2), supporting detoxification of reactive oxygen species. To enhance storage and deployment, the engineered probiotics are designed to be freeze-dried, improving shelf life and stability for on-demand use.

### BUCT — Air2Protein: Turning Martian Air into Programmable Nutrition
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6055) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/buct)

Air2Protein is a modular electro-biocatalytic platform that converts Martian atmospheric resources into microbial protein and functional nutrition precursors. Since the Martian atmosphere is rich in CO₂ and contains exploitable N₂, we first transform CO₂ into a concentrated formate/formic acid feedstock through electrocatalysis, while establishing ammonia as a controlled nitrogen input and evaluating N₂-to-NH₃ conversion as a long-term nitrogen-supply module. These inputs are then integrated with an engineered E. coli chassis carrying the reductive glycine pathway, enabling formate-derived one-carbon units to enter central metabolism and support single-cell protein accumulation. By further redirecting carbon flux toward amino acid biosynthesis and installing a β-alanine branch for L-carnosine production, Air2Protein demonstrates a programmable route from atmospheric gases to both basic biomass and functional nutritional ingredients. Designed for Mars but relevant to Earth, this system proposes a scalable, closed-loop strategy for food production in extreme, resource-limited environments.

### EPFL — OrbitAl
*CHE · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6223) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/epfl)

Every year, hundreds of satellites burn up on re-entry, releasing their metals into the stratosphere. Spacecraft are built largely of aluminium, so re-entry injects aluminium into a layer of the atmosphere that normally receives almost none; pollution that is essentially unmonitored. What matters is not only how much metal comes back down, but the form it takes in the aerosol particles that carry it, since that form decides whether living systems can take it up.

Orbit-AL has two components. The hardware is a balloon-borne sampler that captures stratospheric aerosols and keeps them intact and uncontaminated until they reach the lab. The biology then reads those samples: for aluminium, a protein we designed to fold around Al³⁺, an ion with no natural biological sensor; for iron, engineered Escherichia coli reporters that distinguish ferric (Fe³⁺) from ferrous (Fe²⁺) iron. Mass spectrometry quantifies the same samples, giving the biological signal a chemical reference.

### HAS — AstroFlora: Solutions for Plant Growth in Microgravity
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6176) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/has)

Growing plants in space is essential for controlled ecological life support systems, as plants provide food, oxygen, and purified water, absorb carbon dioxide, improve the environment, and alleviate psychological stress for astronauts, while also laying the groundwork for lunar and Martian settlements. However, microgravity disrupts normal plant growth, partly through excessive accumulation of abscisic acid (ABA) and indole-3-acetic acid (IAA) in roots. Here, we designed a plant probiotic system based on engineered E. coli that reduces this excessive hormonal accumulation in plant roots under microgravity conditions, enabling normal plant growth despite microgravity stress. We also developed a hardware system, Floraspin, that facilitates laboratory simulation of plant growth under microgravity conditions, and verified its effectiveness in promoting Arabidopsis germination.

### HiZJU-China — UNICO-fuel
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6273) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/hizju-china)

UniCO-Fuel aims to address future energy challenges in Mars exploration by developing a light-controlled, sustainable aviation fuel bioproduction system based on in-situ Martian resources. Using abundant atmospheric CO₂ and solar energy on Mars, we builds a green conversion route from CO₂ to methanol and then to epi-Isozizaene (EPI), a high-energy-density aviation fuel. CO₂ is first converted into methanol through photocatalysis, and the methanol is then transformed into EPI by engineered Pichia pastoris. Compared with conventional fuels, EPI offers higher energy density, improved safety, and better adaptability to extreme environments, providing reliable power for future Martian drones, aerial platforms, and even base facilities. To enhance production efficiency, UniCO-Fuel introduces a light-controlled CRISPRi system to dynamically balance cell growth and fuel synthesis, while auxotrophic containment, suicide switches, and radiation-protection strategies ensure stable and controllable operation on Mars.

### KAUST-KSA — SHIHAB
*SAU · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6341) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/kaust-ksa)

SHIHAB is a space biotechnology platform designed to enable on-demand therapeutic production in extraterrestrial environments. The system utilizes a set of robust microbial chassis (Saccharomyces cerevisiae, Pichia pastoris, and Bacillus subtilis) engineered for enhanced resilience to radiation and other stress factors to which cells are exposed in space. These organisms are then modified to synthesize a library of therapeutics and other proteins selected based on the needs of astronauts during long-duration missions. The platform is integrated into a custom-designed bioreactor created for operation under space conditions, enabling reliable, autonomous, and scalable production of essential proteins. SHIHAB aims to reduce dependency on the conventional supply chains and support sustainable human presence beyond Earth.

### LZU-Northwest — Sybio-Gutweaver
*CHN · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6238) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/lzu-northwest)

Sybio-Gutweaver uses Escherichia coli Nissle 1917 (EcN) to explore intestinal radioprotection and repair. We investigated melanin’s biological protective mechanisms and produced DHICA-rich melanin with enhanced free-radical-scavenging activity. We also plan to engineer EcN to secrete L-lactate to support the recovery of damaged cells and the gut microbiota. To support intestinal applications, we designed a controllable colonization module, a lactate concentration monitoring system, and an arabinose-dependent safety system. By bringing antioxidant activity, barrier-recovery support, and bacterial regulation into one design, Sybio-Gutweaver provides a foundation for further synthetic biology research into intestinal radioprotection.

### NMU-China — StellaCista: A Resource-Recycling Co-Culture Platform for Space Nutrition and Flavonoid-Based Wellness
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6370) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/nmu-china)

Far from Earth, supplies dwindle. In The Martian, Mark Watney grows potatoes to survive. His ingenuity sparked our question: could astronauts also grow ingredients to support their health? 

StellaCista explores this possibility through a closed partnership between engineered cyanobacteria and yeast. Cyanobacteria use light to capture cabin CO₂ and supply sucrose to yeast, which uses this carbon and nitrogen recovered from treated wastewater to produce protein-rich biomass. Light-controlled pathways also enable yeast to produce candidate flavonoids for potential support against bone loss, sleep disturbances, and radiation-related damage. Both microbes contribute to nitrogen recovery, while space-adaptation and biosafety strategies support controlled operation. By turning recycled resources into nutritional support and on-demand products, StellaCista moves toward a living pharmacy for deep-space journeys.

### NUS-SPS — Astro-Engineered Guardian of Interface Surfaces (AEGIS)
*SGP · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6474) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/nus-sps)

Astronauts aboard the International Space Station (ISS) rely on the Water Processor Assembly (WPA) to recycle onboard wastewater. However, harmful bacterial colonies often form thick biofilms in hard-to-clean areas of the WPA. Their inevitable dislodgement clogs valves and filters, posing health risks to the crew. Current chemical and mechanical mitigation methods struggle to eradicate the issue.



We propose an alternative solution: using an engineered bacterial strain to out-compete native space strains. Our team will engineer a non-pathogenic strain of Bacillus subtilis and test its ability to competitively exclude biofilm-forming Escherichia coli. To prevent our engineered strain from causing similar clogging, an optogenetic switch will be linked to biofilm-thinning genes, allowing external light to control biofilm growth on demand. We hope that using ecological competition as a biofilm management strategy will be an effective solution on future space missions.

### SZU-SIAT China — Friskoli: Engineering Bacterial Chemotaxis for Degradation under Microgravity
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6100) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/szu-siat-china)

In microgravity, buoyancy-driven convection weakens and diffusion carries most of the local transport, limiting contact between microbes and solid substrates. Friskoli asks whether chemotaxis can close that distance. We engineer E. coli to carry cellulase on its surface and to sense cellobiose, the sugar that cellulose breakdown releases. Cells swim up the gradient and bring their enzymes to the material, using cellulose as our demonstration substrate. So far, we have built six strains with the chb promoter replaced on the chromosome (four of which grow clearly on cellobiose), a chemotaxis model and simulator, the Friskoli-CAD design software, a microfluidic measurement system with AI tracking, and the Space Material Atlas. In our model, visits to the material last about 2.5 times longer; our next experiments will test whether living cells show the same benefit. Crucially, this same cell-and-enzyme system is also suited for processing lignocellulose waste on Earth.

### UFlorida — Computationally Optimized Space Monitoring and Omni-Sensing System
*USA · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6403) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/uflorida)

Long-duration spaceflight poses significant challenges to astronaut health, including accelerated bone and muscle loss, weakened immune function, and heighted physiological stress. Monitoring these responses is essential for maintaining crew health during extended missions. Here, we present a biosensing platform starting from computational design of the synthetic binders all the way to measuring their performance against key biomarkers. Using RFdiffusion 3 and BindCraft, we designed de novo binders targeting osteocalcin, a marker of bone turnover, and PD-L1, a protein involved in immune regulation. Candidate binders were prioritized using AI-based predictions and evaluated through AMBER molecular dynamics to teach our Model what features contribute most to binding affinity, leading to better design. Selected candidates were expressed in Escherichia coli, purified, and assessed experimentally for binding activity. This integrated computational and experimental workflow supports the development of a compact biosensor for space capable of monitoring astronaut physiology with limited laboratory infrastructure.

### Uni-Padua-IT — C.O.S.M.I.C. (Cyanobacterial Oxygen Supply for Martian Inhabitant Complexes)
*ITA · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6151) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/uni-padua-it)

C.O.S.M.I.C. addresses two bottlenecks of crewed Mars missions: oxygen production and perchlorate contamination of water. To tackle both in a single chassis, we chose the cyanobacterium Nostoc sp. PCC 7120, which photosynthetically generates oxygen from ambient CO₂. For perchlorate remediation, we evaluated endogenous nitrate reductase (NarB) activity and designed a heterologous pathway expressing perchlorate reductase (pcrABD) in micro-oxic heterocysts alongside chlorite dismutase (cld). To withstand Martian radiation, we integrated pprA from Deinococcus radiodurans, enhancing double-strand break repair.

Navigating this non-model host, we developed ReTIsigner, an open-source codon-optimization tool for non-standard chassis, and designed an inducible kill switch satisfying planetary protection guidelines. To eliminate invasive sampling while quantifying both dissolved and gaseous fractions, we engineered a non-invasive acoustic oxygen sensor.

Together, these modules form a ‘green lung’ for Mars: one organism that breathes in Martian CO₂, neutralises the  toxic perchlorates, and gives back breathable oxygen, measured without ever opening the vessel.


## Therapeutics (68)

### Aarhus University — RiboLogic: Programming RNA to think before it acts
*DNK · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6332) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/aarhus-university)

RiboLogic is an RNA-based therapeutic platform that utilizes microRNAs (miRNAs) as biomarkers for targeted treatment. A central challenge in modern therapeutics is delivering medicine exclusively where needed to increase efficacy and decrease side effects. Because miRNA expression patterns reflect specific cellular states, they serve as excellent biomarkers and combinatorial inputs for highly specific theranostics.

RiboLogic puts this into practice by coupling therapeutic mRNA with an upstream logic domain. Under normal conditions, this RNA structure folds up and blocks translation. However, once the specific combination of target miRNAs is present, the RNA structure unfolds and allows the cell's machinery to translate the protein. Both the logic section and the output protein sequence are fully modular, making it easy to redesign the system for different miRNA targets, logic operations, or therapeutic proteins.

### AFCM-Egypt — ROS-Responsive Engineered L. plantarum Delivering Silencing RNA via Membrane Vesicles for Atopic Dermatitis
*EGY · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6335) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/afcm-egypt)

Atopic dermatitis affects around 223 million people worldwide. Its symptoms often return unpredictably, while topical treatments are usually applied on a fixed schedule rather than in response to the skin's condition.

FROST (Functional RNA-mediated Optimization of the Skin microbiome for Targeted multi-pathway conditioning) explores a different approach. It uses engineered Lactiplantibacillus plantarum in a hydrogel designed to respond to active lesions. The bacterium responds to oxidative stress and expresses CO-BERA, an RNA scaffold carrying two siRNAs designed to target STAT6 and RelA. Together, these targets address two pathways involved in inflammation.

The design also aims to deliver the RNA into skin cells using bacterial membrane vesicles and improve skin retention through an adhesin. Two signals have separate roles: a skin-associated fatty acid supports the survival system, while lesion-associated oxidative stress triggers therapy.

FROST's validation is built mainly on seven models, each turning an assumption into a testable prediction.

### AIS-China — C.L.E.A.R., Cutibacterium Living Engineering for Acne & Homeostasis Regulation
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6191) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/ais-china)

Cutibacterium acnes, a natural skin commensal, holds promise as a chassis for microbiome-based therapeutics, yet limited genetic tools and transformation barriers hinder its engineering. AIS-China 2026 developed C.L.E.A.R. to advance C. acnes toward a programmable platform, with acne treatment as its first application.



Our toolkit integrates an engineered E. coli intermediate host (EC24) for plasmid methylation, shuttle vectors, fluorescent reporters, inducible regulatory systems, computational workflows for promoter prediction and codon optimization, and biocontainment strategies. For acne intervention, we designed enhanced glycerol metabolism to improve ecological competitiveness and fatty-acid-responsive SodC expression to mitigate oxidative stress. Preliminary characterization in EC24 supported selected modules, while transformation and validation in C. acnes remain future goals.



Beyond acne, C.L.E.A.R. lays the foundation for a reusable engineering platform capable of supporting diverse therapeutic applications through programmable skin commensals.

### BASIS-China — HEAL (Hairball Elimination And Lysis)
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6123) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/basis-china)

China’s urban pet cat population reached 72.89 million in 2025, highlighting the importance of feline healthcare. Hair swallowed during grooming can accumulate and potentially cause gastrointestinal problems, while conventional hairball products mainly facilitate passage rather than degrade hair itself. HEAL explores a P1–Trx–TrxR enzyme system for hair degradation, alongside an exploratory α-ketoglutarate production module for potential intestinal support. Calcium alginate encapsulation and a bile-acid-responsive containment circuit address delivery and biosafety questions, complemented by a food-weighing prototype for feeding records. Separately, a bile-acid-responsive containment circuit tackles biosafety by linking bacterial growth to an bile-acid signal that is scarce in the environment. Integrating stakeholder feedback, in-vitro experiments, and modeling, HEAL advances research toward practical and responsible hairball management while identifying further validation needed before feline application.

### Beagle Bioworks — *(no title)*
*USA · None · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6552)

*(no abstract published)*

### Bielefeld-CeBiTec — α Synu-Clear: Combating Proteinopathy in Parkinson’s Disease
*DEU · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6221) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/bielefeld-cebitec)

Like other proteinopathies, Parkinson's disease is a neurodegenerative condition triggered by the misfolding and subsequent aggregation of proteins. For those affected by such conditions, there is no cure in sight. 



Since aggregating proteins are causing progressive aggravation, we opted to remove the misfolded proteins to slow down or stop progression. To achieve this, we created a system covering everything from delivery to degradation. 



In a final therapy, lipid nanoparticles deliver a plasmid coding for a fusion protein consisting of an α-synuclein nanobody and an LC3B. The utilization of a pipeline consisting of top-notch protein design tools enabled us to model a de novo binding protein targeting the human dopamine transporter on neurons. The expressed and post-translationally modified degrader must interact with the membrane of the autophagy system and bind to its target. The complex is then broken down via the cell's own autophagy process.

### BiHealthy — *(no title)*
*IDN · None · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6564)

*(no abstract published)*

### Cataverse-GreatBay — A New Strategy for Gout Prevention: Preparation of Oral Probiotics for Lowering Uric Acid
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6193) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/cataverse-greatbay)

Residents in southern China have a dietary preference for high-purine foods such as seafood, resulting in a particularly high concentration of patients with gout and hyperuricemia. This has now become a prevalent regional health issue, and traditional treatment methods have numerous limitations. This project utilizes Escherichia coli Nissle 1917 as an engineering platform, incorporating a uric acid response system along with functional modules for uricase and catalase. This system can degrade uric acid and eliminate toxic hydrogen peroxide produced during metabolism within the gut. We have focused on modifying the uric acid transporter to enhance its transport efficiency for uric acid and its precursor, xanthine, thereby inhibiting uric acid production at the source. This oral probiotic formulation holds promise as a safe and highly effective new solution for the prevention and treatment of hyperuricemia and gout.

### CHINAGuangdongShimen — Re-CDI Alert ——A Living Alert System for CDI Recurrence
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6529) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/chinaguangdongshimen)

Clostridioides difficile infection (CDI) presents a major clinical challenge due to high recurrence rates and delayed post-treatment diagnostics. Current management relies on post-symptomatic reactive care, which further disrupts gut microbiota via broad-spectrum antibiotics. To address this, team China_Guangdong_Shimen developed "Re-CDI Alert," a living biotherapeutic alert system for proactive CDI recurrence monitoring. We engineered probiotic Escherichia coli Nissle 1917 (EcN) incorporating the heterologous PcrSR-Pcmh two-component sensing system from Aromatoleum aromaticum EbN1. This engineered strain selectively detects p-cresol—a characteristic metabolite produced during C. difficile germination—and converts local chemical signals into quantifiable reporter outputs (sfGFP). Furthermore, the system serves as a modular chassis for on-demand therapeutic release, such as neutralizing nanobodies. Re-CDI Alert shifts the paradigm from delayed reactive treatment to continuous proactive risk sensing, providing a safer, precise diagnostic platform to safeguard gut microecosystem health.

### CSU-CHINA — BRIDGE: Brain Repair via Injury-Induced BDNF Delivery
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6117) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/csu-china)

BRIDGE is a synNotch-based neural repair system designed to couple injury-associated sensing with localized delivery of brain-derived neurotrophic factor (BDNF). Engineered T cells express an anti-brevican (BCAN) synNotch receptor that links BCAN recognition to BDNF production. Receptor activation triggers sequential S2/S3 cleavage, releasing GAL4-VP64 to activate a 5×UAS promoter and drive BDNF expression and secretion. We integrated pathway modeling with structural analysis of the BCAN–scFv interface to characterize system behavior and guide receptor engineering. Interface-guided mutagenesis was used to refine receptor responses to BCAN stimulation. Through this design, BRIDGE aims to support neuronal survival and repair by concentrating BDNF delivery at injury sites and reducing systemic exposure.

### CUHK-HongKong-SBS — Engineering Lactiplantibacillus plantarum for Topical Therapy in Psoriasis
*HKG · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6309) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/cuhk-hongkong-sbs)

Psoriasis is a chronic, immune-mediated inflammatory skin disease affecting 2–3% of the global population. At present, there is no complete cure due to its multifactorial and complex nature, with current symptom-focused treatments often limited by variable efficacy and long-term side effects. To address these challenges, we developed a topically applied live biotherapeutic product utiizing bioengineered Lactiplantibacillus plantarum as an alternative treatment option for psoriatic patients. This commensal bacterium acts as a targeted delivery vehicle for fusion peptides combining an IL-23 receptor antagonist and an immunomodulator, effectively blocking the psoriatic inflammatory cascade while regulating skin health. Our project was made possible through extensive stakeholder engagement with patients, clinicians, scientists, industry experts, ethicists, iGEM peers, and the public. Complementing our technical design, we implemented a comprehensive educational campaign aimed at advancing synthetic biology education while actively reducing the social stigma associated with psoriasis.

### DBHS-CA — FIV-UR
*USA · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6302) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/dbhs-ca)

Nutrient-responsive pathways are an underused control layer for glycosylation. In Escherichia coli, the PhoR/PhoB two-component system senses phosphate limitation and activates Pho-regulated promoters, letting environmental conditions dictate gene expression. We apply this system to fisetin, a flavonoid whose utility is limited by properties such as low water solubility that sugar addition can improve, making glycosylation timing functionally important. We are engineering a phosphate-responsive circuit to control expression of yjiC, a glycosyltransferase that transfers glycans onto fisetin, first characterizing the promoter with GFP across phosphate conditions before swapping in yjiC. Success would establish nutrient availability as a tunable input for modifying biologically important small molecules.

### Epiphany-NYC — TheraNOva: Fighting Inflammation in Leaky Gut
*USA · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6480) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/epiphany-nyc)

Leveraging the ability of E. coli, a naturally occurring gut bacteria, to produce vesicles that can be modified with a nanobody to target the afflicted gut epithelial cells. Nitric oxide levels rise when inflammation causes the junctions in the cells of the gut to become leaky. We will engineer the bacteria to have a nitric oxide sensor that activates a specific promoter controlling the production of dual therapeutic molecules: an enzyme that facilitates the production of butyrate and a silencing RNA that shuts down tumor necrosis factor production.

### FSU — Lilieum: an engineered Lactobacillus rhamnosus GG probiotic for capture and fecal clearance of dietary phthalates
*USA · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6081) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/fsu)

90% of people in industrialized countries are exposed to (2-ethylhexyl) phthalate (DEHP), and prenatal exposure has been linked to roughly 56,500 preterm births and $3.84 billion in US costs in a single year. The Human Practices Team used systems thinking to identify pregnancy as the window of greatest consequence and thus, highest impact.

The 2026 FSU iGEM Team designed Lilieum - an engineered LGG cell that senses captured DEHP via membrane rigidity and triggers a reduction in LGG's pili expression; thus, the cell no longer adheres in the GI tract and moves out of the body. 

A DEHP-partitioning simulation of an LGG membrane proxy in GROMACS supported LGG's pre-existing capture mechanism. Through Lilieum's prioritized education programs, exposure literacy is treated as part of the intervention rather than as its dissemination.

### Gikal — ZipTide: designing short peptides against zonulin-driven loss of the intestinal barrier
*TUR · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6490) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/gikal)

The intestinal barrier is one cell thick, and zonulin loosens the tight junctions that hold it shut. No approved antagonist exists. ZipTide designs short peptides against that signal. We collapsed the 25.6-billion-sequence space of 8-mers onto 2,220,075 compositions without discarding any, ranked them by physicochemical distance to larazotide, screened 1,500 for toxicity, allergenicity and haemolysis, and carried twenty to structure prediction and docking. Three were chemically synthesised and deposited in the Registry alongside the control. Caco-2 cells challenged with gliadin were read as zonulin release by ELISA, against a criterion written before any data existed. The assay worked: gliadin raised zonulin 4.8-fold and larazotide brought it down. Neither candidate tested reached the bar, though ZT-1 fell in larazotide's direction and may contribute to barrier repair; the third arrived as an empty vial. Eighteen stakeholder engagements reshaped the project's scope, read-out and language.

### GU Frankfurt — HIDE - HLA Interference via Disguise and Erasure
*DEU · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6108) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/gu-frankfurt)

Transplanted organs are often rejected because the recipient's immune system recognizes specific proteins on the donor organ's surface as "non-self" and attacks them. To prevent this, patients currently need to take immunosuppressive drugs for the rest of their lives, which weakens their entire immune system and leaves them vulnerable to infections and other complications. Our project, HIDE, aims to solve this more precisely: instead of broadly suppressing the immune system, we are developing a therapy that locally downregulates these donor proteins directly on the transplanted organ, making it less visible to the immune system. Our long-term goal is to improve transplant outcomes without the long-term effects of conventional immunosuppression.

### Gymnasium Leiden — V.A.U.L.T
*NLD · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6505) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/gymnasium-leiden)

Versatile Adaptive Unified Loading Technology (V.A.U.L.T), focusses on Major Vault Proteins (MVPs) as an alternative platform for thermostable mRNA-based therapies. Our team looked at modifying naturally occurring eukaryotic Vault proteins, to produce them in Escherichia coli. Modifications include the addition of a protein A derived Z-domain, as a potential docking station for IgGs, to give MVPs a target delivery option. Furthermore we have looked at options to evade the natural phagolysosomal pathway.

### HFI — Dual EcN Probiotic Synergistic Therapy for the Rare Disease Hyperlysinemia
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6178) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/hfi)

Hyperlysinemia is a rare amino acid metabolic disorder. Because patients are unable to metabolize lysine, they must follow a low-protein diet for life. We aim to use synthetic biology to address life-threatening conditions faced by patients with hyperlysinemia, such as delayed brain development and epilepsy, and to break them free from the “dietary prison” of having to rely on an extremely low-protein diet for life. Therefore, we will develop “smart synergistic probiotics” by constructing lysine metabolic pathways in engineered bacteria to achieve efficient lysine degradation. Furthermore, we will utilize the SpyTag/SpyCatcher system to establish physical couplings between bacteria, creating “metabolic tunnels” to resolve issues of low efficiency and secondary toxicity caused by product diffusion in cascade reactions. At the same time, we will incorporate a kill-switch to prevent biosafety risks associated with the release of engineered bacteria.

### HKBU — FibroSwitch: Reversing liver scarring with an herbal-inducible smart vector
*HKG · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6148) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/hkbu)

Liver fibrosis, driven by hepatic stellate cell (HSC) activation, is a major global health burden with limited therapeutic options. To address this, we engineered FibroSwitch, a lentiviral smart vector that delivers the therapeutic transcription factor GATA4 under the control of a synthetic, Nrf2-responsive pARE promoter. We successfully established stable engineered LX-2 cell lines and verified robust GATA4 protein expression, which effectively reduced key fibrotic markers including α-SMA. Furthermore, we optimized a sequential treatment strategy that revealed the potential of a powerful dual-action synergy. Triggering the genetic circuit with the herbal compound Baicalein not only drives GATA4 expression but also leverages the compound's intrinsic anti-inflammatory properties to significantly downregulate collagen production (COL1A1). FibroSwitch represents a next-generation, finely tunable gene therapy that combines targeted cellular reprogramming with microenvironmental modulation to safely and effectively reverse liver scarring.

### HKU-HongKong — Omni-Patch
*HKG · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6401) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/hku-hongkong)

Chronic wounds frequently become trapped in persistent inflammation, disrupting tissue repair. Omni-Patch addresses this challenge through a cell-free hydrogel dressing combining a de novo-designed peptide with the tissue repair factor FGF2. Our de novo computational pipeline integrates RFdiffusion, LigandMPNN and molecular dynamics simulations to generate and screen peptide candidates targeting the MD-2/LPS interface of the TLR4 signaling complex.

Experimental screening identified αTi-14 as a promising lead that reduced cell-associated fluorescent LPS in human macrophage-like cells. Through iterative Design-Build-Test-Learn cycles, we refined cellular assays to investigate its anti-inflammatory potential; functional confirmation remains ongoing. In parallel, we explored recombinant peptide production and alginate delivery, with preliminary release studies using FGF2 and peptide variants. Mathematical modeling connected molecular design with delivery kinetics and downstream biological responses. Guided by stakeholder engagement and educational initiatives, these efforts establish a foundation for developing Omni-Patch to locally modulate excessive inflammation while supporting tissue repair in wounds.

### Houston-TX — AKGut: Model-Guided Design of a Probiotic that Produces α-Ketoglutarate
*USA · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6316) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/houston-tx)

Alpha-ketoglutarate (α-KG) extends Caenorhabditis elegans lifespan, maximally at 8 mM (Chin et al., 2014). AKGut asks whether Escherichia coli Nissle 1917, a clinically established probiotic, can be engineered to produce α-KG continuously rather than delivering it as a supplement.



We designed the strain computationally first. Flux balance analysis on the genome-scale model iHM1533 identified glutamate dehydrogenase, not α-KG dehydrogenase, as the largest α-KG drain; placed the production optimum near half-maximal oxygen; and showed that no knockout combination couples production to growth, motivating an α-KG-responsive addiction system for future development. 



Modelling then shaped the build: it reduced the difference between two designs to a single deletion, and caught a residue-numbering error that would have placed the citrate synthase mutation one residue off target. The corrected plasmid is verified; chromosomal knockouts remain in troubleshooting.

### HunanU — NariViva: Engineered Probiotics Delivering Naringenin to Restore Vaginal Health
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6305) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/hunanu)

Genitourinary Syndrome of Menopause (GSM) is a chronic condition associated with declining estrogen levels, affecting quality of life in postmenopausal women. NariViva aims to develop a probiotic-based biomedical platform for localized, pH-responsive naringenin production as a potential strategy for GSM management. We designed a genetic circuit integrating a pH-responsive regulatory module with a naringenin biosynthetic pathway comprising tyrosine ammonia-lyase (TAL), 4-coumarate-CoA ligase (4CL), chalcone synthase (CHS), and chalcone isomerase (CHI). Using Escherichia coli as an initial chassis, we characterized pH-responsive gene expression and investigated a naringenin-responsive regulatory module. Western blot and HPLC analysis showed increased CHI abundance with rising pH in the circuit without negative feedback, whereas the negative-feedback design helped stabilize CHI abundance across the tested pH range. These findings support the feasibility of combining environmental sensing with feedback regulation. Further product-level validation, chassis optimization, and biosafety evaluation are needed before future development in vaginally compatible probiotics.

### HUST-UEVE-UPSaclay — Pet Dental Guard
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6304) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/hust-ueve-upsaclay)

Pet oral inflammatory diseases arise from microbial dysbiosis, pathogenic biofilms, and persistent inflammation. Fusobacterium nucleatum (Fn), a key bridging organism in oral biofilms, contributes to biofilm formation and inflammatory responses. To address these challenges, we developed Pet Dental Guard, an integrated synthetic biology platform combining engineered M13 phages, engineered Escherichia coli Nissle 1917 (EcN), and a double-layer hydrogel. The engineered phages are designed to target Fn and deliver metronidazole for localized antibacterial activity, while engineered EcN responds to inflammatory signals by producing interleukin-10 (IL-10) to regulate inflammation. The hydrogel enhances local retention and enables sequential release of both components. By integrating targeted antibacterial intervention, inflammation regulation, and controlled delivery, Pet Dental Guard aims to provide a comprehensive strategy for managing pet oral inflammatory diseases.

### HZAU-China — PurrFit
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6424) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/hzau-china)

Feline obesity, driven jointly by overfeeding, limited activity, and human care routines, has become a growing “One Health” challenge among pet cats. To address this, our project PurrFit proposes a synthetic biology strategy that combines microbiome engineering with digital care. We employ MetaEdit to engineer Bacteroides thetaiotaomicron, a common gut-associated bacterium, to produce bile salt hydrolase (BSH), which will help regulate bile acid metabolism and lipid-related pathways. Meanwhile, we design an FGF21-ABD fusion protein to improve the stability of FGF21-based metabolic signals and support healthier energy use. As an auxiliary module, a Pet AI Agent could provide personalized feeding and exercise suggestions for owners. By integrating feline health, biosafe microbial engineering and owner behavioral guidance, PurrFit is expected to serve as a cat-focused but transferable framework for companion animal health.

### ICJFLS — ORA-GUARD: Engineered Oral Probiotics for Long-Term Bad Breath Solution
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6098) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/icjfls)

ORA-GUARD is a synthetic biology project aimed at developing a long-acting therapeutic product for halitosis using engineered oral probiotics. we selected Streptococcus salivarius K12, a GRAS certified oral commensal, as the chassis. Using CRISPR Cas9 mediated genome integration, the engineered strain stably expresses codon optimized multi enzyme system that efficiently secretes volatile sulfur compounds (VSC) degrading enzymes. These enzymes convert malodorous VSC (hydrogen sulfide, methanethiol, etc.) into odorless sulfate, achieving precise and sustained deodorization. A multi layered biosafety strategy, including auxotrophy and a temperature sensitive kill switch, ensures environmental containment.

### KCSCD — FELIX：FIPV Extermination Lipase (Spray) & RNAi Interference eXecution Platform
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6492) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/kcscd)

Feline infectious peritonitis (FIP), caused by mutated feline coronavirus (FCoV), remains a fatal disease with no cure. Current strategies lack an integrated solution covering environmental contamination, early intestinal infection, and established FIP. We introduce FELIX — a Anticoronavirus Nanoplatform for Noninvasive, Integrated Host & Environmental Viral Lysis & Antiviral Therapy . It is a unified EcN based system designed to eliminate FCoV at every stage. Our three pronged strategy: Front 1 – Environmental disinfection: Engineered lipases EST29 and LIP29 degrade the viral lipid envelope, neutralizing viruses before host contact. Front 2 – Gut prophylaxis: EcN (E. coli Nissle 1917) continuously produces RNAi molecules targeting conserved FCoV sequences, clearing intestinal viruses before they mutate into FIPV. Front 3 – Therapeutic intervention: When FIP develops, the same EcN platform delivers RNAi therapeutics systemically to silence mutated viral genes, blocking replication and halting disease progression.

### KFUPM-Saudi — ARMOR: Autoimmune Responsive Molecular Origami Refugee
*SAU · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6429) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/kfupm-saudi)

Autoimmune diseases arise when the immune system mistakenly attacks healthy tissues, while conventional immunosuppressive therapies often compromise protective immunity. ARMOR (Autoimmune Responsive Molecular Origami Refuge) proposes a programmable DNA origami nanostructure designed for localized, conditional immunomodulation. The system features a hinged DNA origami box secured by a perforin-responsive aptamer lock. Upon detecting perforin released by cytotoxic T cells, the box is designed to open and expose PD-L1, potentially engaging PD-1 receptors to attenuate local immune activity. Our engineering approach integrates DNA nanostructure design through Cadnano, Oxview, and OxDNA molecular simulations, perforin–aptamer docking, and COMSOL-based transport modeling of a proposed needle-free transdermal delivery patch and a three-compartment analysis for drug delivery. Through iterative computational design and optimization, ARMOR explores the feasibility of coupling molecular sensing with controlled therapeutic exposure. This proof-of-concept platform presents a potential strategy for spatially and temporally regulated immune modulation.

### KULeuven — EMPOWER - Engineering Microbes to Promote Ovarian WEllbeing and Recovery
*BEL · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6299) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/kuleuven)

Polyendocrine Metabolic Ovarian Syndrome (PMOS) affects 1 in 8 women of reproductive age. Despite its prevalence, no approved disease-modifying treatment exists. Emerging evidence implicates the involvement of the intestinal microbiome. We hypothesize that microbial shifts alter the bile acid pool, contributing to metabolic dysfunction, inflammation, hyperandrogenism, and reproductive complications.

Since the gut commensal Phocaeicola vulgatus is reportedly enriched in PMOS patients, bile salt hydrolase (BSH) levels rise in parallel, resulting in alterations of bile acid composition and downstream signaling. Rather than eliminating this bacterium, we aim to engineer it to specifically modulate bile acid metabolism. To overcome the limited genetic tools available for this non-model anaerobe, we developed a synthetic biology toolkit featuring characterized promoters evaluated using four fluorescent reporters, optimized conjugation, and phenotypic characterization of potential chassis strains. Building on this, we developed EMPOWER, designed to selectively modulate BSH activity in P. vulgatus to restore beneficial bile acid signaling.

### LethbridgeHS — Oat Shield: Sustainable cellulose bandages with integrated antimicrobial peptides for wound treatment
*CAN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6066) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/lethbridgehs)

Oat Shield investigated the development of a biodegradable antimicrobial wound dressing using oat husk cellulose, transforming agricultural waste into a sustainable medical alternative. Conventional single-use bandages contribute to healthcare waste and may contain synthetic materials and harmful chemicals. Meanwhile, oat husks are often discarded despite being rich in renewable cellulose. Our team explored cellulose extraction methods to obtain suitable fibres for bandage production. To enhance antimicrobial properties, we investigated the expression and purification of subtilin, a natural antimicrobial peptide produced by Bacillus subtilis that targets Gram-positive bacteria, a leading cause of wound infections. Using an engineered Escherichia coli expression system with an IPTG-inducible promoter and a hexahistidine tag, we produced subtilin for antimicrobial testing. Ultimately, Oat Shield aims to combine cellulose-based materials with subtilin to create sustainable wound dressings that help prevent infections while reducing agricultural and healthcare waste, supporting UN Sustainable Development Goals 12 and 13.

### LHWHS Missouri — A High-throughput Framework for Screening Therapeutic siRNA Cocktails in Hyperlipidemia
*USA · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6427) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/lhwhs-missouri)

Traditional single-gene siRNA therapies often fall short against complex conditions like hyperlipidemia. To overcome this limitation, we propose a combinatorial siRNA strategy utilizing multi-target cocktails to optimize lipid metabolism regulation.



At the core of this initiative is our proprietary technology: a high-throughput screening platform featuring single-cell addressability within a single plate of liver cells. This powerful system rapidly tests myriad siRNA cocktail formulations to identify superior multi-target therapies. By improving silencing efficiency and robustness, our platform establishes a versatile framework for advancing RNA therapeutics across diverse complex diseases.

### LINKS-Union — S.H.I.E.L.D Smart Hydrogel for Integrated Enamel & Lesion Defense
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6454) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/links-union)

Orthodontic braces trap bacteria, and cariogenic acid causes enamel demineralization, white spot lesions, and caries. Existing cleaners suffer from "burst release," losing efficacy quickly. To address this, we developed SHIELD, an acid-responsive hydrogel coating for braces. It uses calcium alginate loaded with CaGP, alkaline phosphatase (ALP), and an antimicrobial peptide (AMP) fusion protein. ALP catalyzes CaGP hydrolysis to form a mineralized nHA layer, creating a dual physical barrier that slows AMP release and competitively resists acid attack. The MtuI intein self-cleaves at pH < 6.5 to release LL-37 on demand. This system enables intelligent responsiveness and long-term sustained release, suppressing biofilms and reducing demineralization. Ultimately, SHIELD embodies the principle of "prevention over treatment," intercepting enamel damage before it becomes irreversible and providing a universal, safe, and convenient solution for long-term oral protection.

### Lund — A Gut-Based Approach to address Androgen Excess in PMOS
*SWE · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6160) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/lund)

Polyendocrine Metabolic Ovarian Syndrome (PMOS), formerly known as Polycystic Ovary Syndrome (PCOS), affects millions of women worldwide, with hyperandrogenism being a key driver of symptoms such as irregular periods, acne, and an increased risk of type 2 diabetes. To address the lack of therapeutically precise treatments, iGEM Lund 2026 is developing a proof-of-concept synthetic biology platform designed to tackle androgen excess locally within the gut. By engineering a bacterial strain to express androgen-metabolizing enzymes under the control of a gut-responsive transcriptional switch, our system aims to convert excess androgens into less bioactive metabolites. Our project seeks to contribute on two fronts simultaneously: improving pathophysiological understanding of PMOS and exploring potential management strategies.

### LUT-CHINA — Bioactive Four-Layer Dressing for Diabetic Foot Ulcers via Synthetic Biolo
*CHN · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6132) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/lut-china)

Diabetic foot ulcers (DFUs) are chronic, hard-to-heal wounds with high amputation risk, while conventional dressings fail to address underlying pathological barriers to tissue repair.

Using synthetic biology, we engineer E. coli cell factories to biosynthesize two herbal bioactives — calycosin-7-glucoside and lobetyolin — for synergistic regulation of the wound microenvironment. We integrate them into a four-layer functional dressing enabling targeted drug delivery, efficient exudate management and atraumatic dressing changes.

We have completed cell-based functional screening, validated lobetyolin biosynthesis via HPLC, curated 5 Registry parts, optimized the ternary formulation and fabricated a preliminary prototype. This project aims to deliver a novel DFU therapeutic option and advance high-value biomanufacturing of local herbal resources.

### LZU GANSU — DENTAL SHIELD: A Probiotic System for Early Detection and Treatment of Dental Caries and Periodontitis
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6082) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/lzu-gansu)

To address the clinical challenges of early diagnosis and treatment of oral infectious diseases such as dental caries and periodontitis, we have designed and validated an engineered Lactococcus lactis probiotic. This probiotic can colonize the oral microenvironment, report early-stage hidden lesions, and enable timely intervention. Our project provides a novel and practical approach for the simple and home-based early prevention and treatment of dental caries and periodontitis.

### LZU-CHINA — INTESTINAL BARRIER: designed for the prevention and treatment of Salmonella infections during the perioperative period.
*CHN · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6079) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/lzu-china)

Our project designed and reported an engineered probiotic Escherichia coli Nissle 1917 (ECN1917) strain with a modified chassis genome. We integrated a dual-switch system into the engineered bacteria, which combines the AI-2 quorum sensing recognition system from intestinal Salmonella and the tetrathionate inflammation signal recognition system. This enables the early detection of disease signals within the gut microbiota, facilitates convenient diagnostics based on stool samples, and suppresses intestinal pathogens through the active expression of antimicrobial peptides.

### MaiAnTiem — *(no title)*
*CAN · None · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6536)

*(no abstract published)*

### McMaster-Canada — Chariot: A Platform Technology for OMV-based Therapeutics
*CAN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6297) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/mcmaster-canada)

Bacterial outer membrane vesicles (OMVs) are promising drug delivery vector and vaccine platforms, but their biomanufacturing is limited by low yields, high variability, and costly manufacturing. We present Chariot, an end-to-end platform for the design of vesicle nucleating peptide (VNp)-tagged therapeutics for high-yield OMV loading. Leveraging OMV biofilm penetration and adjuvanticity, we investigate VNp-mediated loading of antimicrobial peptides and bacterial antigens to combat bacterial infections in preventative and acute contexts. Our in silico models, Apollo and Artemis, expand Chariot’s therapeutic applicability through de novo cargo generation and VNp sequence optimization, alongside a custom bioreactor and microfluidic OMV isolation device. In addition to our OMVS (Observe, Modify, Vision, Synthesis) stakeholder framework, we ground our educational initiatives in the MUST (Mutual, Universal, Sustainable, Thoughtful) learning pillars, highlighting meaningful confrontations between diverse communities and the challenges of implementing engineered therapeutics. Chariot enables robust OMV bioengineering for innovative, targeted therapeutic development.

### Michigan — MagNav
*USA · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6276) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/michigan)

Ovarian cancer and endometriosis are very difficult to treat without invasive standards of care. Harnessing the pathological characteristics of these diseases that elude traditional methods of treatment, our project engineers a non-pathogenic bacteria into a novel, specific, and non-invasive treatment option. AMB-1, a species of bacteria responsive to magnetic fields and hypoxic microenvironments, is combined with fine-tuned RNA thermometers to deliver a therapeutic payload customizable for ovarian cancer and endometriosis. Our project aims to provide an accessible solution to sufficient life-saving and life-changing therapies.

### MSP-Maastricht — Vivora- Treating Recurrent Bacterial Vaginosis
*NLD · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6418) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/msp-maastricht)

One in four women globally is affected by bacterial vaginosis (BV), and although current antibiotic treatments provide short-term relief, BV recurs in over 55% of affected women within 12 months. Vivora aims to lay the design foundations for an engineered live biotherapeutic that combines a two-part detection system and the synthesis of Antimicrobial Peptides (AMPs). The detection mechanism responds to elevated pH and the presence of the biogenic amine putrescine, ensuring targeted treatment delivery while limiting the development of resistant strains. AMPs are naturally derived proteins shown to have antibacterial properties against BV-associated bacteria. The vaginal commensal bacterium Lactobacillus jensenii serves as the chassis for this synergistic system. It is engineered using protocols developed by a previous iGEM team, Vagineering. Ultimately, Vivora aims to reduce the rates of BV recurrence and develop parts applicable to other vaginal infections.

### NCState — AMPack: Engineering probiotic yeast for antimicrobial peptide production
*USA · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6232) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/ncstate)

To address growing antimicrobial resistance this study evaluated the feasibility of engineering S. boulardii to produce four functional antimicrobial peptides (AMPs): LL37, AMP-17, ToAP2, and P113, and assess their anti bacterial effects. Digitally designed plasmids, later constructed in lab via Golden Gate assembly were transformed into S. boulardii strains to evaluate AMP production, growth rates, and cellular morphology.  Unmodified S. boulardii (PY0001) showed no native hemolytic or antimicrobial activity. Modified strains (LL37, AMP17) had significantly reduced growth rates (p=6.4278×10^−6) compared to PY0001, likely caused by nourseothricin (NAT) in the selective media. NAT substantially reduced B. subtilis and E. coli viability, whereas P. putida was moderately sensitive. Notably, AMP17 significantly inhibited P. putida growth (p=0.003553) in our agar diffusion assay. Although NAT reduced growth rates, AMP17 successfully inhibited P. putida, demonstrating the feasibility of engineering S. boulardii as a therapeutic probiotic against resistant bacteria.

### NTU-Singapore — Development of efficient RNA writers
*SGP · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6530) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/ntu-singapore)

The ability to rewrite biological information in living cells has many applications. Although DNA editing can install enduring changes in the genome and correct disease-causing mutations, it suffers from safety issues as any off-target edits will be permanent and thus propagate to subsequent generations. In contrast, RNA engineering offers a safer alternative as any off-target effect will be transient. Moreover, the targeting space is smaller as only expressed genes can be edited, reducing the risk of non-specific alterations. To date, considerable efforts have focused on the development of powerful RNA base editors that can install single nucleotide changes in selected RNA transcripts. However, they cannot introduce any arbitrary edit, such as swapping of entire exons. An attractive strategy to rewrite RNA molecules in a universal manner is trans-splicing. Unfortunately, existing programmable trans-splicing methods are inefficient and lack design rules. To address the problem, we will develop novel RNA writing technologies.

### Patras UPAT — EPIONE: Decoding pain to restore life
*GRC · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6449) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/patras-upat)

Chronic cancer pain is managed almost exclusively with systemic opioids, whose escalating doses bring tolerance, sedation, dependence and respiratory risk. Project Epione asks whether patients can make the analgesic themselves. We deliver the human proenkephalin (PENK) gene, precursor of the enkephalins, as a nonviral lipoplex sprayed intranasally, exploiting the olfactory route that bypasses the blood brain barrier. Working entirely from computational design, we built four bicistronic constructs as a 2x2 matrix of linker chemistry against reporter orientation, with the His tag placed at the amino terminus so the terminal heptapeptide stays intact. Constructs are expressed in HEK293T cells, tracked by mEGFP fluorescence, purified by affinity chromatography and matured in vitro with recombinant PC2, 7B2 and carboxypeptidase E, since these cells lack the convertases of the regulated secretory pathway. We also designed an engineered PENK whose enkephalin cores carry a delta preferring motif, submitted to the iGEM Registry.

### PekingHSC — HEPHA-RNA
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6371) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/pekinghsc)

Chromosome microdeletion syndromes reduce the expression of multiple genes, yet replacing deleted genes remains challenging. HEPHA-RNA offers an alternative by enhancing translation from the remaining allele without adding DNA. Its Binding Domain recognizes the target mRNA’s 3′ UTR, while its Effector Domain recruits the 60S ribosomal subunit. For Wolf–Hirschhorn syndrome, LETM1- and NSD2-targeting domains enhanced luciferase reporter expression. LETM1-targeting domains increased endogenous protein levels to over 1.5-fold those of a non-binding control across two Western blot experiments, without significantly increasing mRNA abundance. NSD2 upregulation also increased H3K36me2, a histone mark associated with gene regulation. Our measurement workflow combines off-target screening, luciferase assays, protein and mRNA quantification, and dose optimization. Computational models connect phenotype-guided gene prioritization and binding-site analysis with AAV delivery considerations. Through Integrated Human Practices and PHAROS, engagement with clinicians, researchers, and patients informed a patient-centered approach spanning diagnosis, treatment, care, and social inclusion.

### PuiChing-Macau — Tuning butyrate with engineered probiotics to prevent Alzheimer’s
*MAC · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6270) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/puiching-macau)

Emerging evidence underscores a strong association between gut microbiota metabolites (e.g., butyrate) and neurodegenerative diseases such as Alzheimer’s disease (AD). Disruptions in gut microbial composition impair intestinal barrier function, allowing pro-inflammatory molecules, such as nitric monoxide (NO), to enter systemic circulation. The persistent inflammatory state contributes to neuroinflammation and accelerates neuronal damage. In AD patients, reduced butyrate-producing bacteria lead to decreased neuroprotective short-chain fatty acids. To address this imbalance along the gut–brain axis, we engineered probiotic E. coli Nissle 1917 to sense and respond to inflammatory signals. We built a pNorVβ-promoter-based biosensor to detect NO, and a self-regulating butyrate biosynthesis system with a negative feedback loop, preventing overproduction. Together with the customized drug-delivery hardware, risk-predicting software, and personalized dosage estimation models, our engineered probiotics provide a new solution to mitigate neuroinflammation in AD.

### RESCUE — *(no title)*
*EGY · None · disqualified* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6539)

*(no abstract published)*

### SCU-WestChina — PerioGuard-A Biohybrid Living Therapeutic Platform for Precision Periodontal Regeneration
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6402) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/scu-westchina)

Periodontitis causes progressive destruction of periodontal tissues, while current regenerative treatments often require invasive surgery. To explore a non-surgical alternative, we developed PerioGuard, an engineered probiotic–material hybrid system integrating regenerative protein delivery with enhanced bacterial retention. We engineered Escherichia coli Nissle 1917 (EcN) to produce and secrete CsgA–amelogenin (AMG) under the control of a HucR-based genetic circuit. Using AI-assisted crystal structure prediction, we developed a porous nucleoside-based adsorptive coating (PNAC) assembled from 2FA and cyanuric acid.
