PNAC promotes bacterial surface attachment, while 2FA enhances biofilm formation and activates CsgA–AMG expression through its metabolic conversion to xanthine. Experiments demonstrated inducible protein expression, PNAC adsorption onto EcN, enhanced biofilm-associated responses, and reduced early macrophage phagocytosis. PerioGuard provides a proof of concept for combining programmable protein delivery with material-assisted bacterial retention, offering a potential strategy for non-surgical periodontal regeneration.

### Sheffield — DRAVEX: An engineered exosome platform for gene therapy delivery in Dravet Syndrome
*GBR · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6430) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/sheffield)

Dravet syndrome is a severe childhood-onset genetic epilepsy commonly associated with pathogenic variants in SCN1A, which encodes the voltage-gated sodium channel Nav1.1. Dysfunction of inhibitory GABAergic interneurons contributes to treatment-resistant seizures and neurological impairment. Current treatments primarily manage symptoms, highlighting the need for therapies targeting the underlying genetic cause. However, delivering genetic therapeutics to the brain remains challenging due to the selective permeability of the blood–brain barrier. This project aims to develop DRAVEX, an engineered exosome-based platform for targeted gene therapy delivery. Exosomes produced by engineered HEK293 cells will display an RVG–Lamp2b fusion protein to enhance BBB transport. The platform is intended to deliver therapeutic cargo capable of restoring functional SCN1A expression. Experimental investigations, complemented by in silico modelling, will explore the potential of engineered exosomes to improve targeted delivery and inform future genetic therapies for Dravet syndrome.

### SJTU-BioX-Shanghai — Dual-Armor
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6054) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/sjtu-biox-shanghai)

Chronic wounds are frequently complicated by Staphylococcus aureus infections, whose biofilms hinder bacterial eradication and contribute to persistent infection and delayed healing. Conventional treatments, including debridement and antibiotics, may fail to fully eliminate biofilms, highlighting the need for integrated therapeutic strategies. Dual-Armor aims to address this challenge through a responsive wound dressing that combines biofilm disruption, targeted antibacterial activity, and wound healing. Two engineered bacterial chassis, Staphylococcus epidermidis and Escherichia coli Nissle 1917, communicate through naringenin-mediated signaling to coordinate these complementary functions. Integrated into a multilayer dressing with freeze-dried bacterial layers and semipermeable membranes, the system is designed for practical local application.

### SNIoE-India — SmogSense: An Engineered Macrophage That Senses Pollution and Resolves Inflammation
*IND · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6519) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/snioe-india)

Fine particulate matter below 2.5 micrometres (PM2.5) is among the most serious environmental health threats facing Indian cities. In Delhi, where exposure is near constant, these particles bypass upper airway defences and reach the alveolar compartment, acting directly on macrophages. PM2.5 is not merely an irritant. It acts as a chronic molecular stressor, locking macrophages into persistent NF-κB activation and continuous release of pro-inflammatory mediators, driving sustained pulmonary inflammation and irreversible tissue damage. SmogSense targets this cascade at the cellular level. We are engineering a synthetic gene circuit for human monocyte-derived macrophages: an autonomous anti-inflammatory system operating natively within the cell. It senses PM2.5-induced inflammatory signalling in real time and responds by expressing interleukin-10 (IL-10), which feeds back to suppress NF-κB hyperactivation. This sense, respond and shut off architecture is intrinsically self-limiting, restoring homeostatic balance.

### Stanford University — Kele Patch: A mRNA logic-gated RADAR sensor for cell-state specific reprogramming in keloid scars
*USA · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6278) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/stanford-university)

Keloid scars are pathological scars which continue to grow beyond the site of the original injury and do not regress. Existing treatments include surgical removal and steroid injections, which often have high recurrence rates. We propose Kele Patch, an mRNA therapy delivered via microneedle patch straight into cells in the keloid tissue. To avoid activation in non-targeted cells, the sensor is designed to detect the transcriptional state of cells and only produce a therapeutic payload in activated keloid fibroblasts. Because of our extensive modeling of the entire pipeline from target selection to sensor design, delivery and activation, future researchers can use our platform RADAR-Scout to develop cell-state specific therapies for other diseases resulting from aberrant transcriptional programs. Kele Patch is the first synthetic biology approach to treat a widespread and overlooked disease and could easily be adapted to treat other cell-state-specific diseases, such as other fibrotic conditions.

### Stony Brook — Engineering Bacteria to Detect and Break Down Biofilms Secreted by Staphylococcus aureus
*USA · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6141) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/stony-brook)

Biofilms are surface-adherent microbial communities encased in extracellular polymeric substances of extracellular DNA, sugars, and proteins, and are a leading cause of healthcare-associated infections on post-surgical wound sites. The goal is to bioengineer Escherichia coli that detects and degrades early- and late-stage biofilms of methicillin-resistant Staphylococcus aureus (MRSA). 



Biofilm formation will be detected through Autoinducer-2, a quorum sensing molecule indicating early-stage formation, and nitric oxide, which accumulates during later stages.    



The YebF protein secretes three substrate-specific enzymes: Nuclease1, an endonuclease that cleaves extracellular DNA; SspA, a protease that targets biofilm-associated proteins; and Dispersin B, a glycoside hydrolase that degrades poly-β-1,6-N-acetyl-D-glucosamine sugar. 



We confirmed secretion of Nuclease1 and SspA, nitric oxide detection at physiological concentrations, and eDNA degradation. SspA showed proteolytic activity; Dispersin B remained membrane-embedded and untested. An arabinose-inducible mazE/mazF kill switch eliminates the engineered bacteria with the introduction of arabinose. Degradation of biofilms remains to be demonstrated.

### SUIS-SFLS — TyroFix: Immobilized Tyrosinase-Driven Mussel Protein Skin Repair
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6199) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/suis-sfls)

Skin injuries often require materials that can both protect damaged tissue and promote healing. Inspired by mussel adhesion, TyroFix harnesses synthetic biology to create multifunctional adhesive proteins with antioxidant and regenerative properties. Using engineered fusion proteins and tyrosinase-mediated DOPA modification, we produce biomimetic mussel proteins capable of enhancing tissue adhesion and supporting skin repair. To improve sustainability and reduce costs, tyrosinase is immobilized on magnetic beads for efficient reuse. TyroFix combines enzyme engineering, biomaterials, and synthetic biology to provide a scalable platform for next-generation wound-care materials and regenerative medicine applications.

### SUSTech — ORCA: On-target RNA Correction of APOE4 for Alzheimer’s
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6085) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/sustech)

ORCA explores programmable C-to-U RNA editing of APOE4, a major genetic risk factor for Alzheimer’s disease, toward an APOE3-like sequence without permanent genome modification.



Through POD and CETUS, we engineered recognition and catalytic domains. PUFFER integrated protein and RNA foundation models, structural models, and generative models. Wet-lab measurements informed modeling, while predictions guided candidate selection and experimental design through iterative feedback. In HEK293T assays, two candidates retained 61–70% target editing while reducing cumulative bystander editing within the measured APOE region by 84–88% versus the parental editor. G.M.A.C. addressed cell-context-dependent and antisense-mediated regulation, and we established an AAV9 preparation workflow for delivery studies.



We developed SONAR and its Measurement Pipeline to connect reporter engineering with interpretable, reusable analysis. Human Practices dialogue with clinicians and researchers shaped priorities for specificity, controllability, and functional evaluation. These efforts provide experimental foundations and tools for APOE RNA-editing research, linking computational design with iterative experimental development.

### SUSTech-MED — FORGE: Functional Outcomes via Reprogramming by Genetically Engineered Lactiplantibacillus in Autism
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6048) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/sustech-med)

SUSTech-MED focuses on the frontier of Autism Spectrum Disorder (ASD) intervention. Our team innovatively utilizes Lactiplantibacillus plantarum L168 as a synthetic biology chassis, engineered to biosynthesize functional molecules—Sulforaphane (SFN) and N-Acetylcysteine (NAC)—while incorporating metabolic pathways to efficiently degrade glutamine and convert neurotoxic brain ammonia into branched-chain amino acids (BCAAs). Delivered via a non-invasive intranasal route to bypass the blood-brain barrier, our system precisely targets the neural microenvironment. By synergistically mitigating ammonia toxicity, modulating neuroinflammation, and alleviating oxidative stress, this platform reshapes the neural microenvironment to relieve core ASD symptoms, pioneering a novel, non-invasive, and promising synthetic biology paradigm for autism care.

### SYPHU-China — LBP-Mototype: An Environment-Dependent Suicide Strategy for IBD Live Biotherapeutics
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6106) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/syphu-china)

Live biotherapeutics for inflammatory bowel disease (IBD) must balance therapeutic persistence with controllable clearance. LBP-Mototype proposes an environment-dependent suicide strategy in Escherichia coli Nissle 1917. Deleting acrB and pspA weakens bile efflux and membrane protection, while inflammation-responsive PspA complementation conditionally restores protection, leaving AcrB absent. Environmental bile stress supplies the damaging agent, avoiding an introduced toxin and aiming to shorten the genetic killing chain. Hydrogen peroxide and tetrathionate are alternative candidate inputs. A reset–targeting–acclimation–clearance cycle allows protective capacity to diverge before stronger stress selects between populations. Protein carry-over could buffer signal fluctuations, while par⁻-associated inheritance variation could produce graded survival and adjust the duration and size of the producing population. Signal reduction offers a candidate manual exit. A separate constitutive module encodes Elafin for local therapeutic delivery. The framework tests whether one conditional-protection pathway can coordinate these functions, linking signal history, viable-cell behaviour, recovery and active extracellular Elafin.

### SZA-Union — Unlocking the Code of Dental Caries Prevention: An Enzymatic Strategy Targeting Oral Biofilm
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6438) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/sza-union)

This project develops a multi-enzyme system targeting the exopolysaccharide matrix of dental plaque biofilms. By recombinantly expressing α-1,3-glucanase and α-1,6-glucanase in E. coli, the team aims to degrade the structural glucans of Streptococcus mutans biofilms. In vitro biofilm models are treated with enzyme cocktails and evaluated via HPLC, TLC, SEM, and staining assays. The system is designed for integration into mouthwashes or degradable oral films. Future directions include enzyme engineering, multi-enzyme synergy optimization, and smart delivery systems. This work offers a non-antibiotic, biofilm-targeting strategy for preventing dental caries.

### Thessaly — ATHENA: Microbiome engineering as a therapeutic approach against rUTIs
*GRC · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6097) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/thessaly)

Urinary tract infections affect over 400 million people worldwide each year, mostly women. Uropathogenic E. coli (UPEC) causes recurrence by invading urothelial cells and forming protected intracellular reservoirs that antibiotics cannot reach,  while antibiotic overuse has driven widespread multidrug resistance. We propose ATHENA, a microbiome engineering approach that converts UPEC into a non-pathogenic bacteria rather than simply killing it. Our system uses a genetically enhanced probiotic strain of E. coli (ABU 83,972), engineered with a functional fimbriae adhesion system to establish within the bladder. This probiotic carries a synthetic multiplexing plasmid that transfers into UPEC via conjugation, where an invasion-activated effector platform disarms the pathogen's virulence mechanisms from within, restoring, rather than disrupting, the bladder's microbiome. To make this kind of engineering accessible beyond UTIs, we also built NOCTUA, a user-friendly software suite making protein and microbiome engineering accessible.

### TU-Eindhoven — RESTART; Receptors Engineered for a SynNotch Therapy Against Rejection in Transplantation
*NLD · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6444) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/tu-eindhoven)

We have developed the basis for a cell therapy designed to induce local immune tolerance and protect transplanted organs from rejection. The proposed approach involves engineering a patient’s own T cells with a cascade of two synthetic Notch (synNotch) receptors. The first receptor recognizes donor-associated HLA-A2 on the graft and induces expression of a second receptor. The second receptor recognizes VCAM-1, a marker associated with inflammation. Recognition of both signals triggers production of interleukin-10 (IL-10), an anti-inflammatory cytokine that can suppress immune responses and promote tolerance.



By combining recognition of a donor-associated antigen with detection of inflammation, the system is designed to restrict IL-10 production to sites where both signals are present. This conditional response could help protect the graft while preserving immune function elsewhere in the body, providing a foundation for a more targeted approach to controlling transplant rejection with reduced reliance on continuous systemic immunosuppression.

### U Copenhagen — Lutealine: Hormone-responsive dosing for women with ADHD
*DNK · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6486) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/u-copenhagen)

Our project addresses the treatment of women with ADHD. ADHD is caused, among other factors, by dysregulated dopamine signalling in the brain. Sex hormones such as estrogen and progesterone both modulate this signalling: estrogen generally enhances dopaminergic transmission, while progesterone counteracts it. Consequently, fluctuating hormone levels across women’s hormonal cycles lead to varying degrees of symptoms. Current treatment, which uses a fixed dose of ADHD medication (e.g., methylphenidate or lisdexamfetamine), does not account for these fluctuating symptoms. The only option currently available is for the patient to adjust her own dose according to cycle phase - an approach that places the burden on the patient and requires accurate cycle tracking.

We are therefore developing a system that adapts dosing to the hormonal cycle, ensuring that women receive sufficient medication on the days they need it to manage symptoms, while avoiding an unnecessarily high dose.

### UAlberta — gleyeco: A glycan-targeting ocular drug delivery platform
*CAN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6254) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/ualberta)

Despite their critical role in regulating cellular interactions and disease pathology, glycans remain a largely untapped therapeutic target. Current treatments for inflammatory eye diseases are limited by toxic side effects and poor ocular retention, with nearly an entire dose washed away in the blink of an eye. Enter gleyeco, a glycan-targeted drug delivery platform that harnesses engineered lectins to selectively anchor therapeutics to inflamed ocular tissues. Candidate lectins were optimized through in silico mutagenesis and binding simulations to improve glycan recognition while minimizing off-target interactions. Our constructs were then validated experimentally for binding performance and functional activity. To enable sustained, localized treatment, therapeutics are incorporated into a hydrogel-based contact lens system. By enhancing drug retention at the ocular surface, gleyeco aims to improve inflammation control, reduce dosing frequency, and minimize systemic effects. This modular approach extends beyond ocular inflammation, opening the door to glycan-targeted therapies tailored to diverse disease contexts.

### UC Davis — INDIGO: INdole Delivery In Gut Oncology
*USA · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6502) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/uc-davis)

Colorectal cancer (CRC) is one of the most prevalent, severe cancers worldwide. The CRC tumor microenvironment is influenced by bacteria within the gut microbiome. We aim to create a site-localized live biotherapeutic product (LBP) to provide patients with a quality-of-life preserving treatment option prior to more aggressive interventions such as chemotherapies, radiation-therapies, and surgery. The proposed therapeutic mechanism acts in three stages: site localization, tumor-suppressive metabolite release, and containment through a genetic kill-switch to prevent environmental release. 

Site localization is mediated by oligopeptides that bind to the Thomsen–Friedenreich antigen (TF) antigen, also known as Gal-GalNAc, a disaccharide overexpressed on the surface of CRC cells. The metabolite released is indole-3-lactic acid (ILA), which has been proven to minimize CRC cell proliferation. Finally, a kill switch mediated by an RNA thermometer prevents the engineered bacteria from surviving outside physiological conditions of the body. Our LBP serves as a foundation for future development.

### UCAS-China — PARCEL: Pumilio-Assisted RNA Carrier via ELP Transition for Asthma Treatment
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6157) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/ucas-china)

The prevalence of asthma is increasing worldwide. Caused by genetic and environmental factors, asthma can severely impair respiratory function and patients' quality of life, yet existing treatments have limitations. We developed PARCEL: Pumilio-Assisted RNA Carrier via ELP Transition for Asthma Treatment, utilizing phase separation of elastin-like polypeptide (ELP) to build protein-mRNA supermolecules for inhaled asthma medication. Integrated ELP-Pum2-EEP13 fusion proteins assemble into micellar nanoparticles, with therapeutic mRNAs captured inside by the Pumilio 2 (Pum2) domain. Via Sortase A-induced antibody conjugation, the nanoparticles can specifically target lung epithelial cells and disassemble after internalization. Triggered by endosomal escape peptide 13 (EEP13), RNAs are released to be translated into asthma-related proteins. Our design establishes a novel RNA delivery platform, throwing new light on safe and specific therapies for asthma and beyond.

### UCS-B — Living Factory: Engineering L-Asparaginase for Improved Microbial Production of Anti-Leukemia Drugs
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6527) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/ucs-b)

Acute lymphoblastic leukemia is a common malignancy among children and adolescents. L-asparaginase is a key therapeutic enzyme that depletes circulating asparagine and suppresses leukemia-cell growth; however, current preparations may face limitations in production efficiency, stability, and adverse immune responses. This project develops an engineered microbial “living factory” for improved L-asparaginase production. We use type II L-asparaginase from Bacillus subtilis as the target enzyme and apply a region-specific rational mutagenesis strategy. Guided by structural modeling, sequence conservation analysis, and active-pocket prediction, nonconserved residues near the substrate-entry region, tetramer interface, and exposed flexible regions are selected for mutation while preserving catalytic residues. Candidate variants are expressed in E. coli BL21(DE3) and evaluated through protein purification, activity assays, half-life measurements, and kinetic analysis. Signal-peptide optimization is further explored to improve expression. This work aims to establish a more efficient and stable enzyme-production platform for future biomanufacturing applications.

### UI-ITB-Indonesia — BELL10: Exosome Encapsulated IL-10 for Blood-Brain Barrier Inflammation Therapy
*IDN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6237) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/ui-itb-indonesia)

Neuroinflammation is increasingly linked to neurological and mental-health disorders, yet anti-inflammatory proteins such as interleukin-10 (IL-10) cannot readily cross the blood-brain barrier (BBB). In Indonesia, where suicide is stigmatized and often untreated, this gap motivated BELL10. We engineered HEK293 cells with a plasmid combining IL-10, CD63, and a TAT-derived peptide, plus an S/MAR region for episomal plasmid retention, to produce IL-10 and explore its delivery via extracellular vesicles. IL-10, secreted by HEK293 cells, reached 126.70 ± 8.73 pg/mL by day eight. Semi-quantitative measurement of exosomes from TEM suggests size of 59nm. The conditioned medium reduced the inflammatory marker TNF-α in inflamed immune cells, though prolonged treatment caused cell death. A pharmacokinetic-pharmacodynamic model of intranasal IL-10 predicted increased claudin-5, with brain entry dominated by direct nose-brain routes. BELL10 is not a proven therapy but an exploratory step toward engineered systems for neuroinflammation, highlighting challenges before biomedical application.

### ULP-CA — SulFlux: Continued Regulation of Bile Acid Sulfonation through the BtSULT Operon
*USA · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6518) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/ulp-ca)

Cholecystectomy, the surgical removal of the gallbladder, can be followed by post-cholecystectomy syndrome (PCS), involving chronic diarrhea and gastrointestinal discomfort. Altered secondary bile acid metabolism may contribute to these symptoms, and prolonged exposure to certain bile acids has been associated with colorectal cancer risk. Existing treatments primarily manage symptoms rather than directly modifying these metabolites.

ULP-CA is developing a probiotic-based therapeutic proof of concept designed to reduce harmful secondary bile acid activity in the gut. Our engineered enzyme, SulFlux, is derived from BtSULT, a bacterial sulfotransferase found in Bacteroides thetaiotaomicron. Computational modeling suggests SulFlux can accommodate lithocholic acid (LCA), a secondary bile acid poorly recognized by native BtSULT. In vitro, we transformed Escherichia coli with our construct, assessed protein production through BCA assays, and observed improved SDS-PAGE band formation. Continued experimentation aims to establish reliable SulFlux folding, catalytic activity, and LCA sulfation.

### UManitoba — H.A.L.T. - UTI
*CAN · overgrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6131) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/umanitoba)

Recurrent urinary tract infections affect millions of people with vaginas, and over a third of those treated experience reinfection within six months. Antibiotic use kills beneficial bacteria along with pathogens, disrupting the vaginal microbiome and driving antimicrobial resistance. Lactobacillus crispatus normally guards against uropathogens, so utilizing it to block pathogen colonization of the vagina may prevent infection before it reaches the urethra.



We demonstrated self-assembly and surface display using GFP, purified several target adhesins and their binders, and encapsulated L. crispatus in hydrogel for testing in simulated vaginal fluid.



By combining AI-guided protein design, engineered probiotics, and localized delivery, H.A.L.T. - UTI shifts recurrent UTI care from treatment toward prevention, offering a non-antibiotic approach that preserves the microbiome and could be adapted to other infections

### Worldshaper-SHMed — AdipoSense: A Dual-Metabolite-Sensing Engineered Probiotic Response System
*CHN · high-school · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6509) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/worldshaper-shmed)

AdipoSense explores how engineered probiotics can respond to gut metabolic signals to support obesity-related research. Using Escherichia coli Nissle 1917 as the chassis, we integrate succinate and butyrate sensing with a genetic logic circuit designed to activate downstream expression only when succinate is high and butyrate is low. This dual-input design links a defined metabolic signal combination to controlled biological output. The circuit incorporates the plant-derived At1g78690p gene to investigate N-acylphosphatidylethanolamine (NAPE)-related lipid biosynthesis, alongside GFP as a measurable reporter. Through iterative Design–Build–Test–Learn cycles, we characterized the sensing modules, demonstrated the intended logic response in vitro, and obtained preliminary evidence of output-module expression. Further work is needed to confirm lipid production and evaluate biological effects. AdipoSense provides a foundation for developing probiotics whose activity is guided by their metabolic environment.

### XJTLU — FOLLICoffee: ROS-Responsive Living Microneedles for Hair-Loss Intervention
*CHN · undergrad · accepted* · [team page](https://teams.igem.org/6218) · [wiki](https://2026.igem.wiki/xjtlu)

Excess reactive oxygen species (ROS) can damage hair follicle cells and contribute to hair loss, although physiological ROS levels support normal cellular signaling. FOLLICoffee proposes a living microneedle patch containing engineered E. coli and calcium alginate carriers encapsulating S-acetylglutathione (SAG)-loaded liposomes. Bacteria would sense hydrogen peroxide (H₂O₂) through OxyR–PkatG and display alginate lyase on their surface to open carriers and release the liposomes. The design aims to quantitatively link H₂O₂ exposure to release while limiting ROS removal at physiological levels. Local SAG delivery would support intracellular glutathione replenishment to reduce oxidative stress around hair follicles and help prevent hair loss as a potential complement to existing interventions.



Our current work includes characterization of H₂O₂-induced promoter response via reporter gene insertion, fusion-protein expression studies, preliminary alginate-cleavage assays, and system modeling. Stakeholder dialogue and public co-design shaped project decisions and helped make participation more accessible.
